## Appendix B. Frozen independent exact-readback source

The input required by this source is exactly Appendix A, saved as
frozen_exact_data.json. Its literal mathematical definitions and arithmetic
operations are included for independent review. The source was run once only.

```python
"""One frozen exact readback. No optimizer, author checker, or rank calculation."""
import json
import sys
import time
import traceback
from fractions import Fraction
from pathlib import Path

START = time.perf_counter()
ROOT = Path('/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/tangent_neighborhood_20261001')
RESULT = {'status': 'running', 'checks': [], 'basis_readbacks': []}


class Q:
    __slots__ = ('a', 'b')

    def __init__(self, a=0, b=0):
        self.a, self.b = Fraction(a), Fraction(b)

    @staticmethod
    def of(x):
        if isinstance(x, Q):
            return x
        if isinstance(x, list):
            return Q(*x)
        return Q(x)

    def __add__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        return Q(self.a + other.a, self.b + other.b)

    __radd__ = __add__

    def __neg__(self):
        return Q(-self.a, -self.b)

    def __sub__(self, other):
        return self + (-Q.of(other))

    def __rsub__(self, other):
        return Q.of(other) - self

    def __mul__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        return Q(self.a * other.a + 2 * self.b * other.b,
                 self.a * other.b + self.b * other.a)

    __rmul__ = __mul__

    def __truediv__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        den = other.a * other.a - 2 * other.b * other.b
        if den == 0:
            raise ZeroDivisionError('exact zero field divisor')
        return Q((self.a * other.a - 2 * self.b * other.b) / den,
                 (self.b * other.a - self.a * other.b) / den)

    def __pow__(self, exponent):
        result = Q(1)
        for _ in range(exponent):
            result = result * self
        return result

    def __bool__(self):
        return bool(self.a or self.b)

    def __eq__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        return self.a == other.a and self.b == other.b

    def encoded(self):
        return [str(self.a), str(self.b)]

    def sign(self):
        a, b = self.a, self.b
        if a == 0:
            return (b > 0) - (b < 0)
        if b == 0:
            return (a > 0) - (a < 0)
        if (a > 0) == (b > 0):
            return 1 if a > 0 else -1
        difference = a * a - 2 * b * b
        return ((a > 0) - (a < 0)) * ((difference > 0) - (difference < 0))


def encode(x):
    if isinstance(x, Q):
        return x.encoded()
    if isinstance(x, list):
        return [encode(v) for v in x]
    if isinstance(x, dict):
        return {k: encode(v) for k, v in x.items()}
    return x


def matrix(rows, cols):
    return [[Q() for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]


def multiply(A, B):
    C = matrix(len(A), len(B[0]))
    for i, row in enumerate(A):
        for k, value in enumerate(row):
            if value:
                for j, other in enumerate(B[k]):
                    if other:
                        C[i][j] = C[i][j] + value * other
    return C


def laplacian(X):
    A = [[-x for x in row] for row in X]
    for i in range(len(X)):
        A[i][i] = sum(X[i], Q())
    return A


def check(name, condition, detail=None):
    RESULT['checks'].append({'name': name, 'passed': bool(condition), 'detail': encode(detail)})
    if not condition:
        raise AssertionError(name + ': ' + json.dumps(encode(detail)))


def main():
    data = json.loads((ROOT / 'frozen_exact_data.json').read_text())
    # Literal definitions from the mathematical manuscript, not from its checker.
    q = Q(0, 1)
    ell = 1 + q
    h = [q, Q(2), q, 1 + ell, q, Q(2), q]
    capacity = ell ** 3
    table = {
        (0, 1): Q(2), (1, 0): 2 * ell, (1, 3): 2 * ell,
        (2, 1): 2 * ell * q, (2, 3): 2 * ell ** 2 + 2 * ell * q,
        (3, 1): 2 * ell, (3, 2): 2 * ell ** 3, (3, 7): 2 * ell ** 2,
        (4, 3): 2 * ell * q, (4, 5): 2 * ell ** 2, (4, 7): 2 * ell * q,
        (5, 3): 4 * ell, (5, 4): 2 * ell ** 3, (5, 7): 2 * ell ** 3 + 4 * ell,
        (6, 3): 2 * ell * q, (6, 5): 2 * ell ** 3 * q,
        (6, 7): 2 * ell ** 4 + 2 * ell ** 3 * q + 2 * ell * q,
        (7, 3): 2 * ell, (7, 5): 2 * ell ** 3, (7, 6): 2 * ell ** 5,
    }
    pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(8) for j in range(8) if i != j]
    zeros = [ij for ij in pairs if ij not in table]
    base = matrix(8, 8)
    for (i, j), value in table.items():
        base[i][j] = value
    L0 = laplacian(base)
    T0 = [[Q(1) if i == j else (h[j] if j < i else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
    s = sum(h, Q())
    omega = [-2 * x for x in h] + [2 * s]
    base_flow = [sum((L0[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - omega[j] for j in range(8)]
    LT = multiply(L0, T0)
    D0 = [x * (2 - x) for x in h] + [-s * (s + 2)]
    M0 = [[(LT[i][j] + LT[j][i]) / 2 + (D0[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
    check('seven steps and exact base capacity', capacity == 1 + s, h)
    check('all twenty base entries positive', len(table) == 20 and all(v.sign() == 1 for v in table.values()))
    check('base original flow', all(not x for x in base_flow), base_flow)
    check('base full remainder is zero', all(not x for row in M0 for x in row), M0)

    equalities = [Q.of(v) for v in data['equality_prices']]
    check('sixteen equality prices', len(equalities) == 16)
    a, b = equalities[:8], equalities[8:]
    svec_prices = [Q.of(v) for v in data['PSD_svec']]
    check('twenty-eight svec prices', len(svec_prices) == 28)
    Z = matrix(7, 7)
    index = 0
    for j in range(7):
        for i in range(j + 1):
            value = svec_prices[index] if i == j else svec_prices[index] / q
            Z[i][j] = Z[j][i] = value
            index += 1
    stored_Z = [[Q.of(v) for v in row] for row in data['PSD_matrix']]
    check('sqrt2 column-major svec decodes the supplied matrix', Z == stored_Z, Z)
    sigma = {(i, j): Q.of(v) for i, j, v in data['all36_sign_prices']}
    check('all thirty-six original zero-pair prices, no duplicates', len(data['all36_sign_prices']) == 36 and set(sigma) == set(zeros))
    signs = [{'pair': list(ij), 'price': sigma[ij], 'sign': sigma[ij].sign()} for ij in zeros]
    check('all sign prices strictly positive in the real embedding', all(sigma[ij].sign() == 1 for ij in zeros), signs)
    box_prices = [Q.of(v) for v in data['all14_box_prices']]
    check('all fourteen box prices exactly zero', len(box_prices) == 14 and all(not v for v in box_prices), box_prices)

    lower = [[Q.of(v) for v in row] for row in data['LDL']['L']]
    pivots = [Q.of(v) for v in data['LDL']['pivots']]
    check('LDL dimensions', len(lower) == 7 and all(len(row) == 7 for row in lower) and len(pivots) == 7)
    check('LDL unit lower triangular', all(lower[i][i] == 1 for i in range(7)) and all(not lower[i][j] for i in range(7) for j in range(i + 1, 7)))
    check('all seven LDL pivots strictly positive', all(d.sign() == 1 for d in pivots), [{'value': d, 'sign': d.sign()} for d in pivots])
    ldl_residual = [[sum((lower[i][k] * pivots[k] * lower[j][k] for k in range(7)), Q()) - Z[i][j] for j in range(7)] for i in range(7)]
    check('every supplied LDL product entry equals Z', all(not v for row in ldl_residual for v in row), ldl_residual)

    # Independent V Z V^T, where V has columns e_i-e_7.
    H = matrix(8, 8)
    for i in range(7):
        for j in range(7):
            H[i][j] = Z[i][j]
        H[i][7] = -sum(Z[i], Q())
        H[7][i] = H[i][7]
    H[7][7] = sum((sum(row, Q()) for row in Z), Q())
    t0e = [sum(row, Q()) for row in T0]
    labels = [('p', i, -1) for i in range(7)] + [('Y', i, j) for i, j in pairs]
    residuals = []
    for kind, row, col in labels:
        p = [Q() for _ in range(7)]
        Y = matrix(8, 8)
        if kind == 'p':
            p[row] = Q(1)
        else:
            Y[row][col] = Q(1)
        S = sum(p, Q())
        LY = laplacian(Y)
        Tp = [[p[j] if j < i else Q() for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
        term1, term2 = multiply(L0, Tp), multiply(LY, T0)
        D1 = [2 * (1 - h[i]) * p[i] for i in range(7)] + [-2 * capacity * S]
        W = [[(term1[i][j] + term2[i][j] + term1[j][i] + term2[j][i]) / 2 + (D1[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
        op = [-2 * x for x in p] + [2 * S]
        f = [sum((LY[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - op[j] for j in range(8)]
        n = [sum(W[i], Q()) for i in range(8)]
        B = [[W[i][j] - W[i][7] - W[7][j] + W[7][7] for j in range(7)] for i in range(7)]
        trace = sum((Z[i][j] * B[i][j] for i in range(7) for j in range(7)), Q())
        original_trace = sum((H[i][j] * W[i][j] for i in range(8) for j in range(8)), Q())
        svec_B = [B[i][j] if i == j else q * B[i][j] for j in range(7) for i in range(j + 1)]
        vector_trace = sum((x * y for x, y in zip(svec_prices, svec_B)), Q())
        price_sign_term = sigma.get((row, col), Q()) if kind == 'Y' else Q()
        value = trace + price_sign_term - sum((a[i] * f[i] + b[i] * n[i] for i in range(8)), Q())
        residual = value + S
        label = kind + str(row) + (',' + str(col) if kind == 'Y' else '')
        common_null_residual = sum(n, Q()) - sum((f[i] * t0e[i] for i in range(8)), Q())
        RESULT['basis_readbacks'].append(encode({'variable': label, 'S': S, 'flow': f, 'W': W, 'We': n, 'B': B, 'PSD_price': trace, 'sign_price': price_sign_term, 'stationarity_residual': residual, 'congruence_price_residual': original_trace - trace, 'svec_price_residual': vector_trace - trace, 'common_null_scalar_residual': common_null_residual}))
        check('original-variable coefficient ' + label, not residual and original_trace == trace and vector_trace == trace and not common_null_residual, residual)
        residuals.append(residual)
    RESULT['independently_rebuilt_model'] = encode({'hbar': h, 'capacity': capacity, 'Lambdabar': base, 'T0': T0, 'original_ordered_pairs': [list(ij) for ij in pairs], 'zero_pairs': [list(ij) for ij in zeros], 'Z': Z, 'H': H})
    RESULT['all_63_stationarity_residuals'] = encode(residuals)
    RESULT['stored_passed_flags_used'] = False
    RESULT['stored_stationarity_residuals_used'] = False
    RESULT['author_checker_imported'] = False
    RESULT['status'] = 'passed_exact_readback_not_formal_verification'


try:
    main()
except BaseException as error:
    RESULT['status'] = 'failed_no_retry'
    RESULT['exception'] = repr(error)
    RESULT['traceback'] = traceback.format_exc()
    traceback.print_exc()
finally:
    RESULT['internal_seconds_before_result_write'] = time.perf_counter() - START
    (ROOT / 'exact_readback_result.json').write_text(json.dumps(encode(RESULT), indent=2) + '\n')
    print(json.dumps({'status': RESULT['status'], 'checks': len(RESULT['checks']), 'basis_readbacks': len(RESULT['basis_readbacks']), 'internal_seconds_through_result_write': time.perf_counter() - START}))
sys.exit(0 if RESULT['status'] == 'passed_exact_readback_not_formal_verification' else 1)
```

## intuition
A strictly priced original-coordinate dual forces every zero-capacity tangent into an equality kernel. Two complete signed child calculations eliminate that kernel. Uniform boundedness of all feasible coefficient arrays, base uniqueness and the exact quadratic remainder then convert strict separation on the joint unit cone into a neighborhood quantified only by the physical word.

## DOC-001546 — handoff_20261010/materials/facts/b98bb807d514e80d.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010\materials\facts\b98bb807d514e80d.md

原始字节数：6230；原始 SHA-256：7241612510a7cccb3833e55d2749a241f353d4f7dc7f9a51c65f750b22ff9d25

证据标签：historical_fact_file_check_original_scope。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001546 -->
---
fact_id: b98bb807d514e80d
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max3
predecessors: [0be8b54e0a33381c]
glossary_introduces:
  C_n: The primitive mass S_circle(n)+1.
  K_n: The explicit maximum of the global Huber bound and all displayed single-cut terms.
  Omega: Symmetric primitive mass join Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2.
  P: Abbreviation P_j inside the single-cut construction.
  P_j: Sum of steps preceding the selected index j.
  T: Abbreviation T_j inside the single-cut construction.
  T_j: Sum of steps following the selected index j.
  a: Left Huber slope magnitude c(b-1)/(2T+1) in the single-cut construction.
  b: The selected step h_j>1 in the single-cut construction.
  c: Right Huber slope 1/(P+1) in the single-cut construction.
  ell^(n): Length-n schedule with t-th entry t+1 for 0<=t<n.
  p: The exponent log_2 3, used only in the crude exact-recursion estimate.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Exact single-cut Huber lower bounds are asymptotically insufficient for the Zhang–Jiang profile.

For any integer n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, all x_0!=x_star, and GD x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and f_star=f(x_star). Put s(h)=sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t. For 0<=j<n let P_j=sum_{t<j}h_t and T_j=sum_{t>j}h_t, and let (u)_+=max(u,0). Define
K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)), max_{0<=j<n} (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},
where the inner maximum is omitted at n=0.
Then W_n(h)>=K_n(h) for every such schedule. Every nonzero term indexed by j is attained as the normalized error of an explicit one-dimensional convex L-smooth instance with a minimizer.

For x,y>=0 define
phi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2,
psi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4.
Define S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,
S_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],
S_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].
Put B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and p=log_2 3.
For all n>=0, S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^p and B_n<=1+4n^p.
For every integer n>=2, the schedule ell^(n)_t=t+1, 0<=t<n, satisfies
K_n(ell^(n))<=16/n^2 and
K_n(ell^(n))/U_n^ZJ<=16(1+4n^p)/n^2 -> 0 as n->infinity.
Consequently this particular maximum of the global Huber witness and all these single-cut witnesses cannot itself yield a uniform asymptotic lower bound (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ. This is only a limitation of K_n; it makes no small-error assertion about W_n(ell^(n)) and does not resolve the unrestricted minimax target.

## proof
## Legal single-cut witness

Scaling permits L=1. Fix j with b=h_j>1; put P=P_j,T=T_j,
c=1/(P+1), a=c(b-1)/(2T+1). Define f(0)=0 and
f'(x)=max(-a,min(c,x)). This derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and
1-Lipschitz. Hence f is differentiable, convex, 1-smooth, and minimized at0.
Start at x_0=1. Up to the checkpoint all gradients equal c: nonnegative steps
give x_i=1-c sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-cP=c for i<=j. Thus x_j=c, and the checkpoint
sends x_{j+1}=c(1-b)=-a(2T+1).

On the suffix all gradients equal -a. Every suffix partial sum is at most T,
so each iterate is at most -a(2T+1)+aT=-a(T+1)<=-a. Therefore
x_n=-a(T+1), f(x_n)=a^2(T+1/2), and

\[
2f(x_n)=a^2(2T+1)=\frac{(b-1)^2}{(P+1)^2(2T+1)}.
\]

The initial squared distance is1. Multiplying f by L proves the same normalized
statement for every L>0. Terms with h_j<=1 are zero and require no construction.
The global Huber term is proved in predecessor fact 0be8b54e0a33381c, proof
part 1, including zero and empty schedules. Taking a finite maximum preserves
the lower bound, because the worst-case function may depend on h and j.

## A crude exact-recursion bound sufficient for the separation

Set p=log_2 3, C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.
The primitive recurrence becomes

\[
C_n=\max_{0\leq k<n}\Omega(C_k,C_{n-k-1}),\qquad
\Omega(a,b)=\frac{a+b+\sqrt{(a+b)^2+4ab}}2.
\]

The function Omega is increasing in both nonnegative arguments. When a>=b>=0,
the inequality sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab)<=a+3b follows by squaring, with the right
square exceeding the left by8b^2. Thus Omega(a,b)<=a+2b.
For real u>=v>0, put r=u/v>=1. The derivative of
(r+1)^p-r^p is p[(r+1)^(p-1)-r^(p-1)]>0, so
(r+1)^p-r^p>=2^p-1=2. Consequently
Omega(u^p,v^p)<=u^p+2v^p<=(u+v)^p.
Symmetry handles v>=u. Since C_0=1, induction on n using
u=k+1, v=n-k proves C_n<=(n+1)^p.

The dominant mass recurrence, obtained directly from psi and also displayed
in predecessor 0be8b54e0a33381c, is

\[
B_n=\max_{0\leq k<n}G(C_k,B_{n-k-1}),\qquad
G(a,d)=2a+\frac d2+\frac12\sqrt{d^2+8ad}.
\]

Since sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a for nonnegative a,d, G(a,d)<=d+4a.
Starting with B_0=1, induction and the primitive bound give, for m=n-k-1,

\[
G(C_k,B_m)\leq1+4m^p+4(k+1)^p\leq1+4n^p.
\]

The last inequality uses u^p+v^p<=(u+v)^p for p>=1 and nonnegative u,v;
this follows, when v>0, by the increasing function (r+1)^p-r^p being at
least its value1 at r=0, and is equality when v=0. Hence B_n<=1+4n^p.

## Linear schedule defeats the single-cut functional

For h_t=t+1, the total sum is n(n+1)/2, so the global Huber term is at most
1/n^2. At checkpoint j, P_j=j(j+1)/2 and (h_j-1)_+=j.
When j>=n/2,

\[
\frac{j^2}{(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)}
\leq\frac{4}{j^2}\leq\frac{16}{n^2}.
\]

When j<n/2, the integers satisfy j+1<=(n+1)/2 and j+2<=(n+3)/2. Thus

\[
1+2T_j=1+n(n+1)-(j+1)(j+2)\geq(3n^2+1)/4\geq n^2/2.
\]

Also 1+P_j>=j, so this cut term is at most2/n^2. This includes j=0,
where the numerator is zero. Combining the cases gives K_n<=16/n^2.
Because U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, the ratio is at most16(1+4n^p)/n^2 and tends to0.
All estimates are analytic and valid for every integer n>=2.

The schedules ell^(n) are only counterexamples to the strength of K_n. No
small upper bound on their actual W_n is asserted; they may be very unstable.

## intuition
The selected cut gives an exact legal asymmetric-Huber witness with no parameter losses. Nevertheless even maximizing all such witnesses misses the benchmark by a vanishing relative factor on explicit linear schedules; richer cross-cut geometry is necessary for this approach.

---
fact_id: c3bfc54321ddec65
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max2
predecessors: [1252b8823bb5a290, b98bb807d514e80d, dfde2fa326c908bf]
glossary_introduces:
  A_mc: Frozen first physical bridge excess divided by M, A>=4B+4.
  B_mc: Frozen second physical bridge excess divided by M, B>=2.
  C_mc: Frozen returning bridge excess divided by M, equal2B+1.
  D_3: Squared norm of E_3, equal2A_mc*((B_mc+a_mc)^2-(B_mc+a_mc)+B_mc/2).
  D_R: Squared norm of E_R, equal2A_mc*Dhat_R.
  D_mc: The nondegenerate tetrahedron conv{0,p_mc,q_mc,r_mc}; local alias D.
  Dhat_R: Return-terminal denominator factor 1-d_R+(B_mc/2)*d_R^2.
  E_3: Difference t_Q-z_mc=M_mc*(B_mc+a_mc)*q_mc+M_mc*r_mc; local alias E in the three-stage terminal calculation.
  E_P: Difference t_P-w_mc used to compare the initial anchor with the return active anchor.
  E_R: Difference t_R-w_mc=M_mc*q_mc+M_mc*d_R*r_mc.
  F_col: Any legal global convex1-smooth completion in R3 of the entire specified collapsed prefix with the retained zero optimum.
  F_col_old: Static old collapsed objective max_(g in conv{0,p_mc,q_col})(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).
  F_old: Static old objective max_(g in D_mc)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), whose gradient is projection onto D_mc.
  F_return: Convex conjugate of H_return, the fixed completed four-stage returning static objective.
  F_three: Convex conjugate of H_three, the fixed completed three-stage static objective.
  H_jet: Conjugate lower quadratic ||v||^2/2+<u,v>-delta of a prefix jet x=u+g, f=delta+||g||^2/2.
  H_return: Explicit eight-anchor full-prefix completion retaining the first r query and returned q queries.
  H_three: Explicit six-anchor 1-strongly convex maximum-quadratic full-prefix completion.
  J_mc: Support function max(0,<p_mc,u>,<q_mc,u>,<r_mc,u>); local alias J.
  Kc: Fixed constant cosh(2) in the universal-lens estimate.
  L_down: Three consecutive copies of Chi_N, each in decreasing step order.
  L_i: Finite nonnegative ordinary list of mass M_mc-1, with its actual entries and physical length retained.
  L_lens_n: The EXACT universal-lens channel sup_(zeta in Lens_n)Gap_h(zeta), with the prescribed transcript and optimum0.
  L_stage: Three-stage terminal coefficient B_mc+a_mc; written local L in that calculation, distinct from global smoothness.
  L_up: Three consecutive copies of Chi_N, each in increasing step order.
  M_2: One plus the actual combined mass after the first bridge and before the final bridge in the specifically defined collapsed policy.
  M_col_return: Collapsed mass M_2=(3B_mc+4)*M_mc for h_return.
  M_col_three: Collapsed mass M_2=(B_mc+2)*M_mc for h_three.
  M_mc: One plus the common ordinary-list mass, M>=2.
  P_D_mc: Euclidean projection onto D_mc; P_D denotes the same projection when D=D_mc.
  P_N: Polynomial of one Chi_N copy, equal its product of step factors and the accepted odd-Chebyshev formula.
  Q_n: Entire normalized convex-quadratic channel max_(t in[0,1])t*product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t)^2.
  Q_xy: Signed ordered interpolation residual between two actual samples at positions x and y, as defined in fact1252.
  Qnorm_mc: Squared norm of q_mc, equal2A/M^2.
  R_n: Original worst-case objective normalization W_n/2; distinct from local squared slope norm R=Rnorm_mc.
  Rnorm_mc: Squared norm of r_mc, equalB*Qnorm_mc/2.
  T_Cheb: Chebyshev normalization (2N+1)^2=2M_mc-1 on the specified growing families.
  U_col: The corresponding comparison upper U_col_three or U_col_return for the indicated word.
  U_col_return: Positive upper comparison 2A_mc*epsilon^2/(3B_mc+4)^2 on the return parameter domain.
  U_col_three: Positive or zero upper comparison 2A_mc*epsilon^2/(B_mc+2)^2.
  V_col: Actual normalized terminal payoff of a collapsed completion using its true closest initial radius1.
  V_return: Actual normalized terminal value2F_return(x_n) with true initial radius1.
  V_three: Actual normalized terminal value2F_three(x_n) with true initial radius1.
  X_D: Third postbridge position z_mc-C_mc*M_mc*r_mc.
  X_R: Second postbridge position y_mc-B_mc*M_mc*q_mc.
  X_mc: First postbridge position -A_mc*e_1; local alias X.
  a_mc: Scalar1-1/M.
  b_3: Three-stage terminal anchor excess A_mc*a_mc*(epsilon-2/M_mc).
  b_R: Return-terminal anchor excess 2A_mc*((B_mc-1/(2M_mc))*epsilon-B_mc*a_mc/(2M_mc)).
  d_N: Integer floor(log_2 N) used in the exact balanced-split lower benchmark bound.
  d_R: Return-terminal coefficient C_mc+a_mc; local alias d.
  delta_D: Support offset (M_mc-1)*Qnorm_mc at t_D.
  delta_P: Support offset a_mc/M_mc at t_P.
  delta_Q: Support offset (M_mc-1)*Qnorm_mc at t_Q.
  delta_R: Support offset (M_mc-1)*Rnorm_mc at t_R.
  e_1: First standard coordinate vector in the fixed witness space R3.
  e_2: Second standard coordinate vector in the fixed witness space R3.
  e_3: Third standard coordinate vector in the fixed witness space R3.
  h_return: Actual word (L_0,A_mc*M_mc+1,L_1,B_mc*M_mc+1,L_2,C_mc*M_mc+1,L_3,1+M_mc*epsilon).
  h_three: Actual word (L_0,A_mc*M_mc+1,L_1,B_mc*M_mc+1,L_2,1+M_mc*epsilon).
  n_return: Actual unpadded physical length of h_return; 12N+4 on the specified growing family.
  n_three: Actual unpadded physical length of h_three; 9N+3 on the specified growing family.
  p_mc: First support slope e1/M in R3.
  q_col: Precisely specified collapsed second slope (-2e_1+sqrt(2A_mc-4)e_2)/M_2; local alias q_2.
  q_mc: Second support slope (-2,sqrt(2A-4),0)/M.
  r_mc: Third support slope -q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)||q||^2)e3.
  rho_col_return: Weak comparison factor3*(3B_mc+4)^2/4; return feasibility forces epsilon>0.
  rho_col_three: Weak comparison factor B_mc*(B_mc+2)^2*(1-1/(2B_mc^3))/2; strict ratio only for epsilon>0.
  rho_crude: Crude exact-recursion exponent log_2(3) from factb98, less than8/5.
  t_D: First returned-q-stage proximal anchor w_mc+(M_mc-1)q_mc.
  t_P: First p-stage proximal anchor a_mc*e_1.
  t_Q: First q-stage proximal anchor y_mc+(M_mc-1)q_mc.
  t_R: First r-stage proximal anchor z_mc+(M_mc-1)r_mc; retained in BOTH completions.
  theta_3: Nonnegative active-anchor coefficient (b_3)_+/D_3.
  theta_R: Return-terminal active-anchor coefficient b_R/D_R.
  v_0: Local displacement x_n-z_mc=-M_mc*epsilon*r_mc in the three-stage calculation, or x_n-w_mc=-M_mc*epsilon*q_mc in the return calculation.
  v_3: Actual three-stage terminal gradient -M_mc*epsilon*r_mc-theta_3*E_3.
  v_R: Actual return-terminal gradient -M_mc*epsilon*q_mc-theta_R*E_R.
  w_mc: Returned fourth-stage zero-height proximal endpoint z-(2B+1)Mr-Mq.
  y_2: Forced collapsed-prefix minimum -A_mc*e_1-M_2*q_col.
  y_mc: Second-stage zero-height proximal endpoint -Ae1-Mq.
  z_mc: Third-stage zero-height proximal endpoint y-BMq-Mr.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
# Exact six/eight-anchor multistage completions and two special word families

All norms and inner products are Euclidean. For an integer n>=0, a finite
nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and L>0, let W_n(h)
be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz
gradient, every minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using the actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2. The witnesses
below use smoothness1 and the true closest initial distance to the complete
minimum set. Multiplication of their objective by any L>0 gives the same
points with steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.

## Definitions of the two completed functions

Fix real parameters M=M_mc>=2, B=B_mc>=2, A=A_mc>=4B+4, and
epsilon in [0,1]. These are local construction parameters, distinct from
the benchmark sequences C_n and B_n below. Put a=a_mc=1-1/M,
C=C_mc=2B+1, and let e_1,e_2,e_3 be the standard basis of R^3. Define

    nu=sqrt(2A-4),
    p=p_mc=e_1/M,
    q=q_mc=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M,
    Q=Qnorm_mc=||q||^2=2A/M^2,
    r=r_mc=-q/2+sqrt((B/2-1/4)Q)e_3,
    R=Rnorm_mc=||r||^2=BQ/2,       <q,r>=-Q/2,
    D_mc=conv{0,p,q,r},
    F_old(x)=max_(g in D_mc){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},
    y=y_mc=-Ae_1-Mq,
    z=z_mc=y-BMq-Mr,
    w=w_mc=z-CMr-Mq,
    t_P=a e_1,              delta_P=a/M,
    t_Q=y+(M-1)q,           delta_Q=(M-1)Q,
    t_R=z+(M-1)r,           delta_R=(M-1)R,
    t_D=w+(M-1)q,           delta_D=(M-1)Q.

Here conv denotes convex hull. For each ordinary list L_i, choose any
finite nonnegative list of total mass M-1 and retain every entry, including
zeros. With concatenation in the displayed physical order, define

    h_three=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,1+M epsilon),
    h_return=(L_0,AM+1,L_1,BM+1,L_2,CM+1,L_3,1+M epsilon).

Their physical lengths are sum_(i=0)^2 length(L_i)+3 and
sum_(i=0)^3 length(L_i)+4. Assertions about h_return additionally assume
M epsilon>=2B^2. Define, for v,x in R^3,

    H_three(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,<t_P,v>-delta_P,
               <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-delta_Q,<z,v>,<t_R,v>-delta_R},
    H_return(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,<t_P,v>-delta_P,
               <y,v>,<t_Q,v>-delta_Q,<z,v>,<t_R,v>-delta_R,
               <w,v>,<t_D,v>-delta_D},
    F_three(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_three(v)},
    F_return(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H_return(v)}.

These are one fixed globally defined function per full physical run,
specified before that run. To define its complete old prefix, run F_old
from x_0=e_1 on the appropriate schedule and retain every triple
(x_i,grad F_old(x_i),F_old(x_i)) with 0<=i<n, together with the
zero optimum triple. The terminal index n is unqueried by the updates.
For any of the resulting actual functions, its signed ordered residual is
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 on
{star,0,...,n}, where f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i), and
x_star=g_star=f_star=0.

## The specifically defined collapsed comparison

For the SAME h_three or h_return, retain the first list L_0 and bridge
AM+1, and treat all subsequent entries before the last 1+M epsilon as
one ordinary list, retaining their physical indices. Its mass is M_2-1,
where

    M_2=M_col_three=(B+2)M,
    or M_2=M_col_return=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M.

Set q_col=(-2e_1+nu e_2)/M_2 and define the static old collapsed function

    F_col_old(x)=max_(g in conv{0,p,q_col}){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Run F_col_old from e_1 on that same full physical word and retain all
its old queried triples at indices 0<=i<n.
The collapsed completion class consists exactly of all everywhere defined
convex differentiable 1-smooth functions on R^3 interpolating its ENTIRE
old queried prefix, including values and gradients, and the optimum triple
(0,0,0). It is nonempty. For any such completion F_col, the same actual word
from e_1 has normalized true-distance payoff V_col(F_col). Define the positive
or zero upper comparison number

    U_col_three=2A epsilon^2/(B+2)^2,
    U_col_return=2A epsilon^2/(3B+4)^2,
    rho_col_three=[B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),
    rho_col_return=(3/4)(3B+4)^2.

## Exact named reference channels and original benchmark

For any h of length n, put s(h)=sum_(i<n)h_i, P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i,
T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, and (u)_+=max(u,0). The accepted single-cut channel is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)),
          max_(0<=j<n)(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},

with the inner maximum omitted at n=0. Define the entire convex-quadratic
channel by

    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1) t product_(i<n)(1-h_i t)^2.

This is the supremum of the normalized payoff over all finite-dimensional
convex quadratics with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, with arbitrary
initial points and chosen minimizers, in the original normalization.

For n>=1 and a complex variable zeta=alpha+i beta, put
Poly_0(zeta)=1, Poly_i(zeta)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t zeta),
xi_i=Poly_i(zeta), gamma_i=zeta Poly_i(zeta), and xi_star=gamma_star=0.
Identify the complex plane with the real plane using <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). On the index set
I_n={star,0,...,n}, the weight of the directed edge j->i is

    q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2.

Let ell_n be the maximum sum of these weights on directed simple paths
n->star. Define Gap_h(zeta)=-2ell_n for zeta in the exact universal lens

    Lens_n={alpha+i beta:
       alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))},
    L_lens_n(h)=sup_(zeta in Lens_n) Gap_h(zeta),
    V_lens(h)=max{L_lens_n(h),1/(1+2s(h))}.

The meaning and legality of this transcript are those of accepted fact_id
dfde2fa326c908bf; the reference is restricted to Lens_n. No upper
comparison with the full schedule-dependent complex legal region is made.

Use the original all-split sequences, exactly as in accepted fact_id
b98bb807d514e80d. The joins have real arguments x,y>=0, and the
recurrences apply for every integer n>=1:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,      lambda=1+sqrt(2),      Kc=cosh(2).

Every maximum here ranges over ALL integer splits, with C_n=S_circle(n)+1
and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 in that predecessor's notation.

For each integer N>=2^60, set

    M=2N(N+1)+1, A=M^(1/12), B=M^(1/120), epsilon=M^(-1/3),
    T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.

Let Chi_N be the list of entries
sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]), 1<=j<=N. Let L_up be three consecutive
increasing copies of Chi_N and L_down three consecutive decreasing copies.
Use L_0=L_up and L_i=L_down for every subsequent i. Thus the two base
word lengths are 9N+3 and 12N+4. For every integer n>=9*2^60+3 define
the padded three-stage word by N=floor((n-3)/9), followed by inserting
n-(9N+3) leading zero steps into that base word. For every integer
n>=12*2^60+4 define the padded return word by N=floor((n-4)/12) and
insert n-(12N+4) leading zero steps. These are the two SPECIAL families
in the conclusions below, with their respective N and parameters.

## Conclusions

(i) F_three and, on its additional parameter domain, F_return are finite
everywhere, convex, continuously differentiable, and have least global
gradient Lipschitz constant EXACTLY1. Each is the pointwise maximal legal
completion of its complete specified old prefix. The exact maximum of
all physical prefix conjugate quadratics is H_three or H_return, with
six or eight distinct affine anchors, respectively; the first r-query
anchor t_R is retained in BOTH laws. Every old prefix point, value and
gradient is preserved. The actual queried gradients are p,q,r for the
three-stage law and p,q,r,q for the return law, one constant gradient on
each ordinary list and its following bridge query. Every signed ordered
interpolation residual, including every remote, optimum and terminal pair,
in both orientations, is nonpositive for the resulting actual function.
The complete minimum sets and minimum values are

    argmin F_three=conv{0,y,z},   min F_three=0,
    argmin F_return=conv{0,y,z,w},   min F_return=0.

Origin is the unique closest minimizer to e_1, at distance1 in both cases.

(ii) For the three-stage law set

    L_stage=B+a, E_3=M(B+a)q+Mr,
    D_3=||E_3||^2=2A[(B+a)^2-(B+a)+B/2],
    b_3=Aa(epsilon-2/M), theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3.

The actual terminal point, gradient and normalized payoff are exactly

    x_n=z-M epsilon r,
    grad F_three(x_n)=-M epsilon r-theta_3 E_3,
    V_three=2F_three(x_n)
       =AB epsilon^2-[Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+]^2/D_3,
    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.

This includes epsilon=0, epsilon=2/M when in range, M=2, and epsilon=1.
For the return law set

    d_R=C+a, E_R=Mq+M d_R r,
    Dhat_R=1-d_R+(B/2)d_R^2, D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,
    b_R=2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)], theta_R=b_R/D_R.

The actual terminal quantities are exactly

    x_n=w-M epsilon q,
    grad F_return(x_n)=-M epsilon q-theta_R E_R,
    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,
    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2.

They include the feasible boundary M epsilon=2B^2. The denominator
bounds are 2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3; the lower-point identity is exactly
1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2=2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.
In particular W_n(h_three)>=V_three and W_n(h_return)>=V_return.

(iii) Every member of the narrowly defined collapsed completion class
has true closest initial radius1 and 0<=V_col(F_col)<=U_col for its word.
On the entire respective parameter domains the WEAK comparisons are

    V_three>=rho_col_three U_col_three>=rho_col_three V_col(F_col),
    V_return>=rho_col_return U_col_return>=rho_col_return V_col(F_col).

At epsilon=0 in the three-stage domain, V_three=U_col_three=V_col(F_col)=0:
the displayed weak comparison has equality and no payoff ratio is asserted.
When epsilon>0 the comparison upper U_col is positive and the ratios
V_three/U_col_three>=rho_col_three>1 and
V_return/U_col_return>=rho_col_return>1 are defined and give strict
domination of every such collapsed completion. Epsilon>0 is automatic
in the return parameter domain. Both factors diverge on their specified
growing families. The comparison covers this exact retained prefix and
second slope, with no claim about other partitions, slopes, or witnesses.

(iv) For each of the two base growing words and each of its padded words,
the following bounds hold with each law evaluated on its corresponding
word and ACTUAL physical horizon n; V denotes that law's attained payoff:

    V_three>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),
    V_return>=(3/2)M^(-7/12),
    V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12)  for either law,
    K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6),
    Q_n(h_three)<=64M^(-49/60),
    Q_n(h_return)<=1600M^(-4/5),
    L_lens_n(h_three)<=128Kc^18 M^(-49/60),
    L_lens_n(h_return)<=3200Kc^24 M^(-4/5).

Writing d_N=floor(log_2 N), the actual original benchmark obeys

    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N)>1.

Consequently each SPECIAL family supplies an attained static witness at
EVERY integer horizon above its stated threshold with V>U_n^ZJ, and
V/U_n^ZJ tends to infinity. Meanwhile

    max{K_n(h),Q_n(h),L_lens_n(h)}=o(U_n^ZJ)

along each family, also with V_lens in place of L_lens_n. These results
neither upper-bound unrestricted W_n on these words nor quantify over
arbitrary words. They give no comparison with all nonlinear scalar
functions, all earlier adversaries, or the full complex legal region.
The original arbitrary-word asymptotic exact-profile theorem remains OPEN.

## proof
# Joint proof of the stated multistage completion theorem

All construction parameters, vectors, lists, functions, channels and benchmark
sequences have the definitions and ranges in the statement. For brevity,
local M,A,B,C,a,p,q,r,Q,R,y,z,w denote the corresponding construction
symbols with subscript mc. A proximal pair means x=u+g with g=P_D_mc(x),
where P_D_mc is Euclidean projection onto D_mc. Partial H denotes the
convex subdifferential of H. In the three-stage terminal calculation, local
L_stage=B+a (written L in the displayed algebra) is a stage coefficient,
not the objective's smoothness constant, which is1 throughout the proof.
The notation E in that calculation means E_3. In the return calculation,
d means d_R. The notation v_0 in each of these separate calculations is
the displacement from its indicated zero-height endpoint to x_n.

The only verified mathematical inputs are fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290
(maximal unqueried-terminal prefix interpolation and signed residual
necessity), fact_id b98bb807d514e80d (the exact all-split benchmark,
K_n, and B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)), and fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf
(the precise complex-polynomial transcript and universal lens, together
with the reciprocal-root Chebyshev identity and its ellipse estimate).
Their exact definitions are reproduced in the statement. In particular,
the benchmark here ranges over every split and the complex comparison is
only on Lens_n. No audit or unverified finding is a mathematical premise;
all new geometry, terminal active-set algebra and reference comparisons
are established jointly below.

## 1. Static old function and every reached query

Let D=conv{0,p,q,r} and

    F_old(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

The maximum is attained uniquely because D is compact and the objective
is strictly concave; the maximizer is P_D(x). The projection normal
condition says, for u=x-g,

    <u,b-g><=0 for every b in D.

It also says g maximizes the support J(u)=max(0,<p,u>,<q,u>,<r,u>).
Thus x=u+g, delta=J(u)=<g,u>, and
F_old(x)=delta+||g||^2/2. Conversely, any displayed pair with g in D
and this support condition is the UNIQUE projection pair. Tied support
planes do not make the envelope gradient ambiguous.

For any two old pairs, direct cancellation in the signed original
residual of fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_xy=F_old(x)-F_old(y)+<g_x,y-x>+||g_x-g_y||^2/2
        =<u_y,g_x-g_y><=0.

This works for every ordered pair, remote pairs, duplicate positions,
and the zero optimum. To check global regularity directly, the two
projection normal inequalities with each other's projected vectors give

    <x-x',P_D(x)-P_D(x')>>=||P_D(x)-P_D(x')||^2.

Cauchy--Schwarz makes the projection 1-Lipschitz. Evaluation of the
attained maximum at its two maximizing vectors bounds
F_old(x+d)-F_old(x)-<P_D(x),d> between0 and
<P_D(x+d)-P_D(x),d>, hence between0 and ||d||^2. Thus F_old is
differentiable with gradient P_D, which is 1-Lipschitz, and convex as
a supremum of affine functions. The slopes are independent: p has
nonzero first coordinate, q has nonzero second coordinate, and r has
nonzero third coordinate. Therefore D has interior, where P_D(x)=x
and F_old has Hessian I. Its least global smoothness is exactly one.

Because 0 is a vertex of D, F_old>=0 and its complete minimum set is

    {x:<p,x><=0, <q,x><=0, <r,x><=0}.

Indeed this condition makes every objective term nonpositive. If any
of the three inner products is positive, a sufficiently small positive
multiple of that vertex belongs to D and makes the objective positive.
The p inequality puts all minima in x_1<=0; origin is the unique
closest one to e_1 and the true distance is one.

The following table derives the actual static chronology. Its variable
t=1+s, where s is the ordinary mass already spent in the current list;
therefore 1<=t<=M. The right endpoint t=M is also the selected following
bridge query. Nonnegativity of every entry ensures every ordinary
partial mass lies in this interval.

| Stage | Proposed proximal point u | Current support height | Exhaustive other-plane comparisons |
|---|---|---|---|
| p | (1-t/M)e_1 | (1-t/M)/M | q height is minus twice this height; r height equals this height; zero is below it |
| q | X-tq, X=-Ae_1 | (M-t)Q | p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; r height=-(M-t)Q/2<=0; zero is below q |
| r | X_R-tr, X_R=y-BMq | (M-t)R | q height=-BMQ+tQ/2<0; p height=(2-A+2B-t/M)/M<0; zero is below r |
| returned q | X_D-tq, X_D=z-CMr | (M-t)Q | r height=-CMR+tQ/2<0; p height=(-A+2t/M)/M<0; zero is below q |

For the first row, the p/r tie holds at EVERY p-stage query. Since p
is an active support subgradient and x=u+p, projection uniqueness
proves the actual gradient is p. It is not necessary to break that tie.
All strict comparisons in the other rows follow from B>=2 and
A>=4B+4, including t=M. At zero height the zero plane may tie with
other planes, but the displayed projection remains unique.

The endpoints and bridges follow by literal GD subtraction:

    after L_0: x=p, u=0;
    p-(AM+1)p = X=-Ae_1;
    y=X-Mq=(2-A,-nu,0),    <q,y>=<r,y>=0;
    (y+q)-(BM+1)q=X_R=y-BMq;
    z=X_R-Mr=y-BMq-Mr;
    <r,z>=0, <q,z>=M(1/2-B)Q, z_1=1-A+2B;
    (z+r)-(CM+1)r=X_D=z-CMr;
    w=X_D-Mq=z-CMr-Mq;
    <q,w>=0, <r,w>=(MQ/2)(1-CB), w_1=2-A.

For h_three the last update is from z+r and reaches z-M epsilon r.
For h_return it is from w+q and reaches w-M epsilon q. Every bridge
is an actual physical query and update. Lists were not collapsed into
oracle calls. A zero entry repeats the same jet and leaves the endpoint
identities unchanged.

At the old three-law terminal z-M epsilon r the first coordinate is
1-A+2B-epsilon<0, the q support is
MQ[(1+epsilon)/2-B]<0, and the r support is -M epsilon R<=0.
At the old return-law terminal w-M epsilon q the first coordinate is
2-A+2epsilon<0, the q support is -M epsilon Q<0, and the r support
is (MQ/2)(1-CB+epsilon)<0. Thus BOTH old terminal values are zero.
Only the unqueried terminal completion creates the stated new payoff.

## 2. Complete conjugate-anchor lists and all prefix rows

For a prefix jet x=u+g, f=delta+||g||^2/2, its conjugate lower
quadratic from fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 simplifies, with no dropped constant, to

    H_jet(v)=||v||^2/2+<u,v>-delta.

Within one constant-gradient stage both u and delta are affine in
the ordinary partial mass. Thus each intervening affine term is a
convex combination of the first-query and endpoint affine terms;
it cannot enlarge their maximum. BOTH endpoint jets are physical
prefix queries. This proves exact equality of the maximum over every
physical prefix jet with the endpoint maximum, rather than replacing
the interpolation problem by an endpoint relaxation.

Here are all six/eight affine terms, including the original optimum:

| Anchor u | Offset delta | Laws retaining it |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0 | both; p-stage bridge and optimum give the same affine term |
| t_P=a e_1 | a/M | both; initial query |
| y | 0 | both; q-stage ending bridge query |
| t_Q=y+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | both; FIRST q query |
| z | 0 | both; r-stage ending bridge query |
| t_R=z+(M-1)r | (M-1)R | both; FIRST r query |
| w | 0 | return; returned-q ending query |
| t_D=w+(M-1)q | (M-1)Q | return; FIRST returned-q query |

The affine anchors are distinct. The vectors q,r are independent and
orthogonal to y, while y!=0. In the affine coordinate basis y,q,r,
y,t_Q,z,t_R,w,t_D have respective coordinates
(1,0,0),(1,M-1,0),(1,-BM,-M),(1,-BM,-1),
(1,-(B+1)M,-(C+1)M),(1,-BM-1,-(C+1)M).
These are pairwise different for the stated parameters. Neither0 nor
t_P=a e_1 equals any of them: representation of e_1 in the basis
y,q,r has y coefficient -1/A, so t_P has negative y coefficient,
whereas the six displayed vectors have y coefficient1. The points0
and t_P are distinct because a>0. Thus the distinct affine-anchor
counts are six and eight. In particular the
old FIRST r query is retained after returning to q; its lower quadratic
does not become redundant merely because the endpoint z is retained.

Define H_three as ||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of the first six affine
terms, H_return using all eight, and F_three=H_three^*,
F_return=H_return^*. All ordered old prefix inequalities and the
optimum jet were proved in Section 1. The maximal-prefix construction
of accepted fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 therefore applies exactly, preserving EVERY old
prefix position, gradient and value, including repeated queries. Its
proof gives finite convex C1 globally 1-smooth conjugates. Fix these
functions before running their words. Induction on the physical indices
then gives the same prefix updates; no online function change is used.

The new terminal gradients are not assumed equal to the old gradients.
At the terminal, x=v+conv(active anchor slopes) is the precise
subgradient optimality condition. It is checked separately below.
Since each completed H is 1-strongly convex, that condition determines
the unique terminal gradient. Incoming AND outgoing terminal rows hold
for the resulting actual function: apply the interpolation necessity in
fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 to any two of its actual prefix, terminal and
optimum samples. This gives Q_ij<=0 in both orientations for every
ordered pair. The formal Gram is a Gram of the actual vectors in R^3,
hence is positive semidefinite. No queried-prefix comparison was deleted.

## 3. Three-stage terminal, including every boundary

Let L=B+a, E=t_Q-z=M L q+Mr, and

    D_3=||E||^2=2A(L^2-L+B/2),
    v_0=-M epsilon r,
    b_3=<E,v_0>-(M-1)Q=Aa(epsilon-2/M),
    theta_3=(b_3)_+/D_3,    v_3=v_0-theta_3 E.

For a in [1/2,1),
L^2-L+B/2=B^2+B(2a-1/2)+a^2-a>=B^2+B/2-1/4>=B^2.
Thus D_3>=2AB^2 and 0<=theta_3<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8.
This pays the convex-combination range and every denominator sign.

All six affine comparisons can be written explicitly:

* z has value -theta_3<z,E>, with
  <z,E>=2A(1/2-B)L<0. This value is nonnegative, and is
  positive when theta_3>0.
* t_Q minus z has affine value b_3-theta_3 D_3, which is zero
  when b_3>=0 and nonpositive otherwise.
* y and 0 have value zero because y is orthogonal to q and r.
* t_R minus z has value (M-1)(<r,v_3>-R). Here
  <r,v_3>=(MQ/2)(-B epsilon+a theta_3)<0 for epsilon>0.
  At epsilon=0 this difference is instead -(M-1)R<0.
* t_P minus z has value <t_P-z,v_3>-a/M. Directly,
  <t_P-z,v_0>=-a epsilon and
  <t_P-z,E>=2L[A(B-1/2)-a]+a>0, so this difference is negative.

If epsilon<2/M, theta_3=0 and z is active, along with y and 0;
if epsilon=2/M, t_Q also ties but z alone may still be selected.
If epsilon>2/M, z and t_Q are active and theta_3 lies strictly
between zero and one. In every case,

    x_n=z+v_0=v_3+z+theta_3 E in partial H_three(v_3).

At epsilon=0 the terminal gradient and value are zero and z is a
minimum. This covers M=2, epsilon=1 as well, where epsilon=2/M;
there is no hidden positive-theta assumption at that endpoint.

The active z term gives the exact conjugate value by completing a
square:

    V_three=2F_three(x_n)
           =||v_0||^2-(b_3)_+^2/D_3
           =AB epsilon^2-(Aa(epsilon-2/M)_+)^2/D_3.

Since the loss is at most A epsilon^2/(2B^2),

    AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))<=V_three<=AB epsilon^2.

The formula is continuous across the active-set boundary; it was
derived from the full six-anchor maximum, not a terminal ansatz.

## 4. Return terminal and the retained old first-r query

The feasible return parameter set itself implies M>=2B^2>=8 and
epsilon>0. Epsilon=0 and small M outside this set are not conclusions
of the return theorem. Set

    d=C+a=2B+2-1/M,
    E_R=t_R-w=Mq+Md r,
    Dhat_R=1-d+(B/2)d^2,
    D_R=||E_R||^2=2A Dhat_R,
    v_0=-M epsilon q,
    b_R=<E_R,v_0>-(M-1)R
       =2A[(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M)],
    theta_R=b_R/D_R,    v_R=v_0-theta_R E_R.

The derivative of 1-d+Bd^2/2 is Bd-1>0 in this range and
2B+1<d<2B+2<=3B. The exact value at the lower comparison point is

    1-(2B+1)+(B/2)(2B+1)^2
        =2B^3+2B^2-3B/2.

Since 2B^2-3B/2>0 for B>=2, the displayed exact lower-point
identity and monotonicity give Dhat_R>=2B^3.

The upper bound Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3 follows by d<=3B and 1-d<0.
Write eta=(B-1/(2M))epsilon-Ba/(2M). Equivalently
eta=B epsilon-(epsilon+Ba)/(2M)<=B epsilon. For the lower
eta>=B epsilon/2 it suffices that
B M epsilon>=epsilon+Ba. But B M epsilon>=2B^3>=B+1>=Ba+epsilon.
Hence

    2B^3<=Dhat_R<=(9/2)B^3,
    epsilon/(9B^2)<=theta_R<=epsilon/(2B^2)<=1/8,

with b_R>0. In particular the active combination is admissible,
including the boundary M epsilon=2B^2 and epsilon=1 when feasible.

The complete eight-anchor screen is as follows.

1. t_R and w tie because their affine difference is
   b_R-theta_R D_R=0. The w value is positive:
   <w,v_0>=0 and <w,E_R>=A d(1-CB)<0.
2. For the old endpoint z, direct substitution, without omitting t_R,
   gives

       <r,v_R>/(Q/2)
          =[M epsilon d(B-1/2)+(Ba/2)(Bd-1)]/Dhat_R.

   Its first numerator term is at least 2B^2 d(B-1/2).
   Since B-1/2>=3B/4 and d<=3B, this is at least B^2 d^2/2.
   Also B Dhat_R<=B^2 d^2/2. The second numerator term is
   strictly positive, so <r,v_R>>R throughout the feasible scope.
   Using the t_R/w tie, the z-minus-w value equals
   (M-1)(R-<r,v_R>)<0. Thus z is inactive even on the stated
   return parameter boundary; this is not presumed from chronology.
3. <q,v_R>=MQ[-epsilon+theta_R(B-1/(2M))]<0, because
   theta_R B<=epsilon/(2B)<epsilon. Thus t_D is below w.
   The t_Q affine value is (M-1)(<q,v_R>-Q)<0.
4. y and 0 have value zero, below the positive w value.
5. For E_P=t_P-w, the independent first-coordinate calculation is

       <E_P,v_0>=2a epsilon,
       <E_P,E_R>=a(d-2)+A d(CB-1)>=4AB^3.

   Indeed d>=2B+1 and CB-1=2B^2+B-1>=2B^2. Consequently
   <E_P,v_R><=2a epsilon-(4AB/9)epsilon<0, since
   A>=4B+4 and B>=2 imply 4AB/9>2. Subtracting delta_P
   makes the t_P-minus-w comparison still more negative.

These cover all eight terms with the original offsets. Since w and
t_R are active and 0<theta_R<1,

    x_n=w+v_0=v_R+w+theta_R E_R in partial H_return(v_R).

The exact value and loss follow from the active w term:

    V_return=2F_return(x_n)=2A epsilon^2-b_R^2/D_R,
    2A epsilon^2(1-1/(2B))<=V_return<=2A epsilon^2,
    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2.

For the lower value bound, b_R<=2AB epsilon and D_R>=4AB^3
give loss<=A epsilon^2/B. This completes the full feasible parameter
screen, including the epsilon and cap-equality boundaries.

## 5. Complete new minimum sets, attainment and sharp normalization

All positive offsets in the anchor table are strictly positive, since
M>=2, a>0, Q>0 and R>0. At v=0 exactly the zero-offset anchors
are active. The subdifferential of a finite maximum of affine functions
is the convex hull of its active slopes: every such convex combination
is a supporting slope; a slope outside that hull is separated from it
by a direction and cannot satisfy the supporting inequality along that
direction. Therefore

    partial H_three(0)=conv{0,y,z},
    partial H_return(0)=conv{0,y,z,w}.

Each H(0)=0, so its conjugate F>=0. Optimality for the strictly
convex conjugate maximization says F(x)=0 precisely when its maximizer
is v=0, equivalently x in partial H(0). These are the COMPLETE
minimum sets, and the minimum value is attained and is zero.

The first coordinates of y,z,w are respectively 2-A, 1-A+2B,
2-A, all strictly negative. Thus each completed minimum hull lies
in x_1<=0 and contains origin. For any minimum x,
||e_1-x||^2=1-2x_1+||x||^2>=1, with equality only at x=0.
The unique closest minimum is origin and the TRUE radius is exactly
one. Completion shrinks the old minimum cone to these bounded hulls;
radius preservation has been proved from the new hulls, not assumed.

For 0<t<1/M sufficiently small, at v=t e_1 every nonzero
zero-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value, and each
positive-offset anchor has strictly negative affine value too. The
initial one requires t_P,1 t<delta_P, which is exactly t<1/M.
There is an open ball around such v on which the zero affine term
alone wins, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. Optimality gives F(x)=||x||^2/2
on that same open ball, with Hessian I. Since global smoothness one
was already supplied by fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290, it is SHARP. There is no hidden
L or distance renormalization. The exact terminal values in Sections
3 and 4 are thus actual attained payoffs in the W_n=2R_n normalization
in R^3.

## 6. Exact growing parameters and physical counts

For each integer N>=2^60 choose

    M=2N(N+1)+1,   A=M^(1/12),   B=M^(1/120),
    epsilon=M^(-1/3),   T_Cheb=(2N+1)^2=2M-1.

M>2^121 implies B>2. Since A=B^10>=2^9 B=512B>=4B+4 for
B>=2, the first bridge hypothesis holds. Also M^(13/20)>2 in this
range, so M epsilon=M^(2/3)>=2M^(1/60)=2B^2. Thus these
are inside the previously screened parameter sets, with epsilon>0.
Accepted fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf gives each Chi_N mass 2N(N+1)/3. Three copies
have mass M-1 and length 3N. The initial list uses three increasing
copies, every later list three decreasing copies. The physical lengths,
including every bridge, are therefore n_three=9N+3 and n_return=12N+4.
The guards in Section 1 cover every entry without executing the lists.

The exact value bounds give

    V_three>=AB epsilon^2(1-1/(2B^3))>=(7/8)M^(-23/40),
    V_return>=3A epsilon^2/2=(3/2)M^(-7/12).

Since B(1-1/(2B^3))>=15/8>3/2, BOTH payoffs obey the common
lower bound V>=(3/2)M^(-7/12). This uses the B factor in V_three,
not only a comparison of power exponents.

## 7. Every original cut and the quadratic/lens channels

With P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i and T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i at the ORIGINAL
physical index, accepted fact_id b98bb807d514e80d defines

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),
           max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.

Write increasing Chi_N entries as csc^2(k pi/(2N+1)),
k=N,N-1,...,1. If theta=pi/(2N+1), concavity of sine gives
sin((k-1)theta)>=(k-1)sin(k theta)/k. Thus consecutive increasing
entry ratios are at most (k/(k-1))^2<=4. The first entry is <=4/3 for
N>=2, because N pi/(2N+1)>=2pi/5>pi/3. Thus an increasing-copy
entry h is <=9(1+preceding mass in that copy); reversing the copy
gives h<=9(1+following mass in that copy), including the small last
entry. Also sin(pi/(2N+1))>=2/(2N+1) gives max Chi_N<=T_Cheb/4<M.
Within-copy mass remains a lower bound for the corresponding full mass.

The following cut partition is complete. All denominators are at the
physical indices; no schedule compression or mass regrouping is used.

| Physical cut | Bound on its K term | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| ordinary entry in L_0 | 41/(AM) | squared prefix ratio<=81; following mass includes AM+1 |
| any later ordinary entry | 9/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; h<=M and h<=9(1+T_j) give h^2/(1+2T_j)<=9M |
| AM+1 | A^2/(2BM) | 1+P_j=M; following mass includes BM+1 |
| BM+1 | B^2/(A^2 M) | 1+P_j>=AM; following mass>=M |
| CM+1, return word | C^2/(A^2 M)<=(25/4)B^2/(A^2 M) | same mass guards; C<=5B/2 |
| final 1+M epsilon | epsilon^2/A^2 | preceding mass>=AM; following mass is zero |
| global Huber term | 1/(2AM) | total mass>=AM |

The last ordinary cut is licensed by DECREASING suffix order. An
increasing final list would not justify this table. After substituting
the parameter powers, the slow possible terms include

    A^2/(2BM)=(1/2)M^(-101/120),
    epsilon^2/A^2=M^(-5/6).

All other displayed terms decay faster than M^(-5/6), with constants
at most 41. Since K is a maximum, BOTH complete words satisfy
K_n(h)<=41M^(-5/6).

Every convex quadratic in any finite dimension diagonalizes, and its
normalized error is a convex combination of t product_j(1-h_j t)^2
for 0<=t<=1. Zero eigenvalues contribute no objective gap; choosing a
farther minimizer only increases the denominator. Consequently the
entire quadratic channel is bounded by
Q_n(h)=max_(0<=t<=1)t product_j(1-h_j t)^2.
Conversely, for each t in (0,1], the scalar quadratic f(x)=t x^2/2
from x_0=1 attains that expression; a constant function gives zero
at t=0. Continuity on [0,1] gives the maximum, so this expression is
exactly the full convex-quadratic channel asserted in the statement.

Let P_N denote the polynomial of one Chi_N copy from fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf. On
[0,1], |P_N(t)|^2<=min(1,1/(T_Cheb t)). The second bound uses
|Cheb_(2N+1)(sqrt(t))|<=1. For the first, set sqrt(t)=sin(phi):
the odd Chebyshev identity reduces to
sin((2N+1)phi)/((2N+1)sin(phi)). The finite unit-complex-number
identity sin(k phi)/sin(phi)=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)
exp(i(k-1-2j)phi) for positive integer k gives
|sin(k phi)|<=k|sin(phi)|; the zero-sine case follows by continuity.
At t=0 the polynomial value is one, which is the meaning of its
displayed min bound at zero.

There are nine Chebyshev copies for h_three, twelve for h_return.
With u=T_Cheb t, the actual bridge coefficients divided by T_Cheb
are <=A,B,1 (three), and <=A,B,C,1 (return). For each
c in {A,B,C}, cM+1<=c(2M-1) follows from c(M-1)>=1, and
1+M epsilon<=2M-1 because M>=2, epsilon<=1. The complete
quadratic expressions are therefore bounded by

    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),
    T_Cheb^(-1)u(1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+Cu)^2(1+u)^2
                         min(1,u^(-12)).

For u<=1 use 1+c u<=2c; for u>=1 use 1+c u<=2c u.
The latter products retain u^(-2) and u^(-3), respectively. The
objective at u=0 is zero. T_Cheb>=M and C<=5B/2 give

    Q_n(h_three)<=64 A^2 B^2/T_Cheb<=64M^(-49/60),
    Q_n(h_return)<=256 A^2 B^2 C^2/T_Cheb<=1600M^(-4/5).

This bounds all convex quadratic witnesses, not general scalar nonlinear
or higher-dimensional smooth convex functions.

For the complex comparison the EXACT named universal lens is

    Lens_n={zeta=alpha+i beta:
            alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}.

Accepted fact_id dfde2fa326c908bf puts it inside the full cycle-legal region. If
n>=N then Lens_n is also inside Lens_N, because cot(pi/(n+2))
increases with n. The accepted ellipse proof for an N-copy gives
|zeta||P_N(zeta)|^2<=cosh^2(2)/T_Cheb on Lens_N. When
T_Cheb|zeta|<=1, the direct product gives instead

    |P_N(zeta)|<=exp(sum Chi_N |zeta|)<=exp(1/6)<=cosh(2),

since sum Chi_N=(T_Cheb-1)/6. The last constant comparison follows
from exp(1/6)<=sum_(k>=0)(1/6)^k=6/5 and cosh(2)>=1+2=3.
Combining the two regimes, with
Kc=cosh(2), yields

    |P_N(zeta)|^2<=Kc^2 min(1,1/(T_Cheb|zeta|)).

At zeta=0 the polynomial is one and Gap_h(0)=0 because every edge
weight is zero; the min bound is interpreted by that value. At every
nonzero point of the lens the accepted maximal complex transcript obeys
Gap_h(zeta)<=(2alpha-|zeta|^2)|Poly_n(zeta)|^2<=2|zeta||Poly_n(zeta)|^2.
With u=T_Cheb|zeta|>0, the complete transcript estimates are

    Gap_h_three(zeta)<=2Kc^18 T_Cheb^(-1)u
         (1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-9)),
    Gap_h_return(zeta)<=2Kc^24 T_Cheb^(-1)u
         (1+Au)^2(1+Bu)^2(1+Cu)^2(1+u)^2 min(1,u^(-12)).

For 0<u<=1, bound 1+c u by2c for each c in {A,B,C,1}.
For u>=1, bound it by2c u; the resulting powers are u^(-2)
and u^(-3), respectively, and are at most1. Therefore

    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_three <=128Kc^18M^(-49/60),
    sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h_return<=3200Kc^24M^(-4/5).

If the channel is augmented by the canonical Huber maximum V_lens,
its extra term is covered in the cut table. These bounds assert NOTHING
about sup_(D_h) Gap_h over the larger schedule-dependent complex
region, or about all scalar functions.

## 8. Original all-split benchmark and actual padded horizons

Here C_n,B_n are benchmark sequences, distinct from construction scalars
C,B. Use EXACTLY the accepted all-split recurrences

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted fact_id b98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_crude, rho_crude=log_2(3). This UPPER
bound is the direction needed to prove reference channels small
relative to U. Since rho_crude<8/5 by 3^5<2^8, M>=2N^2 and actual
n<=16N show

    B_n max(K_n,Q_n,sup_(Lens_n) Gap_h)->0.

The slowest reference decay is M^(-4/5)=O(N^(-8/5)), whereas
B_n=O(N^rho_crude). Fixed cosh(2) constants do not affect the limit.
Thus all three named channels are o(U_n^ZJ) on each selected family
at its actual horizon.

To prove V>U a LOWER bound on B_n is necessary. The all-split
recurrences directly give C_n>=Omega(C_(n-1),1)>C_(n-1), and
balanced splits give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j for lambda=1+sqrt(2).
Selecting the split j=n-1 in B_n gives
B_n>=G(C_(n-1),1)>=2C_(n-1)-1. For d_N=floor(log_2 N),
both physical families obey n-1>=2^(d_N+3)-1, and consequently
B_n>=lambda^(d_N+3). Meanwhile M<=5N^2<20*4^(d_N). Hence
the common attained payoff satisfies

    B_n V >= [3lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                       (lambda/2^(7/6))^(d_N) >1.

This is an exact analytic estimate. Explicitly, lambda>12/5,
2^(7/6)<9/4 (equivalently 2^19<3^12), and 20^(7/12)<8
(use 5^7<2^21). Thus the prefactor is >324/125>2 and the
base is >16/15>1. The payoff exceeds U at EVERY claimed
N>=2^60 and the ratio diverges. No fitted profile constant, assumed
benchmark asymptotic, balanced-only defining recurrence, or DP was used.

For an arbitrary sufficiently large integer horizon n, use

    N=floor((n-3)/9),  k=n-(9N+3) in {0,...,8},
    or N=floor((n-4)/12), k=n-(12N+4) in {0,...,11}.

Insert k zeros at the VERY BEGINNING of the appropriate known word.
They repeat the already retained initial jet, so the exact full-prefix
maximum H is unchanged. The same static F has the same terminal value,
sharp smoothness and closest radius. The terminal remains unqueried.

For K, every new zero cut has numerator zero and every old cut's
preceding/following MASS is unchanged. Quadratic products acquire only
factors one. Lens_n shrinks. In the benchmark estimates use this
actual padded n, which satisfies n<=9N+11<=16N for the three-stage
law and n<=12N+15<=16N for the return law at these large N,
and has n-1 at least the unpadded value. Explicit sufficient start
horizons are 9*2^60+3 and 12*2^60+4. Thus the argument supplies
one selected attained word for each large n; it does not quantify over
every length-n word.

## 9. Precisely defined collapsed policy and its zero-tail boundary

This comparison retains the construction's p=e_1/M, initial point e_1,
first ordinary list and selected bridge AM+1. It treats ALL remaining
steps before the final bridge as one ordinary block, whose total is
M_2-1. Directly counting the original masses gives

    M_2=(B+2)M          for h_three,
    M_2=(B+C+3)M=(3B+4)M  for h_return.

Its specified static second slope is

    q_2=(-2e_1+sqrt(2A-4)e_2)/M_2,
    ||q_2||^2=2A/M_2^2.

For the static envelope over conv{0,p,q_2}, a first-stage proximal
point u=(1-t/M)e_1, 1<=t<=M, has p support (1-t/M)/M>=0,
q_2 support -2(1-t/M)/M_2<=0, and zero support0. Thus p is active
and x=u+p has projection p onto that triangle. The projection
regularity argument of Section 1 applies to this compact convex triangle
as well, proving that F_col_old is a legal member of the collapsed class.
After the first bridge, X=-Ae_1. At ordinary partial
mass s with t=1+s in [1,M_2],

    <q_2,X-tq_2>=(M_2-t)||q_2||^2>=0,
    <p,X-tq_2>=(-A+2t/M_2)/M<0.

Thus every physical query of THIS collapsed policy is legal, including
the old BM+1 and CM+1 steps now acting as individual ordinary entries.
Let y_2=X-M_2 q_2. The last queried jet is x=y_2+q_2,
g=q_2, f=||q_2||^2/2. For EVERY legal smooth-convex completion
of THIS entire prefix with its retained zero optimum, the descent
inequality at that jet gives

    f(y_2)<=f(y_2+q_2)-||q_2||^2/2=0.

Since the retained optimum has value zero, y_2 is forced to be a
minimum. The final physical step 1+M epsilon reaches
y_2-M epsilon q_2, and smoothness about this forced minimum gives

    2f(x_n)<=2A(M epsilon/M_2)^2.

The true radius for every such completion is also one, rather than
an assumed inherited denominator. The retained initial jet is
g_0=p and f_0=1/M-1/(2M^2). For any new minimizer x_star of value
zero, the original interpolation necessity from fact_id 1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_(0,star)=f_0+<p,x_star-e_1>+||p||^2/2
             =x_star,1/M<=0.

So all new minima have first coordinate <=0, and the retained origin
is the unique closest one to e_1. This proves the bound for every
completion of that precise prefix with TRUE radius one.

When epsilon>0, the collapsed upper is positive and division is
legitimate. The three-stage witness-to-upper ratio is at least

    [B(B+2)^2/2](1-1/(2B^3)),

and the return witness-to-upper ratio is at least

    (3/4)(3B+4)^2.

Both factors exceed one and diverge on the chosen growing words,
where epsilon=M^(-1/3)>0. In the return parameter range epsilon>0
is automatic. Thus the substantive advertised growing-family comparison
holds, with exactly the stated collapsed policy as the comparison class.

At epsilon=0 in the three-stage parameter range, its formula in Section 3
gives V_three=0, and U_col_three=0. Every collapsed completion has nonnegative
terminal value and the upper bound zero, hence V_col(F_col)=0. Thus the weak
multiplicative inequality has equality at this boundary; its ratio is not
asserted. For epsilon>0, division by the explicitly positive upper comparison
U_col gives the two stated factors. For B>=2,
rho_col_three>=15>1 and rho_col_return>=75>1, so the new payoff is
strictly greater than every payoff in the respective collapsed class. A
particular collapsed completion may have payoff zero; no division by that
payoff is needed. In the return range epsilon>0 is forced by M epsilon>=2B^2.
On the specified growing words B=M^(1/120) tends to infinity, so both factors
tend to infinity. This comparison is confined to the exactly specified
prefix, second slope and retained optimum.

## 10. Original normalization and scope

For either attained function F, its minimum is0, the unique closest
minimizer to e_1 is0, the squared radius is1, and its least global gradient
Lipschitz constant is1. Thus V=2F(x_n) is exactly the project's W_n=2R_n
normalized payoff. Replacing F by L F for any L>0 makes the prescribed
updates x_next=x-(h_i/L)grad(L F)(x)=x-h_i grad F(x), with the same points
and the same payoff. Therefore W_n(h)>=V for the complete function class
in every stated case.

For each of the two specific padded word families, Sections 6--8 prove
V>U_n^ZJ at every integer horizon above its stated threshold, V/U_n^ZJ
tends to infinity, and the three exactly named reference channels are
o(U_n^ZJ), including the canonical Huber augmentation of the universal
lens. All padding counts and benchmark subscripts use actual physical
indices. These are selected-word lower witnesses and comparisons with
specified reference classes only. There is no bound on unrestricted W_n
from above, no extension to all nonlinear scalar witnesses or the full
complex legal region, and no arbitrary-word partition theorem. The original
arbitrary-word exact-profile theorem remains OPEN.

## intuition
An exact full-prefix conjugate maximum preserves every physical jet while improving only the unqueried terminal. Three-stage and returning active sets use six/eight anchors, with the first r anchor retained. The original arbitrary-word theorem remains open.

---
fact_id: d8b59c7d92425121
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
