glossary_introduces:
  B_ah: Positive finite lift scale satisfying B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah); distinct from the benchmark sequence B_n.
  G_ah: Actual lifted gradients indexed as G_ah(i)=(g_i,e_ah(i)/B_ah).
  K_ah: Compact target polytope; in the strict-source lift, conv{0,Y_ah(0),...,Y_ah(n)} in R^(d+1).
  M_ah: Finite maximum of the positive correction divided by the strict distinct-gradient margin, with the complete ordered index set specified in the statement.
  P_ah: Euclidean projection onto K_ah.
  Phi_ah: Single global function min over Y in K_ah of <lambda_ah,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2.
  W_aff_n: Restricted supremum over affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes in the theorem, with at most n+2 vertices, exposed minimum zero, and orthogonal nonzero initial point.
  X_ah: Actual target trajectory indexed as X_ah(i); in the lift (x_i,z_ah(i)/B_ah).
  Y_ah: Lifted proximal generators indexed as Y_ah(i)=(y_ah(i),delta_ah(i)/B_ah), with Y_ah(star)=0.
  d_ah: Half the squared distance to K_ah: d_ah(U)=dist(U,K_ah)^2/2.
  delta_ah: Strict-source heights delta_ah(i)=v_ah(i)-||g_i||^2/2, with optimum height zero.
  e_ah: Finite vertical residual numerators e_ah(i)=z_ah(i)-delta_ah(i).
  f_eps: Regularized normalized source (1-2epsilon)f+(epsilon/2)||x||^2 for 0<epsilon<1/2.
  gamma_ah: Strict interpolation margin (1/ell-1)/2, positive when 0<ell<1.
  lambda_ah: Target linear-height vector; in the lift (0,B_ah). This is distinct from scalar silver-ratio parameters elsewhere in the project.
  q_ah: For each fixed source index i, the tilted convex function f(x)-<g_i,x> used to derive its ordered row.
  r_ah: Projection residual r_ah(U)=U-P_ah(U).
  v_ah: Strict-source sampled values v_ah(i)=f(x_i), including v_ah(star)=0.
  x_i^eps: Actual GD iterate of f_eps from the same x_0 on the fixed word.
  y_ah: Strict-source proximal anchors y_ah(i)=x_i-g_i, with optimum anchor zero.
  z_ah: Finite vertical-coordinate numerators: z_ah(0)=0 and z_ah(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i).
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes represent the full finite-word gradient-descent supremum.

All spaces, norms, and inner products below are finite-dimensional and Euclidean. For every integer n>=0, every finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and every L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of
    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)
over every convex differentiable f:R^d->R with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, where
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n.
The word is fixed before the objective is selected. Empty words, zero steps, and arbitrarily large finite steps are allowed. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2.

For the same n,h, define W_aff_n(h) by taking the supremum of
    2 Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
over the following choices in arbitrary positive finite dimension D. Let K_ah be a compact polytope with at most n+2 vertices, containing 0, and let lambda_ah in R^D satisfy
    <lambda_ah,Y> > 0 for every Y in K_ah with Y!=0.
The degenerate polytope K_ah={0} is allowed. Define the single global objective
    Phi_ah(X)=min_(Y in K_ah){<lambda_ah,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2}.
Choose X_ah(0)!=0 with <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0, and run the actual updates
    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i)), 0<=i<n.

Then every such Phi_ah is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant at most 1, and has minimum value 0 and complete minimum set {0}. In particular the denominator in its payoff is its true squared initial distance to the entire minimum set. For every finite n and nonnegative h,
    W_aff_n(h)=W_n(h).

More precisely, the following constructive assertion holds. Let f:R^d->R be mu-strongly convex and ell-smooth, with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0. Strong convexity means
    f(v)>=f(u)+<grad f(u),v-u>+(mu/2)||v-u||^2
for all u,v; ell-smooth means the gradient is ell-Lipschitz. Run the same word with advertised smoothness 1, so x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i). For all i=0,...,n, including the terminal sample, put
    g_i=grad f(x_i), v_ah(i)=f(x_i),
    y_ah(i)=x_i-g_i, delta_ah(i)=v_ah(i)-||g_i||^2/2.
Use an extra optimum label star with x_star=g_star=y_ah(star)=0 and v_ah(star)=delta_ah(star)=0. Define
    gamma_ah=(1/ell-1)/2,
    z_ah(0)=0,
    z_ah(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i), 0<=i<n,
    e_ah(i)=z_ah(i)-delta_ah(i), 0<=i<=n.
For a real number t let [t]_+=max(t,0). Define the finite scalar
    M_ah=max { [e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))]_+
                    /(gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2):
               0<=i<=n, j in {star,0,...,n}, g_i!=g_j },
where the maximum of an empty set is defined to be zero. For every finite B_ah>0 with B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), put, in R^(d+1),
    lambda_ah=(0,B_ah),
    Y_ah(i)=(y_ah(i),delta_ah(i)/B_ah),
    X_ah(i)=(x_i,z_ah(i)/B_ah),
    G_ah(i)=(g_i,e_ah(i)/B_ah), 0<=i<=n,
    Y_ah(star)=0,
    K_ah=conv{0,Y_ah(0),...,Y_ah(n)}.
Here conv denotes convex hull and the zero in lambda_ah is the horizontal zero vector in R^d. This K_ah, lambda_ah, and X_ah(0) belong to the stated restricted class. Writing P_ah for Euclidean projection onto K_ah, all the following hold simultaneously:
    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),
    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=G_ah(i),
    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2), 0<=i<=n,
    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i), 0<=i<n,
    <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0,
    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||x_0||^2,
    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
       =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).
Thus the horizontal trajectory is exact in one extra coordinate and the terminal objective correction is nonnegative and is of order B_ah^(-2) for this fixed source and word.

For an arbitrary original source, the equality of suprema uses translation of its selected minimizer to zero, division of the objective by L, and then the regularizations
    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2, 0<epsilon<1/2.
For each fixed source and finite word these are epsilon-strongly convex and (1-epsilon)-smooth with unique minimum at zero; their actual terminal values converge to that of the translated, normalized original source. The lift is chosen separately for each epsilon. This covers arbitrary selected original minima, including nonunique original minimum sets.

The assertion includes repeated samples, equal source gradients, zero source gradients, and all normals involving the original terminal. It asserts neither a quantitative convergence rate or benchmark comparison, nor a uniform approximation across words or horizons, attainment of either supremum, simplex geometry, a uniform bound on B_ah, or an adversary selectable from the schedule alone without a source trajectory.

## proof
The proof is self-contained and uses no predecessor or external theorem.

1. Properties of the target class.

For a nonempty compact convex K_ah in a Euclidean space, let P_ah(U) be the unique minimizer of ||U-Y||^2 over Y in K_ah. Existence follows from compactness; uniqueness follows from strict convexity of the squared norm on the convex set. For Y in K_ah, the characterization of Y=P_ah(U) is
    <U-Y,Z-Y> <= 0 for every Z in K_ah.                         (A)
Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance at the endpoint Y of each segment from Y to Z. Conversely, expanding
    ||U-Z||^2=||U-Y||^2-2<U-Y,Z-Y>+||Z-Y||^2
shows that (A) implies Y minimizes squared distance.

Applying (A) at U and V, using the other projection as comparison point, and adding gives
    <P_ah(U)-P_ah(V),U-V> >= ||P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)||^2.
Let r_ah(U)=U-P_ah(U). Expanding ||r_ah(U)-r_ah(V)||^2 gives
    ||r_ah(U)-r_ah(V)||^2
      =||U-V||^2-2<U-V,P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)>
                   +||P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)||^2
      <=||U-V||^2.
In particular r_ah is 1-Lipschitz.

Put d_ah(U)=dist(U,K_ah)^2/2. For any displacement v, using P_ah(U) as a trial point at U+v, and P_ah(U+v) as a trial point at U, gives respectively
    d_ah(U+v)-d_ah(U) <= <r_ah(U),v>+||v||^2/2,
    d_ah(U+v)-d_ah(U) >= <r_ah(U+v),v>-||v||^2/2.
The difference of the two linear terms has absolute value at most ||v||^2 by the Lipschitz bound. Subtracting <r_ah(U),v> therefore leaves an error between -3||v||^2/2 and ||v||^2/2. This proves differentiability and grad d_ah(U)=r_ah(U).

Completing the square in the definition of Phi_ah yields
    Phi_ah(X)=<lambda_ah,X>-||lambda_ah||^2/2+d_ah(X-lambda_ah).
Its unique minimizing Y is P_ah(X-lambda_ah), and
    grad Phi_ah(X)=lambda_ah+r_ah(X-lambda_ah)
                 =X-P_ah(X-lambda_ah).                         (B)
The Lipschitz bound for r_ah proves that this gradient has Lipschitz constant at most 1.

To check convexity directly, fix U,V and t in [0,1]. Let Y_U=P_ah(U-lambda_ah) and Y_V=P_ah(V-lambda_ah). The point tY_U+(1-t)Y_V lies in K_ah. Use it as a trial point at tU+(1-t)V. Its linear height is t<lambda_ah,Y_U>+(1-t)<lambda_ah,Y_V>, and convexity of the squared norm bounds its squared-distance term by the same convex combination of the two squared-distance terms. It follows that
    Phi_ah(tU+(1-t)V)<=t*Phi_ah(U)+(1-t)Phi_ah(V).
All values are finite because the minimized expression is continuous on the nonempty compact K_ah.

The strict exposing condition implies <lambda_ah,Y>>=0 on K_ah, with equality only when Y=0. Both terms in the defining minimum are nonnegative. At X=0 the choice Y=0 gives value zero. If Phi_ah(X)=0, its attained minimizing Y must have zero height and X=Y; hence Y=X=0. Thus argmin Phi_ah={0}. This includes K_ah={0}, for which Phi_ah(X)=||X||^2/2. With advertised smoothness 1, every restricted instance is consequently an original instance with selected minimizer zero. Therefore W_aff_n(h)<=W_n(h) at L=1.

2. Strict source and every ordered original row.

Fix a mu-strongly convex, ell-smooth source with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0, as in the statement. All samples indexed by 0,...,n are its actual iterates, including n; add the genuine optimum sample star. Use the definitions g_i, v_ah(i), y_ah(i), delta_ah(i) and gamma_ah in the statement.

For any differentiable function with ell-Lipschitz gradient, integration along a segment from u to u+w gives the descent inequality
    f(u+w)<=f(u)+<grad f(u),w>+(ell/2)||w||^2.
Indeed, after subtracting the constant and linear terms, the integral remainder is
    integral_(t=0)^1 <grad f(u+tw)-grad f(u),w> dt,
which is at most integral_(t=0)^1 ell*t*||w||^2 dt.

For any ordered sample pair i,j in {star,0,...,n}, consider q_ah(x)=f(x)-<g_i,x>. It is convex and ell-smooth, and grad q_ah(x_i)=0, so x_i is a global minimizer. Applying the descent inequality at x_j with displacement -(1/ell)grad q_ah(x_j) yields
    q_ah(x_i)<=q_ah(x_j-(1/ell)grad q_ah(x_j))
             <=q_ah(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/(2ell).
Define the ordered residual, in the project's usual orientation,
    Q_ij=v_ah(i)-v_ah(j)+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Rearrangement gives
    Q_ij <= -gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2.                            (C)
Substituting x_i=y_ah(i)+g_i and v_ah(i)=delta_ah(i)+||g_i||^2/2 also gives exactly
    Q_ij=delta_ah(i)-delta_ah(j)+<g_i,y_ah(j)-y_ah(i)>.          (D)
With i=star, Q_(star,j)=-delta_ah(j); thus
    delta_ah(j)>=gamma_ah||g_j||^2>=0.                         (E)

Adding the strong-convexity inequalities in the two directions gives
    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j> >= mu||x_i-x_j||^2.
Therefore equal gradients imply equal points, equal source values, equal y_ah, and equal delta_ah. A zero gradient forces x_i=0, and hence y_ah(i)=delta_ah(i)=0. Conversely delta_ah(i)=0 forces g_i=0 by (E), and then Y_ah(i)=0 in the forthcoming construction. The argument covers coincident labels, repetitions, and zero steps without deleting any index.

3. Simultaneous normal tests for one fixed lift.

Define the finite scalars z_ah(i), e_ah(i) by the statement. They depend only on the fixed finite source trajectory and word. They are finite and independent of B_ah, since their definitions use only finitely many affine operations on finite source data and finite steps. Form lambda_ah, Y_ah, X_ah, G_ah and K_ah exactly as stated.

The convex hull K_ah is compact and has at most n+2 vertices. Its listed generators have
    <lambda_ah,Y_ah(i)>=delta_ah(i)>=0.
Each nonzero listed generator has strictly positive height by (E) and the zero-gradient implication. A convex combination of these generators and zero can have zero height only if every positive weight is on a zero generator. Such a combination equals zero. Consequently lambda_ah strictly exposes zero on all of K_ah.

For every 0<=i<=n and every j in {star,0,...,n}, expand:
    <G_ah(i)-lambda_ah,Y_ah(j)-Y_ah(i)>
      =<g_i,y_ah(j)-y_ah(i)>
          +(e_ah(i)/B_ah-B_ah)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))/B_ah
      =Q_ij+e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))/B_ah^2.            (F)
When g_i=g_j, the source points and heights agree, so Y_ah(i)=Y_ah(j), Q_ij=0, and the correction term is zero. The lifted points X_ah(i) need not agree on a horizontal revisit, and no agreement of their vertical coordinates is assumed or needed.

For g_i!=g_j, the denominator gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2 in M_ah is strictly positive. There are finitely many such pairs, so M_ah is finite. If B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), the positive part of the correction in (F) is at most gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2/2. This follows directly from the defining maximum when M_ah>0. If M_ah=0 every such positive part is zero. Combining with (C), the normal in (F) is at most
    -gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2/2
on every distinct-gradient pair, and zero on every equal-gradient pair.

Since these comparison points generate K_ah, linearity in the comparison point proves
    <X_ah(i)-lambda_ah-Y_ah(i),Z-Y_ah(i)> <=0
for every Z in K_ah and every i=0,...,n. This includes the terminal as the projecting sample and as a comparison generator, all ordered remote pairs, and every sample-to-optimum normal. At the actual lifted optimum X=0, the projection normal at Y=0 is <-lambda_ah,Z><=0 for every Z in K_ah, so P_ah(-lambda_ah)=0 also holds.

The normal test (A) and gradient formula (B) now give
    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),
    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=X_ah(i)-Y_ah(i)=G_ah(i)
for every i=0,...,n. Evaluation at this actual minimizing Y gives
    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))
      =delta_ah(i)+||G_ah(i)||^2/2
      =v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2).                           (G)
If a source gradient is zero while z_ah(i) is nonzero, Y_ah(i)=0 but the lifted vertical residual is retained. Its normals against nonzero generators are distinct-gradient pairs in (F), so the same simultaneous bound controls them.

4. Actual dynamics, radius, and terminal domination.

For each original update 0<=i<n,
    X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i)
      =(x_i-h_i g_i,(z_ah(i)-h_i e_ah(i))/B_ah)
      =(x_(i+1),((1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i))/B_ah)
      =X_ah(i+1).
The equality of G_ah(i) with the gradient of the single static function Phi_ah proves that all these points are its actual GD trajectory. The entire source trajectory and finite scalars were fixed before B_ah and the polytope were chosen. The function is fixed before the lifted run, including its terminal, and is never reselected during that run.

Since z_ah(0)=0, the initial point is X_ah(0)=(x_0,0), is nonzero, and has inner product zero with lambda_ah. The complete minimizer set was proved to be {0}, so
    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||X_ah(0)||^2=||x_0||^2.
Using (G) at the actual terminal gives
    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
      =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).
This proves every assertion of the constructive statement. The extra term is nonnegative. For fixed source and word its coefficient is fixed and finite, so it is of order B_ah^(-2). Terminal domination already holds at each allowed finite B_ah.

5. Arbitrary sources, selected minima, and equality of suprema.

First work at advertised smoothness 1. Choose an arbitrary original source, any one of its minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star. Translate coordinates by x_star and subtract f(x_star). In these coordinates f(0)=0=min f, grad f(0)=0, and the denominator is the positive number ||x_0||^2. No uniqueness or closest-point assumption has been made for the source's selected minimizer.

For each 0<epsilon<1/2, put
    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2.
Convexity of f and positivity of 1-2epsilon give epsilon-strong convexity. Its gradient has Lipschitz constant at most
    (1-2epsilon)+epsilon=1-epsilon<1.
Both terms are nonnegative; equality at zero and the positive quadratic show that its unique minimum is zero at zero.

Let x_i^eps be the actual trajectory of f_eps from this same x_0 with the same h. For each fixed finite word these trajectories converge to the original trajectory as epsilon decreases to zero. The initial points agree. If x_i^eps tends to x_i, then these points are bounded near epsilon=0 and continuity of grad f gives
    grad f_eps(x_i^eps)
      =(1-2epsilon)grad f(x_i^eps)+epsilon x_i^eps
      ->grad f(x_i).
The update with the fixed finite h_i then gives x_(i+1)^eps->x_(i+1). Finite induction proves convergence at n. Continuity of f and boundedness of x_n^eps imply
    f_eps(x_n^eps)
      =(1-2epsilon)f(x_n^eps)+(epsilon/2)||x_n^eps||^2
      ->f(x_n).

For EACH epsilon the constructive assertion applies to this strict source with mu=epsilon and ell=1-epsilon. It gives some restricted instance, in one extra dimension and with at most n+2 vertices, whose payoff is at least 2f_eps(x_n^eps)/||x_0||^2. Consequently
    W_aff_n(h)>=2f_eps(x_n^eps)/||x_0||^2
for every epsilon. The left side is the same fixed-word supremum throughout. Taking the limit in these scalar inequalities yields
    W_aff_n(h)>=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.
Since the original source, selected minimizer, dimension, and initial point were arbitrary, W_aff_n(h)>=W_n(h). Together with the reverse inclusion inequality proved in paragraph 1, this establishes equality at advertised smoothness 1.

For any L>0, translate the selected minimizer and replace the original objective by (f-f(x_star))/L. The resulting source is 1-smooth. Its updates with h are exactly the original updates with h/L on f, and its normalized payoff is exactly 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2). Conversely, multiplication of any restricted Phi_ah by L supplies an admissible L-smooth original objective with the same trajectory and payoff. Thus the same equality holds for every advertised L and respects W_n=2R_n.

The order of choices is fixed n,h; arbitrary original source and selected minimizer; each epsilon; its actual finite run; a finite B_ah for that run; then one restricted instance. No exchange of a horizon limit with a supremum, no uniform choice of B_ah, and no convergence of the lifted polytopes is used. If n=0 the sample set is {star,0}, the recurrence has no updates, and every displayed construction and inequality still applies. Zero steps, repeated samples, equal gradients, source arrivals at zero, arbitrary finite dimensions, and large finite nonnegative steps were covered without deleting any sample. This proves only the stated representation equality and constructive identities.

## intuition
Strict smoothness supplies simultaneous strict ordered interpolation margins, while strong convexity resolves equal-gradient degeneracies. A vertical coordinate realizes all sampled proximal heights as one linear functional on a finite polytope. The exact lifted trajectory retains the initial radius and gains a nonnegative terminal objective correction; regularization recovers arbitrary original sources at each fixed finite word.

## DOC-001549 — handoff_20261010/materials/facts/dfde2fa326c908bf.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010\materials\facts\dfde2fa326c908bf.md

原始字节数：14623；原始 SHA-256：e0a4c20dfe36761df6daedb9c62aa96ba05c5b165024d0cd37dbbd173d2d0173

证据标签：historical_fact_file_check_original_scope。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001549 -->
---
fact_id: dfde2fa326c908bf
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max2
predecessors: [b98bb807d514e80d]
glossary_introduces:
  Area_C: Signed doubled polygon area sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) over a directed cycle.
  Cheb_j: Chebyshev polynomials Cheb_0=1, Cheb_1=x, Cheb_{j+1}=2xCheb_j-Cheb_{j-1}.
  Chi_n: Any ordering of the n positive reciprocal squared Chebyshev roots specified in the statement.
  D_h: Set of complex parameters satisfying every stated smooth-convex interpolation cycle.
  Energy_C: Sum of squared edge lengths of that directed cycle.
  Gap_h: The maximal normalized terminal error -2ell_n for the given complex-polynomial transcript.
  H: The maximum of shifted quadratic functions whose convex conjugate constructs the interpolant.
  I_n: Finite interpolation index set {*,0,...,n}.
  Lens_n: Explicit universal legal lens alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2)).
  Poly_i: Ordered prefix polynomial product_{t<i}(1-h_t z), with Poly_0=1.
  V_lens: Maximum of the universal complex-lens witness supremum and the canonical sum-based Huber witness.
  ell_i: Maximum q-weight over paths i->*, with empty optimum path allowed.
  gamma_i: Complex gradient z Poly_i(z), identified with a vector of R^2; gamma_*=0.
  q_ij: Smooth interpolation lower-increment edge weight on directed edge j->i.
  v_i: Maximal interpolating function value -ell_i with optimum value zero.
  xi_i: Complex prefix point Poly_i(z), identified with a vector of R^2; xi_*=0.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Complex-polynomial transcripts, an explicit legal spectral lens, and a quantitative limit of that lens.

For each integer n>=1 and each real nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), define Poly_0(z)=1 and Poly_i(z)=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1-h_t z) for 1<=i<=n. Identify the complex plane with the real Euclidean plane, with inner product <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). For z=alpha+i beta set xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z) for 0<=i<=n, and xi_*=gamma_*=0. Put kappa=alpha-|z|^2 and I_n={*,0,...,n}. For each ordered pair define
q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2,
regarded as the weight on the directed edge j->i. For each directed simple cycle C in I_n define
Area_C=sum_{j->i in C} Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),
Energy_C=sum_{j->i in C}|xi_i-xi_j|^2.
Define D_h to be the set of z satisfying kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for every such cycle.

(a) The data (xi_i,gamma_i), including the optimum sample *, are interpolable by a finite everywhere-defined convex differentiable function f:R^2->R with 1-Lipschitz gradient and grad f(0)=0 if and only if z belongs to D_h. For z in D_h let ell_i be the largest q-weight of a directed path i->*, allowing the empty path at *, and put v_i=-ell_i. The maxima exist over simple paths and ell_*=0. There is such an f with f(xi_i)=v_i for every i. These values are simultaneously maximal among all interpolating f satisfying f(0)=0. Its exact GD recursion for h starts at xi_0=1, and its normalized terminal error is
Gap_h(z):=-2ell_n.
In particular
|z|^2|Poly_n(z)|^2 <= Gap_h(z) <= (2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.

(b) The parameter lens
Lens_n={alpha+i beta: alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}
is contained in D_h for every h.

(c) Let Cheb_j be the polynomials defined by Cheb_0(x)=1, Cheb_1(x)=x and Cheb_{j+1}(x)=2xCheb_j(x)-Cheb_{j-1}(x). For every n>=2 define the positive schedule Chi_n, in any permutation, by the n entries
sec^2((2k-1)pi/(2(2n+1))), k=1,...,n.
Its terminal polynomial is (-1)^n Cheb_{2n+1}(sqrt(z))/((2n+1)sqrt(z)), interpreted as a polynomial including z=0, and its sum is s(Chi_n)=2n(n+1)/3. For all z in Lens_n,
Gap_{Chi_n}(z) <= 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.

For an arbitrary schedule of length n>=1 define
V_lens(h)=max{sup_{z in Lens_n} Gap_h(z), 1/(2s(h)+1)}.
Let W_n(h) be the project worst-case normalized objective error, namely the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and s(h)=sum h_t. Then V_lens(h)<=W_n(h). For the exact benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1) defined by the recursions in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, and p=log_2 3 from that predecessor, the explicit schedules satisfy
0<=V_lens(Chi_n)/U_n^ZJ
 <= 2cosh^2(2)(1+4n^p)/n^2 ->0.
Thus the universal-lens witnesses, even augmented by the canonical sum-based Huber witness, cannot prove the required uniform matching lower bound. This is a limitation of that witness family only, not an upper bound on W_n(Chi_n) and not a decision of the unrestricted minimax target.

## proof
1. Necessary smooth-convex inequality.
For any convex differentiable 1-smooth f and any x,y, put k(u)=f(u)-<grad f(y),u>. Convexity and grad k(y)=0 imply k(y)<=k(u). Integrating the Lipschitz gradient along a segment gives k(u+d)<=k(u)+<grad k(u),d>+||d||^2/2. Take d=-grad k(u) and combine these inequalities to obtain
f(x)>=f(y)+<grad f(y),x-y>+||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2/2. (1)
Applied to any proposed interpolant, (1) says its values obey f_i-f_j>=q_ij.

2. Cycle identity and necessity.
For complex u,v, <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Expand
<z xi_j,xi_i-xi_j>
=alpha Re(conj(xi_j)(xi_i-xi_j))+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).
The real part is (|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2-|xi_i-xi_j|^2)/2. Also
||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2=|z|^2|xi_i-xi_j|^2.
Therefore
q_ij=(alpha/2)(|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2)+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i)
      -(kappa/2)|xi_i-xi_j|^2. (2)
The norm-difference terms telescope on a cycle, whose weight is beta Area_C-kappa Energy_C/2. Both orientations are available, and their areas have opposite signs but equal energies. Consequently nonpositivity of every cycle is equivalent to the definition of D_h. Summing (1) on cycles proves the necessary implication in (a). The cycle *->0->* also forces kappa>=0.

3. Values from paths.
Assume z is in D_h. Every closed walk is a sum of simple directed cycles, after splitting at repeated vertices; thus every closed walk has nonpositive weight. Any path to * can have repeated vertices deleted, and deleting the intervening nonpositive cycle cannot reduce the weight. There are finitely many simple paths on I_n, so ell_i is finite and is a maximum over them. At * the empty path has weight zero and every closed walk has weight at most zero, so ell_*=0.
Concatenate edge j->i with a maximum path i->*, then delete cycles if needed. It follows that ell_j>=q_ij+ell_i. Hence v_i=-ell_i satisfies v_i-v_j>=q_ij. For any other feasible values F_i with F_*=0, summing the inequalities along a path i->* yields -F_i at least the weight of that path. Thus F_i<=-ell_i=v_i for every i.

4. A finite-dimensional global interpolant.
Define on the real plane
H(g)=max_{i in I_n}{<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i+<xi_i,g-gamma_i>+||g-gamma_i||^2/2}.
This is the maximum of finitely many quadratics with Hessian equal to the identity. It is finite, continuous and 1-strongly convex. The inequality v_j-v_i>=<gamma_i,xi_j-xi_i>+||gamma_j-gamma_i||^2/2 is exactly the assertion that at g=gamma_i the j-th quadratic is at most the i-th quadratic. Thus
H(gamma_i)=<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i.
The i-th quadratic is active and has derivative xi_i at gamma_i. Its supporting affine function is below H, so xi_i belongs to the subdifferential of H at gamma_i.

Define f(x)=sup_g{<x,g>-H(g)}. For each fixed x, the maximizer g(x) exists and is unique: H(g)-<x,g> is continuous, strongly convex, and coercive because even any one term of the defining maximum has leading term ||g||^2/2. Optimality says x belongs to the subdifferential of H at g(x). Strong convexity implies that for all x,y,
<x-y,g(x)-g(y)>>=||g(x)-g(y)||^2.
To justify this implication, strong convexity and x in partial H(u) imply H(v)>=H(u)+<x,v-u>+||v-u||^2/2: combine H((1-t)u+tv)>=H(u)+t<x,v-u> with the strong-convexity upper bound along the segment, divide by t>0, and let t decrease to zero. Write this inequality for u=g(x),v=g(y) and in the reverse direction, then add. Cauchy--Schwarz gives ||g(x)-g(y)||<=||x-y||.
The definition of f yields, for every displacement d,
<g(x),d><=f(x+d)-f(x)<=<g(x+d),d>.
It follows that the difference between f(x+d)-f(x) and <g(x),d> lies in [0,||d||^2]. Therefore f is differentiable with gradient g(x), and that gradient is 1-Lipschitz. It is finite by existence of the maximizers and convex as a supremum of affine functions. The subgradient condition xi_i in partial H(gamma_i) gives g(xi_i)=gamma_i and f(xi_i)=v_i. At the optimum sample this gives f(0)=0 and grad f(0)=0, so convexity makes 0 a minimizer.

The prefix-polynomial identity Poly_{i+1}=(1-h_i z)Poly_i gives xi_{i+1}=xi_i-h_i gamma_i. Thus the interpolation is a legal GD trajectory, with ||xi_0||=1. Apply (1) in both directions between n and *:
v_n>=||gamma_n||^2/2,
v_n<=<gamma_n,xi_n>-||gamma_n||^2/2.
Since <gamma_n,xi_n>=alpha|Poly_n(z)|^2, these are the two bounds in (a).

5. A discrete area-energy inequality.
Consider any r>=2 complex numbers w_0,...,w_{r-1}, with w_r=w_0. Put
d_k=(1/r)sum_{j=0}^{r-1}w_j exp(-2pi i k j/r), 0<=k<r.
The finite geometric-sum identity reconstructs w_j=sum_k d_k exp(2pi i k j/r). The same identity, on expanding the quadratic sums, gives
sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})=r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin(2pi k/r),
sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2=4r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin^2(pi k/r).
The k=0 terms vanish. For 1<=k<r, the ratio of the absolute sine coefficient in the first sum to the coefficient in the second is (1/2)|cot(pi k/r)|<=cot(pi/r)/2. The triangle inequality proves
|sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})| <= (cot(pi/r)/2)sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2. (3)
This also covers r=2, where the left side and cot(pi/2) are zero.
Every simple cycle on I_n has length r<=n+2. The function cot(pi/r) increases as the integer r>=2 increases. From (3) and the defining inequality for Lens_n one gets kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C|. This proves (b).

6. Explicit real positive Chebyshev steps and their sum.
The recurrence for Cheb_j, the cosine addition formula, and induction give Cheb_j(cos t)=cos(jt). Its parity is the parity of j and its degree is j. Put m=2n+1. The odd polynomial Cheb_m(x) is divisible by x; hence (-1)^n Cheb_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is a real polynomial of degree n. Differentiating Cheb_m(cos t)=cos(mt) at t=pi/2 gives Cheb_m'(0)=(-1)^n m, so its value at z=0 is 1.
The positive roots of Cheb_m are cos((2k-1)pi/(2m)), k=1,...,n. Squaring them gives exactly the distinct roots lambda_k=cos^2((2k-1)pi/(2m)) in (0,1) of the degree-n polynomial. It follows from the value at zero that the polynomial equals product_k(1-z/lambda_k). Thus its reciprocal roots are precisely the stated positive schedule, regardless of their order.

Twice differentiating the cosine identity and eliminating the angle gives the polynomial identity
(1-x^2)Cheb_m''(x)-xCheb_m'(x)+m^2 Cheb_m(x)=0.
Differentiation at x=0 gives Cheb_m'''(0)=-(m^2-1)Cheb_m'(0). The coefficient of z in (-1)^n Cheb_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is consequently -(m^2-1)/6. The coefficient of z in the product of schedule factors is minus their sum. Hence s(Chi_n)=(m^2-1)/6=2n(n+1)/3.

7. The lens lies in an ellipse of small width.
Let n>=2, N=n+2, theta=pi/N, t=tan(theta)>0, and u=asinh(t/2)>0. For z=alpha+i beta in Lens_n,
0<=alpha<=1 and |beta|<=t alpha(1-alpha),
because alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=0 and dropping beta^2 only weakens the defining inequality. Put s=alpha(1-alpha), so 0<=s<=1/4. Since sinh(u)=t/2 and cosh(u)>=1,
(2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(u)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(u)
 <=1-4s+16s^2<=1. (4)

For completeness (4) implies there is a complex w with |Im w|<=u and z=(1+cos w)/2. If beta=0, take a real w with cos w=2alpha-1. If beta!=0, the continuous function
v -> (2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(v)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(v)
decreases from infinity as v increases from zero and is at most 1 at v=u. Choose v in (0,u] where its value is 1, and choose a real angle a with
cos a=(2alpha-1)/cosh(v), sin a=-2beta/sinh(v).
Then w=a+i v has cos w=2alpha-1+2i beta.
A square root of z is cos(w/2). The cosine identity for Cheb_m holds for complex arguments as well, because it follows from polynomial recurrence and the complex cosine addition formula. Thus
|z| |Poly_n(z)|^2
 =|Cheb_m(sqrt(z))|^2/m^2
 =|cos(mw/2)|^2/m^2
 <=cosh^2(m u/2)/m^2. (5)
The polynomial interpretation covers z=0 as well; both sides of the equality before the bound are zero there.

The inequality asinh(t/2)<=t/2 follows by integrating its derivative, which is at most 1 on the nonnegative line. The function tan is convex on [0,pi/4], so it lies below its chord and tan(theta)<=4theta/pi=4/N. Therefore m u/2<=m t/4<=m/N<2. Combining (5) with (a) and alpha<=|z| proves
Gap_{Chi_n}(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2
 <=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2<=2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.

8. Canonical Huber witness and the exact-profile comparison.
For any h>=0 let S=s(h) and delta=1/(2S+1). In dimension one define
f_delta(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise.
The matching derivative is x clipped to [-delta,delta], hence is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The function is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth and minimized at 0. Starting from x_0=1, every partial sum of h lies in [0,S]. Induction gives x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-delta S=(S+1)/(2S+1)>=delta. Thus every gradient equals delta and
2f_delta(x_n)=2delta(1-delta S)-delta^2=1/(2S+1).
Multiplying either this function or the interpolant from part 4 by L produces the same normalized error for the specified arbitrary L>0. Consequently each term defining V_lens(h) is a legal lower-bound witness for W_n(h), proving V_lens(h)<=W_n(h).

For Chi_n the Huber term is
1/(1+4n(n+1)/3)<=3/(4n^2).
Part 7 bounds its lens term by 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2. Since 2cosh^2(2)>=2>3/4, both terms are at most 2cosh^2(2)/n^2.
Verified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d states, for exactly the project phi/psi recursions and the project normalization, that B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2. Multiply the preceding bound on V_lens by B_n to obtain the displayed ratio estimate. It tends to zero because n^{-2}->0 and n^{p-2}->0. This uses no assumed limit for S_bullet(n)/n^rho and no numerical estimate of the profile constant.

Only the explicitly restricted witness family was upper bounded. None of the calculations gives an upper bound for the unrestricted W_n on the displayed schedules.

## intuition
All chronological prefix-polynomial points must satisfy interpolation cycles, not merely pairwise curvature conditions. A Fourier area-energy inequality yields a legal universal complex lens. A Chebyshev schedule remains O(n^-2) on that lens, showing exactly why this simplification cannot match the project profile. The full schedule-dependent cycle region is left open.

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010\materials\facts\e9a55f57461f253c.md

证据标签：historical_fact_file_check_original_scope。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001550 -->
---
fact_id: e9a55f57461f253c

author: max3
predecessors: [1252b8823bb5a290]
glossary_introduces:
  D59: The coordinate and core-value denominator10^15; denoted D locally in the proof.
  F59: The specified66-dimensional convex1-smooth conjugate interpolant with unique minimizer0.
  G59_i: Gradient of F59 at X59_i, including the unqueried terminal gradient.
  H59_j: Finite words defined recursively by H59_0 empty and H59_(j+1)=(H59_j,1+a_59*c_59^j,H59_j),j0through5.
  J59: The63by63 Gram matrix of the chronological gradient differences jump59_i.
  M59: The fixed mass1+S59.
  R59: True initial distance to the unique minimizer0 of the specified function; denoted R locally.
  S59: Sum of all63 entries of h59.
  T59: The65by65 lower-triangular integer matrix specified by the inline defining data; denoted T locally in the proof.
  X59_i: Actual GD iterate of F59 at indexi,0through63.
  a_59: Physical initial bridge parameter5(sqrt2-1)/7 for the fixed H6 word.
  c_59: Physical geometric parameter(7/6)(1+sqrt2) for the fixed H6 word.
  eta59: Exact rational residual allowance1/500000000000, denoted eta locally.
  h59: The fixed63-step word H59_6.
  jump59_i: Chronological gradient difference G59_i-G59_(i-1),1through63.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
A finite critical H6 smooth-convex adversary with true-distance payoff above four times the proposed reciprocal mass, and a restriction on its gradient jumps. Set c_59=(7/6)(1+sqrt(2)) and a_59=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7. Define finite words H59_0=() and H59_(j+1)=(H59_j,1+a_59*c_59^j,H59_j) for integers j=0,...,5. Let h59=H59_6, which has length63, let S59=sum_(i=0)^62 h59_i, and let M59=1+S59. There exist an explicitly specified convex differentiable function F59:R^66->R with1-Lipschitz gradient and argmin F59={0}, and an initial point X59_0!=0, such that, with X59_(i+1)=X59_i-h59_i*G59_i and G59_i=grad F59(X59_i) for i=0,...,63 (only i<63 is used in the recursion), one has
2(F59(X59_63)-F59(0))/dist(X59_0,argmin F59)^2 > 56/2711 > 1/50 > 4/M59.
In particular the unrestricted worst-case value W_63(h59) defined in predecessor1252b8823bb5a290 exceeds56/2711 and4/M59. For this same full gradient sequence define jump59_i=G59_i-G59_(i-1),1<=i<=63, and J59_ij=<jump59_i,jump59_j>. Its increment Gram satisfies
2 sum_(1<=i<j<=63)|J59_ij| > 5 trace(J59).
Consequently J59 cannot be expressed as a finite sum of positive semidefinite matrices each supported on at most six of its63 coordinate indices. Equivalently as a necessary obstruction, there is no Euclidean realization of the same full gradient Gram with an orthonormal coordinate system in which every scalar coordinate of the sequence(G59_0,...,G59_63) changes value at most six times. The witness can be rescaled to initial distance1 without changing the schedule or normalized payoff. Multiplying its objective by any L>0 gives the same normalized payoff for L-smooth GD with steps h59_i/L. This is a statement about the fixed actual H6 word with epsilon1/6 only, not an asymptotic assertion or a statement about63-step subwords whose enclosing horizon changes their parameters.

## proof
Verification scope and aliases. This is the single selected C59 package under verification_allocation_c59.json, requesting independent gpt-6-astra/max checking. All finite arithmetic can be checked in under a second by the READ-ONLY standard-library audit appended here, using the frozen defining integers also appended here. No optimization or reconstruction is needed. The audit recomputes physical positions and all4160 interpolation expressions from the coordinates directly and does not trust saved diagnostics. Please check the symbolic argument as well as these integer comparisons.

In the following proof, c,a,H_j,h,S,M6,F,X_i,G_i,R²,J are local aliases of c_59,a_59,H59_j,h59,S59,M59,F59,X59_i,G59_i,dist(X59_0,argmin F59)^2,J59 in the statement. All other local symbols are explicitly defined. Only accepted predecessor1252b8823bb5a290 is used.

# C59: a fully specified legal H6 adversary above 4/M6

Status: **exact finite reconstruction and joint proof candidate; UNVERIFIED**.
No new fact is claimed and no verifier submission is allocated. The only
accepted mathematical predecessor is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for full
smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate construction.

This is a finite diagnosis of the actual H6 word, not an asymptotic theorem.
The word here uses epsilon=1/6. It is not the length-63 subword obtained by
fixing epsilon=1/m for a much larger horizon m.

## 1. Statement and exact input object

Put

    c=(7/6)(1+sqrt(2)), a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<6,
    h=H6, n=63, S=sum_(i<63)h_i, M6=1+S.

There is a completely specified convex differentiable 1-smooth function
on R^66 with unique minimizer0 and an actual GD trajectory for this h
such that

    2(F(X_63)-F(0))/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2
             > 56/2711 > 1/50 > 4/M6.              (1)

In particular, this finite word cannot have a full valid upper certificate
at level1/M6. No assertion is made about eventual large-m feasibility.

The rational construction data are the lower-triangular integer matrix T
of order65 and the64 integer values v_i in

    research/max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json.

The PUBLIC file's SHA256 is

    7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113.

Its fields `coordinate_integer_rows_lower_triangular` and
`function_value_numerators` are part of the definition, with the common
denominator D=10^15. For reproducibility the complete defining integer
data are also appended to the shared proof finding. No numerical solver
claim is used as a premise of the construction below.

Let the rational vectors in R^65 be

    v_start=T[0,:]/D,  q_i=T[i+1,:]/D (0<=i<=63),
    u_i=v_i/D.

Define the core positions by the EXACT algebraic GD recursion

    z_0=v_start,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i q_i.                            (2)

Here q_63 is not used in (2), but will be the core terminal gradient.
The original numerical Gram is not treated as an exact Gram. These
rational vectors define their own positive semidefinite Gram by dot
products, regardless of any properties of the source solver output.

## 2. The finite exact checks, with their integer certificate

For these core data include the zero optimum jet. Define

    Q^core_ij=u_i-u_j+<q_i,z_j-z_i>+||q_i-q_j||^2/2,
    delta^core_i=u_i-||q_i||^2/2,

with the star's position, gradient and value all zero. The following
strict bounds are verified by integer comparisons of the defining data:

    Q^core_ij < eta=1/500000000000 for EVERY i!=j,
    delta^core_i > -eta for every finite i,
    ||v_start||^2 < 1001/1000,
    u_63 > 21/2000,
    200<S<256,  h_i>1 for every i.                  (3)

The first line checks all65*64=4160 ordered pairs, including both optimum
directions and both terminal directions. Here are the exact formulas used
to audit (3), so the finite part is not a feasibility-tolerance assertion.

The data file gives each step as

    h_t=(A_t+B_t sqrt(2))/54432

with integer arrays A_t,B_t. These are exactly the recursively defined
steps: arithmetic in Q(sqrt(2)) with a=(-5/7,5/7) and c=(7/6,7/6)
produces those integer pairs. Let

    G_ab=sum_k T[a,k]T[b,k].

Thus <q_i,q_j>=G_(i+1,j+1)/D^2 and
<q_i,v_start>=G_(i+1,0)/D^2. Set Hden=54432 and define integer arrays

    R_ij=Hden G_(i+1,0)-sum_(t<j) A_t G_(i+1,t+1),
    I_ij=                  -sum_(t<j) B_t G_(i+1,t+1).

Then <q_i,z_j>=(R_ij+I_ij sqrt(2))/(Hden D^2).
When j=star, set both of these numerators to zero. When i=star, every
term containing q_i is zero. Set v_star=0 and the corresponding gradient
Gram entries to zero. The exact pair numerator formula is

    Qa_ij=2 Hden D(v_i-v_j)+2(R_ij-R_ii)
                   +Hden(G_(i+1,i+1)+G_(j+1,j+1)-2G_(i+1,j+1)),
    Qb_ij=2(I_ij-I_ii),
    Q^core_ij=(Qa_ij+Qb_ij sqrt(2))/(2 Hden D^2),    (4)

using the displayed star conventions. The missing diagonal terms for a
star index in (4) are zero.

For the radical, use the rational interval

    Lrad/10^50 < sqrt(2) < (Lrad+1)/10^50,
    Lrad=141421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694.

Its validity follows by squaring the two positive integer endpoints.
For each pair an upper-bound numerator with denominator
2 Hden D^2*10^50 is

    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*(Lrad+1)  if Qb_ij>=0,
    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*Lrad      if Qb_ij<0.          (5)

The data file contains all4160 integer triples from (4)--(5). Every one
satisfies `upper_numerator*500000000000 < denominator`. The largest
upper bound occurs at the directed pair (31,30) and is approximately
1.371014491112536e-12; the exact numerator and denominator are retained
in `audit.max_Q_upper_numerator` and `audit.max_Q_upper_denominator`.
This decimal is descriptive, whereas the integer comparison proves the
inequality used in (3).

The other checks in (3) use the same radical interval for S and each h,
and use, without a radical,

    delta^core_i=(2v_i D-G_(i+1,i+1))/(2D^2),
    ||v_start||^2=G_00/D^2.

The minimum core delta is already positive in these data (approximately
8.5986e-11), but only the weaker stated lower bound is needed. The audit
script is PUBLIC at `research/max3_c59_h6_exact_primal_repair.py`.
Its single finite integer check completed in under0.1second and used no
optimization, search, eigenvalue clipping, or large symbolic computation.
This is a transparent finite arithmetic component of an unverified proof
candidate; it is not a substitute for the verifier's acceptance.

## 3. One controlled auxiliary coordinate repairs all pairs

Choose

    kappa=1/512, t0=1/8,
    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i.

Every factor is positive since h_i<=S<256. For any initial segment,
the elementary product inequality

    product_t(1-a_t)>=1-sum_t a_t  (a_t>=0, sum a_t<1)

follows inductively from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b. It gives

    1>=xi_i>=1-kappa S>1/2.

For i<j, positivity and h_i>1 imply

    xi_i-xi_j>=xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa/2.

The distance to the optimum sample xi_star=0 is also greater than
kappa/2. Thus these bounds cover every distinct pair, including star.

Define the66-dimensional jets and values

    X_i=(z_i,t0 xi_i),
    G_i=(q_i,t0 kappa xi_i),
    f_i=u_i+t0^2 kappa xi_i^2/2
       =u_i+xi_i^2/65536, 0<=i<=63,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (6)

Their GD recursion is EXACT by (2) and the recursion for xi. The full
interpolation residual separates by coordinates into

    Q_ij=Q^core_ij
          -t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2.      (7)

This is a polynomial identity, not a claimed separability of the final
function. The second term is the residual for the single quadratic
auxiliary coordinate at the displayed samples. Since1-kappa>1/2,
its magnitude is strictly greater than

    t0^2 kappa^3/16=2^(-37)>eta.

Combining with (3) proves Q_ij<0 for ALL4160 ordered pairs simultaneously.
The same common values and coordinates are used in every pair.

## 4. One global function and the true initial distance

For each finite sample put

    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.

Equation (6) gives

    delta_i=delta^core_i+t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2
             >-eta+t0^2 kappa/16>0.                (8)

Add the star with Y_star=0,delta_star=0, and define

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,63})
                              [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],
    F(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                         (9)

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and (7), this is one globally defined finite
convex differentiable function with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating
every value and gradient in (6). Therefore its GD trajectory is the one
already specified, with the actual ordered word H6.

The finite set of strict gaps in (8) makes the star's zero affine function
uniquely active in a neighborhood of v=0. Hence H(v)=||v||^2/2 there and
partial H(0)={0}. The maximizing-vector condition for (9) identifies the
minimizers of F with partial H(0), as in the accepted predecessor. Thus
argmin F={0}; no remote or nonclosest minimizer is used in normalization.

The true squared distance and terminal value satisfy

    R^2=||v_start||^2+1/64 < 1001/1000+1/64
                              =8133/8000,
    2F(X_63)=2u_63+t0^2 kappa xi_63^2 >21/1000.

Consequently

    2F(X_63)/R^2> (21/1000)/(8133/8000)
                 =56/2711>1/50>4/(1+S),

which proves (1), subject only to the explicitly supplied finite integer
checks on the defining data. If a unit initial norm is desired, use
F_unit(x)=F(Rx)/R^2 and initial point X_0/R; the smoothness remains1,
the steps remain h, and the normalized payoff is unchanged. Multiplication
of the function by any L>0 supplies the corresponding L-smooth instance
with steps h_i/L.

## 5. A concrete feature of the surviving geometry

The rational core gradients have the exact inner product

    <q_35,q_36>
      =-14715168270572693173715883215/10^30
      <-14/1000.

The extra coordinate changes this by at most t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24),
since0<xi_i<=1. Thus

    <G_35,G_36><-1/100.

Both indices are queried. The actual object retains substantial signed
gradient correlation; it is not a Huber common-gradient line, a mutually
orthogonal gradient transcript, or an all-gradients acute-cone trajectory.
The auxiliary coordinate repairs small interpolation errors in an already
strong correlated core, in contrast to attempting to repair the grossly
illegal fixed scalar C55 template. No claim is made that this description
is a scalable generating rule.

There is also a basis-independent distinction from a bounded-jump
coordinate construction. Let d_i=G_i-G_(i-1),1<=i<=63, and let J be their
Gram matrix. If an orthonormal coordinate system makes every coordinate
of the gradient list change at most k times, then

    2 sum_(i<j)|J_ij| <= (k-1) trace(J).             (10)

Indeed each scalar coordinate of the jump list has at most k nonzero
entries. Cauchy--Schwarz gives (sum|a_i|)^2<=k sum a_i^2, hence
2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|<=(k-1)sum a_i^2. Sum over coordinates and apply the
triangle inequality to the resulting off-diagonal entries. Since J is
unchanged by orthogonal changes of physical coordinates, (10) applies to
every such representation.

For the exact rational core, the integer increment Gram is saved PUBLICLY
in `research/max3_c59_exact_increment_gram.json`. Its entries follow
directly from the rational vectors already defined, with denominator D^2.
Exact integer summation gives

    trace(J_core) approximately 0.44249400386221016,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij| approximately 2.306374594722243,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij|-5trace(J_core)>9/100.       (11)

Only the last, rational inequality is used. The auxiliary jump vector is
a_i=t0 kappa(xi_i-xi_(i-1)); all entries are negative and
(sum|a_i|)^2<t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24). Its contribution is aa^T. By the
triangle inequality, adding aa^T can lower the left side of (11) by at
most

    2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|+5sum_i a_i^2
           <=5(sum_i|a_i|)^2<5*2^(-24).

Thus the full repaired J still satisfies
2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|>5trace(J). Equation (10) rules out every representation
in which all coordinate gradients have at most SIX chronological jumps.
In particular this actual finite adversary is outside the common two-jump
primal cone. The large core margin in (11) ensures this distinction is
not caused only by the tiny auxiliary quadratic coordinate.

## 6. Scope and next open interface

This candidate is finite, exact in its definitions, and includes all
function values, gradients, positions, actual GD equations, and the true
distance. It does not rely on a numerically feasible Gram or a nearly
positive semidefinite dual. The source solver arrays are preserved
unchanged; the rational data and added coordinate are separately labeled
new objects.

The estimate rules out1/M6 for this one actual word. It does NOT refute an
upper bound valid only at sufficiently large horizons, establish a common
pattern for the changing parameters epsilon=1/m, or prove the all-schedule
Zhang--Jiang profile. In particular the length63 block changes physically
as the enclosing horizon changes. The missing scalable interface is an
analytic rule for the correlated core that controls ALL interpolation
pairs and distance under those parameter changes. Repeating this finite
data set, extrapolating its numerical eigenvectors, or fixing only one
turning pair would not provide that rule.

No routine verification allowance was used. This remains a complete
finite proof candidate, not an accepted fact.

## Read-only exact arithmetic checker

The following audit accepts either the inline object above or the frozen public JSON. It does not read the source numerical Gram, solve any optimization, perform a floating-point factorization, or write any file. In particular it recomputes the step word in Q(sqrt(2)), all position coordinates, all4160 original directed residuals, normalization and strict-value bounds, and the increment-Gram inequality. The radical interval is proved by integer squaring. The verifier can invoke audit(data) on the inline object; its __main__ path is only a convenience for the same frozen public data.

```python
"""Read-only exact audit of frozen C59 definitions. Standard library only.

No optimization, reconstruction, Cholesky, NumPy, file writes, or reliance
on the stored audit fields. audit(data) also accepts the inline definitions
in the fact submission, with lower-triangular rows completed by zeros.
"""
from fractions import Fraction
import hashlib
import json
import math
from pathlib import Path
import sys


def audit(data):
    den = int(data['coordinate_denominator'])
    assert den == 10**15
    if 'T_lower_rows' in data:
        T = [row+[0]*(65-len(row)) for row in data['T_lower_rows']]
    else:
        T = data['coordinate_integer_rows_lower_triangular']
    fnum = data['function_value_numerators']
    assert len(T) == 65 and len(fnum) == 64
    assert all(len(row) == 65 for row in T)
    assert all(type(x) is int for row in T for x in row)
    assert all(type(x) is int for x in fnum)
    assert all(T[i][j] == 0 for i in range(65) for j in range(i+1,65))
    def fadd(x,y):
        return x[0]+y[0],x[1]+y[1]
    def fmul(x,y):
        return x[0]*y[0]+2*x[1]*y[1],x[0]*y[1]+x[1]*y[0]
    c=(Fraction(7,6),Fraction(7,6))
    a=(Fraction(-5,7),Fraction(5,7))
    power=(Fraction(1),Fraction(0))
    h=[]
    for j in range(6):
        bridge=fadd((Fraction(1),Fraction(0)),fmul(a,power))
        h=h+[bridge]+h
        power=fmul(power,c)
    assert len(h)==63
    hd=math.lcm(*(x.denominator for pair in h for x in pair))
    assert hd==54432
    ha=[int(x*hd) for x,y in h]
    hb=[int(y*hd) for x,y in h]
    radical_den=10**50
    radical_lo=math.isqrt(2*radical_den**2)
    assert radical_lo**2 < 2*radical_den**2 < (radical_lo+1)**2
    def upper(aa,bb):
        return aa*radical_den+bb*(radical_lo+1 if bb>=0 else radical_lo)
    def lower(aa,bb):
        return aa*radical_den+bb*(radical_lo if bb>=0 else radical_lo+1)
    assert lower(sum(ha),sum(hb))>200*hd*radical_den
    assert upper(sum(ha),sum(hb))<256*hd*radical_den
    assert all(lower(aa,bb)>hd*radical_den for aa,bb in zip(ha,hb))

    # Point coordinates are generated directly from the rational gradients.
    # Both integer arrays have denominator hd*den.
    xa=[[hd*t for t in T[0]]]
    xb=[[0]*65]
    for t in range(63):
        xa.append([xa[-1][k]-ha[t]*T[t+1][k] for k in range(65)])
        xb.append([xb[-1][k]-hb[t]*T[t+1][k] for k in range(65)])
    g=T[1:]+[[0]*65]
    xa.append([0]*65);xb.append([0]*65)
    vals=fnum+[0]
    eta_den=500_000_000_000
    qden=2*hd*den**2
    count=0
    largest=None
    largest_pair=None
    for i in range(65):
        for j in range(65):
            if i==j:
                continue
            qa=2*hd*den*(vals[i]-vals[j])
            qa+=2*sum(g[i][k]*(xa[j][k]-xa[i][k]) for k in range(65))
            qb=2*sum(g[i][k]*(xb[j][k]-xb[i][k]) for k in range(65))
            qa+=hd*sum((g[i][k]-g[j][k])**2 for k in range(65))
            up=upper(qa,qb)
            assert eta_den*up<qden*radical_den,('pair',i,j)
            if largest is None or up>largest:
                largest,largest_pair=up,(i,j)
            count+=1
    assert count==4160
    for i in range(64):
        dnum=2*den*vals[i]-sum(t*t for t in g[i])
        assert eta_den*dnum>-2*den**2
    normnum=sum(t*t for t in T[0])
    assert 1000*normnum<1001*den**2
    assert 2000*vals[63]>21*den
    corr=sum(g[35][k]*g[36][k] for k in range(65))
    assert 1000*corr < -14*den**2

    # Independent direct scalar-coordinate audit of the full jump Gram.
    jumps=[[g[i][k]-g[i-1][k] for k in range(65)] for i in range(1,64)]
    tr=sum(sum(x*x for x in row) for row in jumps)
    off=2*sum(abs(sum(x*y for x,y in zip(jumps[i],jumps[j])))
              for i in range(63) for j in range(i+1,63))
    assert 100*(off-5*tr)>9*den**2
    assert eta_den>2**37
    assert Fraction(21,1000)/(Fraction(1001,1000)+Fraction(1,64))==Fraction(56,2711)
    assert Fraction(56,2711)>Fraction(1,50)>Fraction(4,201)
    assert Fraction(9,100)>Fraction(5,2**24)
    return {'passed':True,'directed_pairs':count,'largest_upper_pair':largest_pair,
            'largest_Q_upper_numerator':str(largest),'largest_Q_upper_denominator':str(qden*radical_den),
            'jump_margin_numerator':str(off-5*tr),'jump_margin_denominator':str(den**2)}


if __name__=='__main__':
    path=Path(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv)>1 else Path('/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json')
    blob=path.read_bytes()
    if path.name=='max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json':
        assert hashlib.sha256(blob).hexdigest()=='7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113'
    print(json.dumps(audit(json.loads(blob))))

```

To justify the matrix-decomposition conclusion in the statement, diagonalize each positive semidefinite summand supported on at most six indices. This expresses it as a sum of rank-one aa^T with each a supported on at most six indices. For each such a, Cauchy--Schwarz gives 2sum_(i<j)|a_i*a_j|<=(6-1)sum_i a_i^2. Summing these bounds and applying the triangle inequality yields 2sum_(i<j)|J59_ij|<=5trace(J59), contradicting the strict bound proved for the specified object. The analogous scalar-coordinate decomposition proves the stated representation obstruction. This does not claim that every other legal adversary is outside those classes.

## intuition
The full finite primal has a robust payoff but tiny interpolation residuals. Rational coordinates make its Gram exactly positive semidefinite; one explicit small auxiliary quadratic dominates every residual without materially enlarging the true initial distance. Strict adjusted values force a unique minimizer. A direct invariant of the actual increment Gram certifies that the turning geometry is outside the at-most-six-jumps coordinate class.

## 0. Mathematical verdict

**Not solved.**

The fixed goal is to prove `lim_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1`, uniformly over all sufficiently large horizons and every predetermined nonnegative word, or rigorously disprove it by a full-class asymptotic separation. Here `W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)` over every finite dimension, convex L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every original selected minimizer and distinct start; `W=2R`. The exact benchmark is `U_n^ZJ=1/B_n`, with all integer splits in the supplied Zhang--Jiang recurrence. Neither the target nor its quantifiers change.

Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` closes the exact benchmark comparison and conditional separation threshold; `1e0bf3f41457d8e4` closes finite-word projection-simplex transfer and the actual H3 law; `b98bb807d514e80d` closes the single-cut portfolio at its stated scope. None proves the target. A candidate upper architecture is a retained PD join whose full-class history input remains unproved. Its quantitative scalar margin is derived, but does not establish that input.

The latest draft supplies an actual method failure: a weak-history composition retaining at least the whole weak left residual is impossible. It does not supply proposition failure: on that same trajectory the requested parent history remains strictly positive. A separate fixed-depth coercivity proof restricts a possible violation to internal depths growing with the enclosing depth. This is not a uniform all-depth result. PARTIAL and SUBSTANTIAL do not mean almost solved; the unmatched interface is still a theorem about the full original class.

Current best proof skeleton:

1. Keep `C_0=B_0=1`, `C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l)` and `B_n=max G(C_k,B_l)` over ALL k+l=n-1, with `Omega=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2` and `G=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2`.
2. The supplied Zhang--Jiang schedule gives `W_n^*<=1/B_n`.
3. Accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` provides the matching lower bound whenever `sum h<=(B_n-1)/2`. The other words remain uncovered.
4. Independently, keep the literal perturbed hierarchy defined below, with no child parameter reset.
5. Prove its displayed weak history input for all required inherited children; this is the central missing lemma.
6. The retained PD identity and its actual leaf then produce a full-class dominance certificate of total `1+(32/17)S_m`.
7. This yields `W(H_m)<=1/[1+(32/17)S_m]<=17/[16(1+2S_m)]` at every original selected radius.
8. Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` then gives a strict `799/800` subsequence comparison and resolves the original question negatively. Steps 5--7 are not established.

Central missing lemma: for every sufficiently large integer m, set `epsilon=1/(4m)`, `beta=1-epsilon`, `c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon)`, `a=beta/c`, `H_0=()`, `H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j)`, `S_j=sum H_j`, `C_j=1+S_j`. For EVERY 1<=j<m and every original convex 1-smooth actual H_j trajectory, prove

    E_j = 2 sum h_i(f_i-f_end) - sum h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2
          - [(7/10) C_j^2-C_j] ||g_end||^2 >= 0.

All children inherit the same enclosing m. The conditional joining calculation, fixed-depth strict positivity and Huber calibrations do not prove this assertion. The alternative affirmative route still needs a uniform high-mass lower witness at the exact B_n profile.

## 1. Closed components and obsolete routes

Signed-closed components:

- Exact all-integer benchmark comparison and conditional full-class separation threshold — `931b9653ce0f7201`.
- Fixed-word projection-simplex supremum transfer and the actual same-parameter H3 U law — `1e0bf3f41457d8e4`.
- Exact single-cut Huber portfolio and its precise insufficiency — `b98bb807d514e80d`.

Every new result in the following table is a manual draft, not a fact-graph acceptance. Full statements and derivations are in R13_FINAL_HANDOFF_20261009.md and the two complete source proofs it identifies.

| Route | Status | Concrete reason |
|---|---|---|
| Unit or larger weak-left retention plus nonnegative residuals/rows/PSD | FALSE AS STATED | Actual asymmetric Huber gives E_P<E_L at every internal j>=3; even an unrestricted PSD remainder cannot repair that inequality |
| Positive interval-mode cone for fixed increasing physical weights | FALSE AS STATED | Actual column and full diagonal conditions force `1+r^3<=1+r/6`, but `6r^2>1`, r=sqrt(2)-1 |

General nonseparable matrices, growing-depth nonhomogeneous trajectories, and the original proposition are not excluded by these statements.

## 2. Interface contract table

### Weak history to the original separating upper bound

**Input required.** The exact central missing lemma for every inherited child, together with the genuine full-class leaf dominance input. **Output claimed.** Fixed nonnegative dominance weights of total `1+(32/17)S_m`, hence the original-radius upper bound and separation. **Available facts.** `931b9653ce0f7201` supplies only the final benchmark implication. **Missing verification on the actual model.** The all-depth history inequality itself; after it is proved, a finished-object audit of the entire implication. **Failure mode if ignored.** Primitive positivity at perturbed higher levels would be assumed rather than proved. **Status.** SUBSTANTIAL.

### Exact Silver coercivity to perturbed growing depth

**Input required.** At epsilon zero the leaf and original-row join imply standard history positivity; equality with zero terminal gradient forces all gradients zero. **Output claimed.** For each FIXED j, some eta_j>0 and M_j<infinity give `E_j>=(eta_j/2) sum ||g_i||^2` for every m>=M_j and every finite-dimensional actual history. **Available facts.** No fact graph entry certifies this new draft. **Missing verification on the actual model.** Quantitative constants controlling j growing with m, or another uniform argument. **Failure mode if ignored.** Exchanging `for each j, eventually m` with `eventually m, for all j<m` falsely closes the goal. **Status.** PARTIAL.

### A non-unit weak-history join to all-depth preservation

**Input required.** Actual interpolation rows connecting original left and right queries, and full child inequalities with exactly their warranted coefficients. **Output claimed.** A preservation rule allowing a weak-left coefficient below one while restoring the prescribed physical function-value coefficients and proving the complete remainder nonnegative. **Available facts.** None supplies this construction; R13's actual obstruction only excludes coefficient at least one. **Missing verification on the actual model.** Actual coefficients, every row sign, the full gradient-space remainder, the leaf and a uniform induction. **Failure mode if ignored.** A missing function-value coefficient or a signed temporal-moment term can invalidate the claimed identity. **Status.** UNKNOWN.

### Full increasing-profile matrix to a dominance certificate

**Input required.** S=S_m, T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, `u_i=(1+T_i/(2S))h_i` before the terminal query, `u_n=1+(32/17)S-sum u_i`; an actual zero-row-sum C with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and columns b_i=u_i, b_n=-sum_(i<n)u_i. With Bmat the actual trajectory matrix and L the graph Laplacian of weights `u_j(u_i-h_i)`, require FULL PSD of `R=L+diag(u_i(1-u_i)+b_i-L_ii)-(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T)`. **Output claimed.** Nonnegative original dominance residual and the same separating upper bound. **Available facts.** `931b9653ce0f7201` only after the upper premise. **Missing verification on the actual model.** Actual C outside the excluded families and the full uniform PSD proof. **Failure mode if ignored.** A positive Laplacian is only one summand; the whole positive interval cone already fails the full diagonal. **Status.** PARTIAL.

### Legal high-mass witness to an affirmative exact-profile bound

**Input required.** One static admissible function and complete actual trajectory for every high-mass nonnegative word, including zero and arbitrarily large finite steps, at the original selected radius. **Output claimed.** `W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n` uniformly in h. **Available facts.** `b98bb807d514e80d` closes only the complementary low-mass region. **Missing verification on the actual model.** A globally coupled constant-preserving construction and an all-word selection rule. **Failure mode if ignored.** Changing a wall can change a previously recorded gradient; using another minimizer can silently replace the original radius. **Status.** PARTIAL.

## 3. Dangerous heuristic lines and strategies not to pursue

“The weak history holds at every fixed depth, so it holds at j=m.” **DANGEROUS.** The compactness constants depend on j without a bound. Growing depth is the missing content, not an endpoint convention.

“Retain the weak left residual in full and repair the bridge by more positive rows.” **FALSE AS STATED.** The actual Huber construction has E_P<E_L. This excludes even arbitrary positive original rows and a full PSD remainder under that retention coefficient.

“The fixed increasing weights have a positive Laplacian, so diagonal compensation can be tuned inside the interval cone.” **FALSE AS STATED.** The new obstruction uses the FULL diagonal after the Laplacian cancellation. General matrices remain outside its scope.

“A positive Huber phase test proves the profile.” **DANGEROUS.** The complete covered Huber family has a positive margin, but other starts, functions and dimensions are not included.

“A cone distance bound proves the weak history input.” **DANGEROUS.** The cone argument rules out a specific absorbed negative-history mechanism; it does not establish the terminal objective and gradient terms for arbitrary functions.

- Do not repeat the unit-left weak-history join with different bridge/right coefficients: the actual obstruction covers all such nonnegative representations.
- Do not retune a positive interval-mode combination for the fixed increasing profile: the entire named cone fails.
- Do not use a bounded internal-depth scan or continuity limit as a uniform induction.
- Do not call a free negative matrix direction an actual smooth-convex trajectory.
- Do not restart any child with new enclosing parameters or replace its original minimizer/radius.
- Do not promote the conditional PD identity, exact benchmark implication, or a scoped draft exclusion to the original answer.

## 4. Missing bridge lemmas

### Bridge 1 — close the weak history at growing inherited depth

**Target statement.** The exact central missing lemma in Section 0, for all sufficiently large m and every 1<=j<m. **Prerequisites.** Original nonnegative interpolation rows, true child coefficients and same enclosing parameters. **Existing facts to use.** `931b9653ce0f7201` supplies the consequence only; no accepted fact supplies the new input. **Missing checks.** A non-unit left-retention construction with actual cross-subtree rows, or a quantitative whole-tree bound on the retained signed defects, with explicit constants sufficient for the three-tenths terminal loss. **Closure criterion.** A complete dimension-uniform original-space inequality covering all required j, followed by the retained PD induction. This is the primary upper-route bridge; it is independent of the affirmative lower construction.

### Bridge 2 — instantiate a full global dominance certificate

**Target statement.** For all sufficiently large m, construct the exact increasing-profile C and prove the complete R from Section 2 PSD, or supply another fully specified original-radius dominance certificate of the same sufficient total. **Prerequisites.** The actual physical trajectory matrix, all column/row constraints and nonnegative interpolation coefficients. **Existing facts to use.** `931b9653ce0f7201` for the final comparison. **Missing checks.** Genuine coefficients outside the excluded positive interval and single-destination families; full positivity rather than separate convenient signs. **Closure criterion.** The full original-class upper bound, not its matrix reformulation. This is an independent sufficient upper route.

### Bridge 3 — attain the exact lower profile on every high-mass word

**Target statement.** For every eta>0, all sufficiently large n and every nonnegative word with `sum h>(B_n-1)/2`, construct an original admissible instance with payoff at least `(1-eta)/B_n`, with threshold independent of h. **Prerequisites.** One final fixed function, every physical query and the original radius; correlations must survive all face updates. **Existing facts to use.** `b98bb807d514e80d` for the complementary words and `1252b8823bb5a290` for full interpolation scope. **Missing checks.** A globally coupled witness cost and a constant-preserving uniform selection rule. **Closure criterion.** Every quantifier of the original lower bound follows. This is independent of the two upper routes and remains theorem-strength.

Use finite positive probabilities, mean-zero Euclidean variables, K={g:Eg0,g>=-1}, F(X)=max E(Xg)-Eg²/2. Projectiong=(X-t)+-1 withE(X-t)+1. # Unallocated R11 lead: a non-diagonal Brownian-bridge remainder for dominance

**UNVERIFIED manual algebra. No full certificate, model allocation, mathematical program or formal submission. Original target OPEN.** This note preserves a genuinely non-diagonal remainder and changes the function-value weights; the positive-diagonal interval dictionary excluded in R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md does not automatically cover it. Its actual coefficient/positivity problem is UNSOLVED.

## 1. Exact original-radius dominance residual

Fix an arbitrary physical word h_0,...,h_(n-1)>=0 and its actual normalized smooth-convex trajectory. All sampled gradients include the actual terminalg_n. Use

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||²>=0,
    Q_star,i=2fstar-2f_i+2<g_i,x_i-xstar>-||g_i||²>=0.

Choose fixed nonnegative weightsu_0,...,u_n with totalB=sum u_i>0. This local B is a certificate weight, distinct from the original benchmark B_n. Define the doubled dominance residual

    D_u=||x0-xstar||²-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2B(f_n-fstar)-||x0-xstar-sum_i u_i g_i||².

It is exactly

    D_u=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_n)
         +sum_i u_i(1-u_i)||g_i||²
         +2sum_(j<i)u_i(h_j-u_j)<g_i,g_j>.             (1)

Every j<i satisfiesj<n, so h_j in(1) is always an actual physical step. If D_u>=0 for ALL actual smooth-convex trajectories, then nonnegativity ofQ_star,i and of the final square gives the full-class bound W_n(h)<=1/B, at the original selected minimizer/radius. No such nonnegativity is assumed.

## 2. A covariance that represents the cross-gradient terms exactly

Choose parameters

    1>rho_0>rho_1>...>rho_n>0,
    u_i=rho_i h_i for i<n.

The strict ordering is convenient for a nonsingular covariance; weak ordering is possible with degeneracies treated explicitly. Chooseu_n>0 separately. Introduce scalar weightsell_i=h_i fori<n andell_n=u_n/rho_n. The LASTell_n is solely an algebraic weight, NOT a physical step or another update.

Define the matrix

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j [min(rho_i,rho_j)-rho_i rho_j].   (2)

It is positive semidefinite, since

    sum_(i,j)K_ij<g_i,g_j>
      =integral_0^1 ||sum_i ell_i g_i
                   (1_(t<=rho_i)-rho_i)||² dt >=0.   (3)

This is the covariance of a centered Bernoulli-threshold process, often called the Brownian-bridge covariance. Formula(3) proves the PSD statement directly; no external stochastic theorem is needed.

Fori>j, rho_i<rho_j implies

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j rho_i(1-rho_j)=u_i(h_j-u_j).

Its diagonal isK_ii=u_i(ell_i-u_i). Therefore the exact same functional(1) can be written

    D_u=2sum_(i<n)u_i(f_i-f_n)
        +sum_i u_i(1-ell_i)||g_i||²
        +sum_(i,j)K_ij<g_i,g_j>.                     (4)

This does not replace the physical gradients by random ones. The covariance is a deterministic positive matrix acting on the ORIGINAL queried gradient vectors.

## 3. Combine with original interpolation rows without forcing diagonal slack

Let Bmat be the actual trajectory matrix with diagonal1 and Bmat_ij=h_j forj<i. Suppose C is a zero-row-sum matrix with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums

    b_i=u_i fori<n, b_n=-sum_(i<n)u_i=u_n-B.

Then, usinglambda_ij=C_ji fori!=j,

    sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij
       =2sum_i b_i f_i+g^T(H-diag(b))g,
    H=C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T.

Consequently the EXACT remaining matrix is

    D_u=sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij+g^T R g,
    R=K+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)-H.                    (5)

For a word satisfying the companion interval modes' h_i>=1 assumptions, those modes make H diagonal, soR=K+diag(mu), but the diagonalmu is NOT required to be nonnegative. The complete R must be proved PSD. This distinction matters: imposingmu>=0 throws away the non-diagonal mechanism and returns to a much smaller method.

Formula(3) gives an exact alternative route to that matrix proof: partition[0,1] at the rho_i, express the integral as a finite positive sum of squared original gradient partial sums, and compare it against the signed diagonalmu. A valid proof would be a weighted discrete inequality on ALL vectors; no independence or freedom of actual histories is used to assert a counterexample if that sufficient test fails.

## 4. Direct relation to the desired complete-H_m upper target

For S=sum H_m, set the desired total weight

    B_target=1+(32/17)S.

For any selected decreasingrho_i in(0,1), the choice

    u_n=B_target-sum_(i<n)rho_i h_i

is positive, because B_target>S. A successful explicitC and fullPSDproof for(5), uniformly over every sufficiently large inheritedm, would give

    W(H_m)<=1/B_target<=17/[16(1+2S)].

The accepted benchmark ratio would then close the original negative direction. No suchrho profile, C array or PSD estimate is given in this note. The mass identity by itself is only bookkeeping.

The positive A/D conic-hull obstruction in the companion note fixed physical function weightsb_i=h_i and imposed nonnegative physical diagonal slack. Hereb_i=rho_i h_i andthe FULLnon-diagonal matrixR is kept. That old obstruction cannot simply be reused without a new derivation. Conversely, a named covariance does not prove the new family feasible.

The R3 weighted-prefix report tested two PARTICULAR outgoing minimizer-row laws for a different last-prefix objective. Its full matrix obstruction must be respected at those exact profiles; it is not a blanket exclusion of allu in(1). The old R2 history family, R3 source laws, and the present original-terminal dominance functional must be matched explicitly before claiming novelty or impossibility.

A future allocation should first choose an actualrho law and matchingC, then prove the full original-space inequality or reject that precise law using an exact original-space obstruction. It should not stop at the equivalent feasibility statement(5), and must not invoke an unallocated solver. No new native, mathematical-program or formal allowance follows from this note.

## Lower attempt: a genuine coupled triangle, with a precise full-hierarchy ceiling

The conic first approach is an explicit instantiation of an OLDhomogeneity obstruction, notnewnovelty. ForanyclosedconvexconeC andh>=1, x=P_Cx+n,n∈Cpolar gives||P_C(x-hP_Cx)||<=(h-1)||P_Cx||. Otherwise the ENTIRElastH_(m-1) startswithradialfactorq=beta^(2^(m-1))/c<2^(-m²), forcingb/r0<=9q A_m andpayoff<1/D_m. Theseareanalyticuniformestimates, notfinitearrays.

Scope: this does NOT cover third-faceactivation, above-apex/interior/vertex queries, dwellingononeedge,severalhigher-dimensionalfaces, or arbitrarycoupledlaws. It is NOT a fullW(H_m) upper. Repeatedfacesalone have now beenrealizedbutremaininsufficientinthispolicy.

## Source-method check and a genuine positive-conic-hull exclusion

Primary method sources checked: [Zhao2609.11784v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11784v1), Sections3.1--3.3, and [Chikhi2609.13765v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v2), with the constant-step/proximal scope kept separate fromtheoriginalproblem. The NOVELTYCHECKthenidentifiedeachmodeasanoldA/Datom. Morestrongly, theoldcentralcutextends to ANYfinitepositivesum ofsuchatoms. Withq_k=1+2sum_nu A_(k-1)^nu D_k^nu andnonnegativephysicaldiagonalslackmu_k=q_k-(h_k-1)²q_(k+1), combinedcutmatchingb_i=h_i implies

At a palindrome'scentralexcessr andchildmasss, everyatommonotonicitygivesq_(k+1)>=(3(s+1)+r)/(s+1-r),while r²q_(k+1)<=q_k<=(s+1)². Hence r/(s+1)<=sqrt2-1. ActualinheritedH_m violatesit.
