<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001572 -->
# Static activation R5: curvature paid at birth and a complete Huber relay

Status: **UNVERIFIED direct analytical draft. Original target OPEN.**

Project: `gd-exact-profile-20260926`. Allocation: one fresh Astra/ultra invocation, at most two substantive lines, mathematical programs 0, numerical arrays/scans/solvers 0, formal submissions 0. No new model calls, agents, installs, service actions or truth-store mutations were made. The parent owns the allocation ledger. All new mathematics below requires independent verification before use as a fact.

The current `OPERATOR.md`, the COMPLETE `PROBLEM.md`, the supplied task, the scoped spiral activation obstruction, Sections 1--3 and the conclusion of the specified strong-reactivation report, and the relevant shared-history warning in R4 were read. No closed numerical data were opened.

## Target and source use

Put (B_n=2S_\bullet(n)+1), with the exact integer-split sequence in `PROBLEM.md`. The requested lower bound is

\[
 \forall\varepsilon>0\ \exists N_\varepsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\varepsilon\ \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:
 \quad \mathcal W_n(h)\ge (1-\varepsilon)/B_n.
 \tag{T}
\]

Here (\mathcal W=2\mathcal R). All functions below have advertised smoothness 1. Multiplication of the function by (L>0), with physical steps (h_t/L), preserves their iterates and normalized payoff. A restricted-family upper bound below is never an upper bound for (\mathcal W_n(h)).

[Zhang--Jiang v2, Sections 3.1 and 3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2) was read through both concatenation proofs. Its primitive certificate retains distance, value and terminal-gradient energy together; the dominant concatenation finishes a square using the same minimizer and weighted future gradients. This motivates accounting for the full curvature matrix and the complete original trajectory here. Those upper certificates do not supply a converse construction for arbitrary words. No decimal asymptotic constant is used.

## Line 1. A fresh bank of positive ridges cannot borrow saturated old curvature

### 1.1 The actual global condition, including unvisited regions

For fixed finite vectors (v_j\in\mathbb R^d), thresholds (t_j\ge0), and a symmetric (A\succeq0), consider the SINGLE static function

\[
 F(x)=\tfrac12x^TAx+H(x),\qquad
 H(x)=\sum_{j=1}^m \tfrac12(\langle v_j,x\rangle-t_j)_+^2,
 \qquad M=\sum_jv_jv_j^T.
 \tag{1}
\]

Every coefficient is frozen before the run. Positive scalar ridge weights can be absorbed into (v_j,t_j). The sufficient global condition is

\[
 A+M\preceq I. \tag{2}
\]

Indeed, the gradient difference between any two points equals

\[
 \nabla F(x)-\nabla F(y)=(A+\sum_j\theta_jv_jv_j^T)(x-y),
 \qquad 0\le\theta_j\le1,
\]

using scalar divided differences of (z_+). The matrix is positive semidefinite and at most (I); this proves global convexity, differentiability and 1-Lipschitz gradient. Moreover (F\ge0=F(0)), and its complete minimum set is

\[
 \arg\min F=\ker A\cap\{x:\langle v_j,x\rangle\le t_j\ \text{for every }j\}.
 \tag{3}
\]

If the ridge normals are linearly independent, (2) is also NECESSARY for this representation to be 1-smooth. The map (x\mapsto(\langle v_j,x\rangle)_j) is onto, so some open region has every ridge strictly active. Its Hessian is exactly (A+M).

The same necessity holds for independent one-sided Huber ridges, with any strictly positive cap for each ridge: choose all residuals strictly within their quadratic intervals. Therefore disjoint curvature intervals ALONG THE RUN do not remove the full global curvature condition when the fresh normals are independent. This observation concerns this additive representation, not all smooth convex functions.

### 1.2 The whole old hull, not only the last query

Suppose the proposed new bank has (H(x_i)=0) at all earlier actual queries and at the selected original minimizer 0. Then it vanishes on

\[
 K=\operatorname{conv}\{0,x_0,\ldots,x_k\}. \tag{4}
\]

This follows directly from convexity and nonnegativity. In particular (\nabla H=0) on (K), since every point of (K) is a global minimizer of (H). An actual first activation can occur only at a point outside (K). A fresh orthogonal coordinate does not on its own establish such an escape.

For a unit normal (u), a squared hinge zero on (K) needs a threshold at least (\sup_{z\in K}\langle u,z\rangle). Its positive residual at (y) is therefore at most (\operatorname{dist}(y,K)). This is sharp over unit normals for (y\notin K): take the outward normal from the Euclidean projection of (y) onto (K). The projection inequality supplies the supporting threshold, and Cauchy--Schwarz supplies the reverse bound.

The aggregate bank has the stronger matrix bound

\[
 2H(y)\le\inf_{z\in K}(y-z)^TM(y-z).
 \tag{5}
\]

For each (z\in K), each positive residual is at most (\langle v_j,y-z\rangle_+); sum its square and then discard the positive-part restriction. This proves (5), with no assumptions on the dimension or number of hinges.

If (x=x_k\in K), (g=Ax) is the old gradient at the last query, and the next ACTUAL point is (y=x-hg), then

\[
 2H(y)\le h^2g^TMg\le h^2g^T(I-A)g,
 \qquad 2H(y)\le y^TMy.
 \tag{6}
\]

The second inequality uses (0\in K). Also

\[
 \|\nabla H(y)\|^2\le h^2g^T(I-A)g, \tag{7}
\]

because (\nabla H=V^Tr), (r_j=(\langle v_j,y\rangle-t_j)_+), (\|V\|^2=\|M\|\le1), and the same residual estimate bounds (\|r\|^2). These statements remain valid for one-sided Huber profiles, whose value is at most the squared hinge and whose derivative is between 0 and the positive residual.

### 1.3 A sharp transverse budget, including its actual attained example

Let (e) be a unit vector, (0<\delta<1), and let

\[
 A=(1-\delta)ee^T,\qquad D=I-A.
\]

Consider a possible first crossing to (y=-re), (r>0), from an earlier collinear history on which the new bank was zero. For every static bank of the preceding type satisfying (2),

\[
 \|P_{e^\perp}\nabla H(-re)\|\le \frac{r\sqrt\delta}{2}.
 \tag{8}
\]

This includes arbitrary nonnegative thresholds and arbitrary positive Huber caps. To derive it, for each ridge write its derivative at (-re) as (\theta_j(-r\langle v_j,e\rangle)), where (0\le\theta_j\le1); ridges with nonnegative (\langle v_j,e\rangle) have derivative zero. Set (N=\sum_j\theta_jv_jv_j^T). Then (0\preceq N\preceq M\preceq D), and (\nabla H(-re)=-rNe).

For (T=D^{-1/2}ND^{-1/2}), one has (0\preceq T\preceq I), hence (T^2\preceq T). Writing (a=\langle e,Te\rangle),

\[
 \|P_{e^\perp}Te\|^2\le a-a^2\le\tfrac14.
\]

Since (P_{e^\perp}Ne=\sqrt\delta P_{e^\perp}Te), this proves (8).

The constant is attained by the explicit TWO-dimensional function

\[
 F(x)=\frac{1-\delta}{2}\langle e,x\rangle^2
       +\frac12\left\langle-\sqrt{\delta/2}\,e+\frac{w}{\sqrt2},x\right\rangle_+^2,
 \qquad w\perp e,\quad\|w\|=1. \tag{9}
\]

Its global curvature condition follows from

\[
 v^TD^{-1}v=1,\quad v=-\sqrt{\delta/2}\,e+w/\sqrt2,
\]

which gives (vv^T\preceq D). Starting at (x_0=e), every positive collinear query has an inactive hinge. Any actual step (h>1/(1-\delta)) taken from (e) lands at (y=-re), where (r=h(1-\delta)-1). The new transverse gradient is (r\sqrt\delta,w/2). The minimum set is

\[
 \{x:\langle e,x\rangle=0,\ \langle w,x\rangle\le0\},
\]

with value 0, and 0 is its closest point to (x_0=e). Thus the original radius is exactly 1; there is no source reset or prepaid fresh initial coordinate in this attainment example.

Equation (8) rules out obtaining an order-one transverse gradient at a bounded crossing distance by adding arbitrarily many silent positive ridges to an old direction whose curvature tends to 1. It does NOT bound the eventual objective of the whole function: old energy remains, and an arbitrary future word may amplify the new component. The attempt to obtain a lossless recursive child simply by increasing the fresh bank's dimension therefore stops at this quantified cost. It does not exclude a globally chosen, substantially different prefix.

## Line 2. A static arbitrary-depth relay that pays every return guard

The second line avoids a near-unit source curvature. It gives a literal high-dimensional static function and complete chronology for a stated word, then tests its paid constant. It is not a freely selectable state recurrence.

### 2.1 Word, normals, and global function

Fix an integer (K\ge1), and partition the original chronological word as

\[
 h=(a^{(0)},b_0,a^{(1)},b_1,\ldots,b_{K-1},a^{(K)}),
 \qquad s_j=\sum a^{(j)}\ge0.
 \tag{10}
\]

Every (a^{(j)}) is the complete original intervening subword, including zero entries. No entries are removed. Choose (d,c>0) with

\[
 2c\cos\frac\pi{K+2}<d,\qquad
 d+2c\cos\frac\pi{K+2}\le1. \tag{11}
\]

There are explicit independent vectors (v_0,\ldots,v_K\in\mathbb R^{K+1}) with

\[
 \langle v_i,v_j\rangle=
 \begin{cases}d&i=j,\\-c&|i-j|=1,\\0&|i-j|>1.\end{cases} \tag{12}
\]

For completeness put (\vartheta=\pi/(K+2)), and for (1\le\ell\le K+1) define coordinates

\[
 (v_j)_\ell=\sqrt{d-2c\cos(\ell\vartheta)}\,
 \sqrt{\frac2{K+2}}\sin((j+1)\ell\vartheta).
 \tag{13}
\]

The finite sine basis diagonalizes the tridiagonal matrix in (12): the identity (\sin((j+2)t)+\sin(jt)=2\cos t\sin((j+1)t)), with the two zero boundary values, gives its eigenvalues (d-2c\cos(\ell\vartheta)). Orthogonality of distinct eigenvectors and the identity (\sum_{j=1}^{K+1}\sin^2(j\ell\vartheta)=(K+2)/2) give the normalization in (13). Thus every eigenvalue lies in ((0,1]), and

\[
 \sum_{j=0}^K v_jv_j^T\preceq I. \tag{14}
\]

These formulas define finite vectors analytically; no array or recurrence was executed.

For (\mu>0), use the explicit one-sided Huber function

\[
 \phi_\mu(z)=
 \begin{cases}
 0&z\le0,\\
 z^2/2&0\le z\le\mu,\\
 \mu z-\mu^2/2&z\ge\mu.
 \end{cases}
 \quad q_\mu(z)=\phi_\mu'(z)=\min\{\mu,\max\{z,0\}\}.
 \tag{15}
\]

Assume (b_j>1/d). Choose an arbitrary scale (\mu_0>0). Define

\[
 A_1=c(b_0-1/d)\mu_0,
 \qquad
 \mu_j=\frac{A_j}{1+ds_j}\quad(1\le j<K),
 \qquad A_{j+1}=cb_j\mu_j\quad(1\le j<K),
 \qquad\mu_K=\frac{A_K}{1+2ds_K}. \tag{16}
\]

All these quantities are positive, including when a block is empty. Set

\[
 t_0=t_1=0,\qquad t_j=c\mu_{j-1}s_{j-1}\quad(2\le j\le K),
 \quad x_0=\frac{\mu_0(1+ds_0)}d v_0,
 \quad F(x)=\sum_{j=0}^K\phi_{\mu_j}(\langle v_j,x\rangle-t_j).
 \tag{17}
\]

This is one function fixed from the entire word before the run. Equations (14)--(15) prove global convexity and 1-smoothness by the divided-difference argument used in Line 1. Its complete minimum set and minimum value are

\[
 \arg\min F=\{x:\langle v_j,x\rangle\le t_j\ \forall j\},
 \qquad F_\star=0. \tag{18}
\]

All thresholds are nonnegative, so 0 is a minimizer. The halfspace (\langle v_0,x\rangle\le0) alone forces every minimizer's distance from the positive multiple (x_0) of (v_0) to be at least (\|x_0\|). Equality is attained at 0. Consequently the TRUE original squared radius is

\[
 R^2=\operatorname{dist}(x_0,\arg\min F)^2
 =\frac{\mu_0^2(1+ds_0)^2}{d}. \tag{19}
\]

### 2.2 Exact guards, including backward reactivation

The required conditions are

\[
 c\mu_{i+1}(s_{i+1}+b_{i+1})\le\mu_i(db_i-1)
       \quad(0\le i\le K-2),
 \qquad
 c\mu_Ks_K\le\mu_{K-1}(db_{K-1}-1).
 \tag{20}
\]

For (K=1), the first set is empty. These are conditions on (10)--(17), not assumptions that future queries may be selected independently. Under (20), the COMPLETE ACTUAL chronology is:

* Throughout (a^{(j)}), and at its following bridge query if (j<K), only ridge (j) has positive derivative. Its derivative is exactly (\mu_j).
* Every earlier ridge has derivative zero and stays off; every later ridge has derivative zero until the corresponding bridge has physically crossed its threshold.
* The last ridge remains in its linear Huber region through the terminal point.

Here is the induction including all region boundaries. Write (r_j=\langle v_j,x\rangle-t_j). While only ridge (j) is active at cap (\mu_j), an actual step (h\ge0) changes the residuals by

\[
 r_j\longmapsto r_j-hd\mu_j,\qquad
 r_{j-1}\longmapsto r_{j-1}+hc\mu_j,\qquad
 r_{j+1}\longmapsto r_{j+1}+hc\mu_j,
 \tag{21}
\]

with every other residual unchanged. These are the inner products of the actual update (x\mapsto x-h\mu_jv_j).

Initially (r_0=\mu_0(1+ds_0)), (r_1=-c\mu_0(1/d+s_0)), and (r_j=-t_j\le0) for (j\ge2). During the first ordinary block, (r_0\ge\mu_0), while (r_1\le-c\mu_0/d<0). At the first bridge the old residual becomes (\mu_0(1-db_0)<0), and the next one becomes (A_1>0). This proves both the first crossing and the subtraction (1/d) in (16). Omitting that subtraction would fail to preserve the original minimum and radius in (17)--(19).

For (1\le j<K), the entry residual is (A_j=\mu_j(1+ds_j)). During its ordinary block it remains at least (\mu_j); the next ridge starts at (-c\mu_js_j) and reaches exactly 0 at the bridge query. At the bridge, ridge (j) becomes negative and ridge (j+1) obtains (cb_j\mu_j=A_{j+1}>0). Any still later ridge is unchanged and remains nonpositive.

The only possible return is to ridge (j-1). It entered this block with residual (\mu_{j-1}(1-db_{j-1})). Its total increase over the block AND bridge is at most (c\mu_j(s_j+b_j)). The appropriate condition in (20) keeps it nonpositive at every actual query, because the partial sums of nonnegative steps are at most the full block sum. After this stage it never changes again, by (12). This proves every old-return guard; no assumption of one-way activation is hidden in the construction.

At the final block, (r_K=A_K=\mu_K(1+2ds_K)), so after any partial sum (s\le s_K), it equals (\mu_K(1+2ds_K-ds)\ge\mu_K). The final condition in (20) keeps ridge (K-1) off. Boundary residual 0 has derivative 0, and residual (\mu_j) has derivative (\mu_j); hence equality guards, empty blocks and zero steps cause no ambiguity.

### 2.3 Exact normalized terminal objective

The terminal residual is (r_K=\mu_K(1+ds_K)). Every other ridge is zero in value as well as gradient. Therefore

\[
 2F(x_n)=\mu_K^2(1+2ds_K)=\frac{A_K^2}{1+2ds_K}.
\]

Combining this with (16) and the ORIGINAL radius (19) gives the complete witness payoff

\[
 \boxed{
 E(h)=\frac{2F(x_n)}{R^2}
 =\frac{d\,c^{2K}(b_0-1/d)^2\prod_{i=1}^{K-1}b_i^2}
 {\left[\prod_{i=0}^{K-1}(1+ds_i)^2\right](1+2ds_K)}.
 } \tag{22}
\]

This is a valid lower witness for the ORIGINAL (\mathcal W_n(h)) whenever the displayed hypotheses and guards hold. No interpolation oracle, origin replacement, new initial component, unproved active-set law or infinite-dimensional limit occurs in this calculation.

It does not establish (T). In particular, positivity and the global norm alone do not imply the guards. For example, for (K\ge2) the first return guard, after cancellation of the positive (b_0-1/d), is exactly

\[
 c^2(s_1+b_1)\le d(1+ds_1). \tag{23}
\]

If this is violated, the old ridge really reactivates before the claimed second bridge has finished. Its nonzero derivative changes the actual update; formula (22) is then inapplicable.

The globally paid coupling also satisfies

\[
 \frac cd<\frac1{2\cos(\pi/(K+2))}. \tag{24}
\]

After rewriting (1+ds_i=d(s_i+1/d)), (22) contains ((c/d)^{2K}). As (K\to\infty), the upper edge of this coefficient behaves as (4^{-K}) up to a factor tending to 1. The statement follows from (cos x=1-x^2/2+O(x^4)), so (-2K\log\cos(\pi/(K+2))\to0). This is a REAL paid factor, not an (o(1)) that can be discarded. It is not by itself a proof that every choice of checkpoints fails: their numerators can grow too. It does show that this static relay cannot be presented as a cost-free realization of the Zhang--Jiang split using the same source budgets.

### 2.4 A uniform failure of the entire central one-transfer subfamily

This tests the preceding exact function family on a relevant unbounded sequence of words, without evaluating a finite trajectory. For (K=1), let the two side sums be equal to (s>0), and write the central bridge as (b). The Gram conditions imply (c<d), (d+c\le1). The last return guard is automatic:

\[
 \frac{c^2s}{1+2ds}\le d,
\]

using (c\le d). Formula (22) is valid for every (b>1/d), and the same-word standard Huber witness is (H_{\rm base}=1/(1+4s+2b)).

Put (z=ds>0), (r=b/s). Since (c\le d) and (db>1),

\[
 \frac{E}{H_{\rm base}}
 \le (4+2r+1/s)\frac{z(rz-1)^2}{(1+z)^2(1+2z)}
 < r^2\left(2+r+\frac1{2s}\right).
 \tag{25}
\]

The last step uses (0<rz-1<r(1+z)) and (z/(1+2z)<1/2). Thus (25) is UNIFORM over all admissible (d,c), including choices depending on the enclosing horizon. The arbitrary scale (\mu_0) has already canceled. This is not one tuned two-dimensional example: it bounds every central one-transfer member of the stated relay architecture.

For the inherited-(m), parameter-(1/4) word identified in the supplied historical report, define analytically

\[
 \lambda=1+\sqrt2,\quad c_m=\lambda(1+1/(4m)),\quad
 a_m=\frac{1-1/(4m)}{c_m},\quad
 H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a_mc_m^j,H_j)
 \quad(0\le j<m).
 \tag{26}
\]

All children use the SAME enclosing (m). The two sides of the central bridge have sum

\[
 s_m=v_mc_m^{m-1}+(1-v_m)2^{m-1}-1,
 \quad v_m=\frac{a_m}{c_m-2},
 \quad b_m=1+a_mc_m^{m-1}. \tag{27}
\]

Equation (27) follows by summing the explicitly defined binary concatenation, or by substituting into its sum recurrence. Since (v_m\to1), (c_m\to\lambda>2), and (s_m\to\infty),

\[
 \frac{b_m}{s_m}\longrightarrow \lambda-2=\sqrt2-1=:q.
\]

Equations (25)--(27) yield

\[
 \limsup_{m\to\infty}\sup_{\substack{d,c\text{ admissible}\\b_m>1/d}}
 \frac{E(H_m)}{H_{\rm base}(H_m)}
 \le q^2(2+q)=q<1. \tag{28}
\]

Thus this central relay cannot improve even the same-word Huber baseline on these horizons, regardless of its admissible curvatures and cap scale. If (b_m\le1/d), this architecture does not perform its first activation and is outside the relay hypothesis; it cannot be counted as an unproved alternative history satisfying (22).

The historical `strong_reactivation_and_failed_screens.md`, Section 1, attributes \(H_{\rm base}(H_m)/U_{2^m-1}^{\rm ZJ}<237/250\) for \(m\ge1536\) to accepted fact `5fd396eba75651e7`. The parent subsequently confirmed this exact scope by a fresh `fact_search`: the fact treats the same enclosing-m word, the Huber comparison, and two specified spiral-family optima; it does NOT certify the complete scalar-quadratic pool. Equations (25)--(28) were derived here and do not require that fact. Importing its Huber comparison places this relay at most \((237/250)(\sqrt2-1)+o(1)\) of the target on that word. No assertion that this word defeats the complete quadratic baseline pool is made. No raw data were reopened.

The bound in (28) covers the central one-transfer subfamily. It does not cover every partition in (10), other sign patterns of the Gram matrix, all additive ridge functions, or the unrestricted smooth convex class.

## Closure audit

The two lines produce no complete candidate for either answer to the original problem.

* Line 1 settles the birth budget for this silent positive-ridge architecture and supplies an exact attained transverse constant, with the original minimum and radius. It leaves the future terminal amplification unresolved.
* Line 2 constructs one static finite-dimensional function for arbitrary depth and proves all physical activation, return, minimum, radius and terminal-value assertions under explicit guards. Its entire central one-transfer family fails on an unbounded sequence of the relevant Huber-gap words. Its many-transfer version still pays the spectral coupling factor and needs an actual all-word selection and terminal comparison; those are not proved and are not called technical lemmas.
* Neither line constructs a witness meeting ((1-o(1))/B_n) for every sufficiently large (n) and every (h\ge0). Neither supplies a full-function upper separation. No recurrence was substituted for the original minimax theorem.

The present allocation ends after these two lines. The original Goal stays OPEN, and no verifier call is warranted for an alleged full solution. A subsequent grant should not merely retune the one-transfer example or drop condition (20). A new mechanism would need to pay curvature and backward returns while retaining the whole joint distance/value/gradient budget, or provide a different complete candidate.

# R12 fixed-profile certificate handoff

**UNVERIFIED. Grant 1ad8f4912d077d53 complete; stop this invocation. Original mathematical target OPEN.**

## Result

No requested full-PSD certificate exists for either fixed parent Profile A or B on every sufficiently large inherited m. An exact one-dimensional Huber trajectory of the COMPLETE physical H_m gives D_A<0 and D_B<0 for every sufficiently large ODD enclosing m. For every admissible zero-row-sum matrix C with the exact prescribed columns and nonnegative off-diagonal entries, the SAME actual gradient vector consequently satisfies

    g^T R_p(C)g = D_p - sum C_ji Q_ij <= D_p <0,
    p in {A,B}.

This excludes all such C, including arbitrary nonseparable C and non-diagonal full remainders. It is stronger than an interval-mode obstruction, but it is a statement about these fixed dominance functionals only. No original-target proof/disproof or full-class W(H_m) bound was obtained.

## Exact construction and audit chain

For one enclosing m>=1536, retain epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, and the SAME parameters in every child H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Define s_j=sum H_j, k_j=a c^j,

    X_1=beta,
    X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),  j>=2.

Use the single fixed function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and |x|-1/2 otherwise, original minimizer x_star=0, and original start x_0=X_m. The proof establishes k_j>=X_j>0 at EVERY inherited depth. Each complete left child and bridge is saturated with its current sign and reflects to the negative critical child start. The ACTUAL final point is

    x_n=g_n=(-1)^m a beta.

For odd m it is -t, t=a beta>0. All physical gradients except the last are +/-1; the last is +beta. Every Q_star,i=0 identically. Thus, if Z_p=sum_(i=0)^n u_i^p g_i,

    D_p=2X_m Z_p-Z_p^2-Bcert*t^2.

The strict signs follow from fully enclosing-m-uniform scalar asymptotics. With r=sqrt(2)-1 and v=r^2/(1+2sqrt(2)), the proof derives

    X_m/S -> r,
    (sum h_i^2)/S^2 -> v,
    (sum h_i x_(i+1))/S^2 -> v.

For Profile A the exact summation-by-parts identity is

    sum_(i<n)rho_i^A h_i g_i
       =X_m-x_n/2-(sum h_i x_(i+1))/(2S).

Consequently

    Z_A/S -> -9r/68-(2-r)v/4 <0.

For Profile B, physical indices satisfy

    0<=rho_i^A-rho_i^B
       =T_i(S-1-T_i)/[2S(S+1+T_i)]<=r^2/2.

This sign is asserted only for i<n; the terminal rhos have the reverse order. The physical negative-gradient mass divided by S tends to (1-r)/2. Transferring the A bound gives

    limsup_(odd m) Z_B/S
       <=-9r/68-(2-r)v/4+r^2(1-r)/4
       <-19r/272<0.

Both D_p are therefore strictly negative on an unbounded sequence of literal complete words with the original radius and actual last iterate. The explicit moment formulas and uniform error bounds are in PROOF_ATTEMPT.md, Sections 3-6.

## Boundary of the conclusion

The witness's normalized objective is t^2/X_m^2=Theta(S^(-2)), whereas the desired upper bound is of order S^(-1). Thus it does NOT violate the proposed objective upper bound. It invalidates the prescribed gradient-weighted square and the associated dominance residuals. The accepted 931b9653ce0f7201 implication to 799/800 separation still lacks its full-class upper premise.

The original target remains lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1 or a rigorous full-class asymptotic separation, with W=2R, exact all-integer U_n^ZJ, every original selected minimizer/radius and actual last iterate. No target, truth store, Goal status, service, worker, configuration or other packet was changed. No fact was submitted, accepted, revoked, or finalized by this call.

The parent relayed the independent primal search's matching trajectory while this call was in progress. This packet independently derives the legal recurrence, the uniform moment asymptotics, both exact signs and the implication for EVERY admissible C. That analytic agreement is not formal verification; all new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED.

The parent subsequently proposed a modified theta=1/8 Profile C only if a second approach remained unused. Both authorized approaches were already exhausted by A and B. This call did not open, analyze or claim a result for that new profile and did not extend its grant.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001574 -->
# R12 fixed-profile matrix certificate: original-trajectory obstruction

**UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Grant 1ad8f4912d077d53. Original target OPEN.**

The requested PSD certificate does not exist for either fixed parent profile on all sufficiently large odd enclosing depths. This draft gives an actual one-dimensional smooth-convex trajectory for the literal complete word H_m on which the specified dominance functional is strictly negative. Consequently, for every matrix C satisfying the requested row, column and off-diagonal conditions, the same actual gradient vector is a strictly negative direction of the FULL remainder R. This is a rejection of these two sufficient dominance profiles, not a counterexample to the proposed full-class objective bound or a resolution of the original minimax problem.

No mathematical program, generated mathematical array, solver, formal submission, extra model, or extra agent was used. There were two analytical approaches: (1) Profile A, including the necessary original-space test of the full matrix construction; (2) transfer of that actual obstruction to the fixed Profile B. No replacement profile is proposed.

## 1. Locked sources and scope

Read completely: OPERATOR.md; the original PROBLEM.md; R11_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md; MATHEMATICAL_ELABORATION_20261009_R11_FINAL.md; R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md; R12_FIXED_DOMINANCE_PROFILES_20261009.md; and the complete R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md, including Section 5. The latter's old positive-cone/physical-diagonal obstruction is not used to infer infeasibility here.

Checked SHA-256 values:

- TASK.md: e05ccb4f67bafd913490af950228e50deefc8f02a8c4949d3d4c040ab1080f57.
- R12_FIXED_DOMINANCE_PROFILES_20261009.md: 277b5d3e3473a358acf98ae9f10c64b063b462195da307c73c1ca0dd0d7439f4.
- R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md: 0fcebe8f462bc029b6a9cf45a1d83abf10854b3f4de386b4bc37d5a01e8d95f7.

The original target is lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1 or a certified full-class asymptotic separation. Here W_n(h) = sup 2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite-dimensional convex L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every original selected minimizer, and every distinct initial point. W = 2R. The exact benchmark is U_n^ZJ = 1/[2S_bullet(n)+1], with the all-integer maximization defining S_bullet in PROBLEM.md. No decimal asymptotic constant replaces that sequence.

For each enclosing integer m >= 1536, fix ONCE

    c0 = 1 + sqrt(2), epsilon = 1/(4m), beta = 1-epsilon,
    c = c0(1+epsilon), a = beta/c,
    H_0 = empty, H_(j+1) = (H_j, 1+a c^j, H_j).

Every child uses these same enclosing-m parameters. Set n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum h, T_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, and

    Bcert = 1 + kappa S,       kappa = 32/17.

This local kappa is the doubled coefficient 32/17; it is not the 16/17 symbol used in some earlier projection notes. Bcert is not the exact benchmark denominator.

For Profile A and Profile B respectively,

    rho_i^A = 1-T_i/(2S),
    rho_i^B = (S+1)/(S+1+T_i).

For each profile p, u_i^p=rho_i^p h_i for i<n and u_n^p=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i^p. The actual boundary rho_0=1 is retained. The algebraic terminal ell_n=u_n/rho_n is never a physical update.

## 2. The exact matrix necessity being tested

Use the actual smooth-convex rows

    Q_ij = 2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
    Q_star,i = -2(f_i-f_star)+2<g_i,x_i-x_star>-||g_i||^2.

All these rows are nonnegative on normalized convex 1-smooth data. Let

    Z_p = sum_(i=0)^n u_i^p g_i.

The prescribed doubled residual is exactly

    D_p = ||x_0-x_star||^2 - sum_i u_i^p Q_star,i
          -2Bcert(f_n-f_star)-||x_0-x_star-Z_p||^2.       (2.1)

For any zero-row-sum C with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums b_i=u_i (i<n), b_n=-sum_(i<n)u_i, the source identity gives

    D_p = sum_(i,j) C_ji Q_ij + g^T R_p(C) g,            (2.2)

where Bmat has its actual physical lower entries h_j and diagonal 1, and

    R_p(C) = K_p+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)
             -(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T).

Therefore one legal trajectory with D_p<0 gives, for EVERY such C,

    g^T R_p(C) g = D_p-sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij <= D_p < 0.  (2.3)

This tests the entire non-diagonal matrix. It does not impose individual diagonal signs, use a free-gradient relaxation, or infer anything from positivity of K alone.

The coefficient constraints on C themselves are nonempty: for example, C_ni=u_i for i<n, C_nn=-sum_(i<n)u_i, and all other entries zero satisfy them. Equation (2.3) will exclude PSD for this explicit C and every other admissible C, including signed interval expansions whose final off-diagonal entries are nonnegative.

## 3. One fixed function and an exact complete-hierarchy trajectory

Choose the symmetric Huber function

    f(x) = x^2/2              if |x|<=1,
           |x|-1/2           if |x|>=1.

The function is convex and differentiable, and its derivative g(x)=clip(x,[-1,1]) is 1-Lipschitz. Its unique global minimizer is the ORIGINAL point x_star=0. At every real query x,

    -2f(x)+2x g(x)-g(x)^2 = 0.                         (3.1)

Thus every Q_star,i vanishes on this fixed function. No function, wall, or minimizer is changed during the trajectory.

Write s_j=sum H_j and k_j=a c^j. Define

    X_1=beta,
    X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),    j>=2.           (3.2)

We next prove that these starts are legal for every level of the literal inherited word and that

    T_(H_j)(X_j)=(-1)^j t,       t=a beta=beta^2/c.     (3.3)

### 3.1 Exact room for the saturated left child

Put

    alpha = beta/[c(c-2)] = a/(c-2).

Then 0<alpha<1, and direct summation of the physical word gives

    s_j+1 = alpha c^j + (1-alpha)2^j.                  (3.4)

For j>=1 define

    d_j = k_(j+1)-(s_j+1)
        = (beta-alpha)c^j-(1-alpha)2^j.

The first term is strictly positive:

    d_1=beta c-2-a
       =epsilon[2/(c0(1+epsilon))-c0 epsilon] > 0.     (3.5)

Indeed epsilon<=1/6144, c0<5/2 and 1+epsilon<2 make the first term in the bracket greater than 2/5 and the second less than 5/(2*6144). Moreover,

    d_(j+1)=c d_j+(c-2)(1-alpha)2^j > d_j >0.         (3.6)

Let e_j=k_j-X_j. Equation (3.2) gives e_1=0 and

    X_(j+1)=s_j+1+e_j,
    e_(j+1)=d_j-e_j.                                  (3.7)

Inductively 0<=e_j<=d_(j-1) for j>=2: if this holds, then 0<=d_j-e_j<=d_j by (3.6). In particular k_j>=X_j>0 for every j.

### 3.2 Physical forward execution

The base word H_1 contains the one actual step 1+a. Since X_1=beta<1,

    T_(H_1)(X_1)=beta-(1+a)beta=-a beta=-t.

For j>=2, the start is X_j=s_(j-1)+1+e_(j-1). During the entire first H_(j-1), the iterate remains at least 1 and its gradient is exactly 1. The left child ends at 1+e_(j-1). The following bridge has length 1+k_(j-1), also uses gradient 1, and lands exactly at

    1+e_(j-1)-(1+k_(j-1))=-X_(j-1).

The map x -> x-h clip(x,[-1,1]) is odd for every physical step h. Hence the last child is the negative of the already constructed complete H_(j-1) trajectory. This proves (3.3) by induction.

For odd enclosing m, take x_0=X_m. The ORIGINAL squared radius is X_m^2>0, and the ACTUAL terminal point and gradient are

    x_n=g_n=-t,        f_n=t^2/2.                      (3.8)

All physical gradients except the last physical query have absolute value 1. The last physical gradient is (+beta) for odd m, and its step length is h_(n-1)=1+a>1. This description follows by descending the rightmost recursive child until the base H_1; it includes all 2^m-1 physical updates.

## 4. Uniform enclosing-m asymptotics of the actual trajectory

All limits below let the enclosing m tend to infinity while retaining its parameters throughout all children. They are not limits with fixed internal depth followed by an unproved interchange.

Set

    r = sqrt(2)-1 = 1/c0,
    v = r^2/(1+2sqrt(2)) >0.

We prove

    X_m/S -> r,
    V_m/S^2 -> v,        V_j=sum_(h in H_j)h^2,
    M_m/S^2 -> v,        M_j=sum_(i in H_j) h_i x_(i+1), (4.1)

where the M_j path starts at X_j and uses the same enclosing parameters.

### 4.1 The starting point and the physical second moment

Let

    d(c)=(c-1)/(c+1),
    P=alpha c d(c), Q=2(1-alpha)/3, R0=beta-P-Q.

Solving (3.2), with (3.4), gives the exact finite-depth expression

    X_j=P c^(j-1)+Q 2^(j-1)+R0(-1)^(j-1).            (4.2)

For example, the coefficient of c^(j-1) follows from
P(1+1/c)=alpha+beta/c, using beta=alpha c(c-2). This also checks the base X_1=beta.

The physical second moment is exactly

    V_j=(2^j-1)
        +2alpha(c^j-2^j)
        +a^2(c^(2j)-2^j)/(c^2-2).                    (4.3)

The identity can also be checked from V_j=2V_(j-1)+(1+k_(j-1))^2 and V_0=0.

Write

    v(c)=(c-2)^2/(c^2-2).

Since c belongs to the fixed compact interval [c0,c0(1+1/6144)] and alpha is uniformly positive, (3.4), (4.2) and (4.3) imply, with constants uniform in the enclosing m and all 1<=j<=m,

    s_j=alpha c^j+O(2^j),
    X_j=d(c)alpha c^j+O(2^j),
    V_j=v(c)alpha^2 c^(2j)+O(c^j+2^j).              (4.4)

At j=m, the relative errors tend to zero because 2/c<=2/c0<1. As c->c0 and beta->1, d(c)->r and v(c)->v. This proves the first two limits in (4.1), and t->r.

### 4.2 Exact first moment of the physical iterates

For j>=2 abbreviate s=s_(j-1), k=k_(j-1). The left child has x_(i+1)=X_j-T_i-h_i, so its contribution to M_j is

    s X_j-(s^2+V_(j-1))/2.

The bridge contributes -(1+k)X_(j-1). The final child is the negative of the previous critical trajectory. Therefore

    M_j+M_(j-1)
      =s X_j-(s^2+V_(j-1))/2-(1+k)X_(j-1).           (4.5)

The exact base is M_1=-(1+a)a beta. Negative small-depth values are allowed; no positivity of the moment has been assumed.

Substitute (4.4) into (4.5) and use the exact equality
k_(j-1)=(c-2)alpha c^(j-1). The leading term is

    F(c)alpha^2 c^(2j-2),
    F(c)=2d(c)-(1+v(c))/2,

and the error is O((2c)^j), uniformly over the same compact parameter set. Define

    w(c)=F(c)/(c^2+1).

Then E_j=M_j-w(c)alpha^2 c^(2j) satisfies

    E_j+E_(j-1)=O((2c)^j).

Summing this scalar recurrence with absolute values gives E_j=O((2c)^j), with a uniform constant: the geometric ratio 2c is uniformly greater than 1, and the base is uniformly bounded. Thus

    M_m/S^2=w(c)+O((2/c)^m) -> w(c0).                (4.6)

Finally w(c0)=v. One exact check is

    v=(8sqrt(2)-11)/7,
    4r-1=v(2c0^2+3),

which is equivalent to 2r-(1+v)/2=v(c0^2+1). This proves the last limit in (4.1). Every error estimate above is uniform in the actual enclosing-m parameters.

## 5. Approach 1: Profile A fails on that original trajectory

Denote its physical weight and weighted physical gradient by

    U_A=sum_(i<n) rho_i^A h_i,
    G_A=sum_(i<n) rho_i^A h_i g_i.

The fixed-profile identity gives

    U_A=3S/4+V_m/(4S),
    u_n^A=1+kappa S-U_A.                             (5.1)

The actual GD recursion h_i g_i=x_i-x_(i+1) and a finite summation by parts give

    G_A=X_m-x_n/2-M_m/(2S).                          (5.2)

To check the endpoint coefficient, rho_i-rho_(i-1)=-h_(i-1)/(2S) and rho_n=1/2. Using M_m including its final term h_(n-1)x_n yields precisely (5.2), without adding a terminal step.

On the odd-m trajectory x_n=g_n=-t, put

    Z_A=G_A-t u_n^A.

Equations (4.1), (5.1) and (5.2) give the exact limiting coefficient

    Z_A/S -> r-v/2-r(kappa-3/4-v/4)
             = -9r/68-(2-r)v/4 <0.                  (5.3)

No decimal sign test is needed: r>0, v>0 and r<1. Consequently Z_A<0 for every sufficiently large odd enclosing m.

By (3.1), (3.8) and (2.1),

    D_A=X_m^2-Bcert t^2-(X_m-Z_A)^2
       =2X_m Z_A-Z_A^2-Bcert t^2 <0.                (5.4)

All terms use the original radius X_m^2 and the actual terminal iterate. The strict failure is at the COMPLETE depth m, not at an internal fixed level. In fact its leading negative term is of order S^2, since X_m/S->r and Z_A/S has a strictly negative limit.

By (2.3), there is no admissible C with R_A(C) PSD for every sufficiently large enclosing m. For each of the infinitely many odd m in (5.4), the actual original gradient vector is a negative direction of R_A(C) for every admissible C.

## 6. Approach 2: the fixed Profile B also fails

Use the SAME physical function, word, original start/minimizer and odd-m trajectory. For physical indices i<n set

    delta_i=rho_i^A-rho_i^B
           =T_i(S-1-T_i)/[2S(S+1+T_i)].              (6.1)

Since the last physical step is 1+a>1, every such T_i satisfies
0<=T_i<=S-(1+a)<S-1. Hence delta_i>=0. This statement is restricted to physical indices: rho_n^B>rho_n^A, and no false terminal inequality is used.

For z=T_i/S in [0,1],

    delta_i <= 1-z/2-1/(1+z)
              =z(1-z)/[2(1+z)] <= r^2/2.           (6.2)

The last maximum occurs at z=r, since the derivative has the sign of 1-2z-z^2. This is an exact uniform bound.

Let Delta=sum_(i<n)delta_i h_i. Then U_B=U_A-Delta and u_n^B=u_n^A+Delta. Therefore

    Z_B-Z_A=-sum_(i<n)delta_i h_i(g_i+t)
            <= (r^2/2) N_minus,                     (6.3)

where N_minus=sum_(g_i<0)h_i(-g_i). For (6.3), terms with g_i>=0 are nonpositive, and a term with g_i<0 is at most delta_i h_i(-g_i). No equality between the profiles' terminal rhos is presumed.

On the odd-m trajectory all physical gradients have magnitude 1 except the last, which is +beta. Thus

    sum_(i<n)h_i |g_i|=S-(1+a)(1-beta),
    sum_(i<n)h_i g_i=X_m+t,

and consequently

    N_minus=[S-(1+a)(1-beta)-X_m-t]/2,
    N_minus/S -> (1-r)/2.                            (6.4)

Combining (5.3), (6.3) and (6.4),

    limsup_(odd m) Z_B/S
       <= -9r/68-(2-r)v/4+r^2(1-r)/4
       < -9r/68+r/16
        = -19r/272 <0.                              (6.5)

Here r(1-r)<1/4 because r is in (0,1) and is not 1/2; the additional negative v-term is simply retained or discarded in the valid direction.

It follows that Z_B<0 for every sufficiently large odd enclosing m. The same original-radius identity gives

    D_B=2X_m Z_B-Z_B^2-Bcert t^2<0.                  (6.6)

Thus (2.3) also rules out every admissible full-PSD C for Profile B at those complete depths.

## 7. What this settles and what remains open

The new statement is a complete analytic obstruction to the TWO SPECIFIED fixed dominance functionals and therefore to their full matrix certificate targets. It covers arbitrary admissible C, not merely the old positive interval dictionary, diagonal H, a separable factorization, or independent diagonal slack. It supplies a real one-dimensional function and every physical query through an exact recursive execution. No freely assigned gradient table is used.

It does not establish W(H_m)>1/Bcert. Indeed this witness has terminal objective t^2/2 while its original squared radius X_m^2 is of order S^2, so its normalized objective is of order S^(-2), much smaller than the proposed S^(-1) upper bound. The failing part is the particular prescribed gradient-weighted square in D_u. The argument therefore supplies no negative answer to the original minimax conjecture and no refutation of the possible full-class upper bound for H_m.

The conditional implication involving accepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 remains conditional: a genuine full-class bound W(H_m)<=1/Bcert would imply the stated 799/800 subsequence separation, but neither profile proves that premise. No signed fact, TARGET, Goal status, original minimizer, benchmark, physical word, shared truth store, or other packet was changed.

The parent relayed the independent primal search's same critical trajectory during this call. The recurrence, legal saturated path, uniform moment limits, both profile signs and their consequence (2.3) were checked analytically here. This is not an independent model-backed formal verification and remains UNVERIFIED under the Danus contract.

## 8. Resource and stop record

- Native research invocations: 1, this fresh grant only; requested model/effort gpt-6-astra/ultra.
- Substantive analytical approaches: 2, Profile A and the permitted Profile B transfer.
- Mathematical programs, generated mathematical arrays, solvers, formal submissions: 0 each.
- Extra agents/models, service/worker changes, old grants reused, shared administration: 0 each.
- File operations: source reads, the two permitted Markdown outputs, and SHA-256/readback checks only.
- No per-call token receipt is exposed here; none is invented.

Stop after writing PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md and reporting their final hashes. The original target remains OPEN; these fixed profiles should not receive further coefficient tuning as if feasible.

# R11 handoff: genuine coupled returns, failed in a precise geometric class

**UNVERIFIED. Original target ACTIVE / OPEN. No complete candidate for formal review.**

Grant `3b9683ee8aaee8c8`, TASK SHA256 `832c4fbeadaeb4fa84199eeaa36fedb18b57f99f8a730ecace7d4848644f4ef5`. One native gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation; two substantive analytical approaches; mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal/verifier/additional agents/additional models all zero. Only PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and this HANDOFF.md were written. Parent owns budget/shared administration. Stop at this handoff.

The original target remains `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1`, W=2R, or a full-class asymptotic disproof. Every sufficiently large integer horizon, every nonnegative predetermined word, exact all-split C_n/B_n, original selected minimum/radius and actual endpoint remain required. No restricted witness ceiling is promoted to an upper bound on W.

## What was actually constructed

1. A fixed compact two-ray cone truncation realizes alternating slanted faces at EVERY physical step of the complete inherited H_m. Its radius is exactly 1 and terminal payoff lies between Pi_m^2/4 and Pi_m^2. The general homogeneous-cone argument already forces the upper bound Pi_m^2, even with the original closest polar minimum. The parent identified this as an instantiation of an existing public route boundary (including finding 21d26350c9141917), so it is not new novelty or a promising mechanism.

2. A SINGLE translated triangle genuinely couples a transverse drift to repeated returns between two nonorthogonal edges. Write `u=(a,s), v=(-a,s), a^2+s^2=1`, vertex `e=(0,-b)`, and `K=conv{e,e+Ru,e+Rv}`, with `R>b/s`. The origin is interior and is the UNIQUE original minimum of the projection-envelope objective. At points `x_t=((-1)^t r_t,-b-w_t)` with `w_t>=0` and `alpha_t=a r_t-s w_t>0`, nonbinding third face gives the exact coupled recurrence

       r_(t+1)=(h_t-1) r_t-h_t s(s r_t+a w_t),
       w_(t+1)=w_t-h_t b+h_t s alpha_t,
       2F(x_t)=b^2+2b w_t+alpha_t^2.

   This is one body, not an orthogonal product or independent Huber bank. The same alpha drives both physical coordinates. The full original radius is `r_0^2+(b+w_0)^2`.

For literal H_m, let `P_t=prod_(k<t)(h_k-1)`, `p=min_t P_t>0`, `S=S_m`, `D=1+2S`. The explicit choice

    b=1, r_0=1, w_0=2S,
    s=min(1/4,p/[16 S(3S+1)]), R=2/s+2

is proved feasible through the entire word. It has `S<=w_t<3S`, `(15/16)P_t<=r_t<=P_t`, and `alpha_t>P_t/2`. Thus every sign flip and projection face is real. Its original start is `(1,-D)`, original radius `D^2+1`, terminal shape `((-1)^n r_n,-1-S-sQ)`, and EXACT payoff

    [D+2sQ+alpha_n^2]/[D^2+1],  Q=sum h_t alpha_t.

All geometry is fixed before the forward run. No wall increase, overwritten future, auxiliary query, free height, child radius, or alternative invalidated minimum is used.

## Decisive full-hierarchy result for this policy

The proof then permits ARBITRARY b>0, angle 0<s<1, original point, and nonbinding truncation length within the entire geometric policy: all queries lie at or below the horizontal line through the translated vertex; the two slanted edges alternate at every step; every projection is on an open slanted edge; the third face never binds.

On complete H_m with the one enclosing m>=1536 and its inherited parameters, it derives

    2F(x_n)/||x_0||^2 <= 33/(32 D_m).

With the supplied accepted comparison `B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50`, the benchmark-normalized payoff is strictly below `1551/1600`. This is a complete scoped manual failure proof, still UNVERIFIED.

The quantitative mechanism is explicit. Set `A_m=sum h_t P_t`. Whole-word recursion gives `A_m<3(3/2)^m`. The original-radius identity yields

    payoff <= [1+s^2 a^2 A_m^2]/D_m + Pi_m^2.

For `s A_m<=1/8`, this is at most `33/(32D_m)`. Otherwise the actual final H_(m-1) child starts with radial amplitude at most `q r_0`, where `q=beta^(2^(m-1))/c<2^(-m^2)`. Keeping its w coordinate nonnegative charges

    b/r_0 <=9 q A_m,

and the terminal face condition gives `w_n/r_0<8 A_m Pi_m`. These estimates make the entire payoff smaller than `1/D_m`. The right-child argument uses its actual inherited prefix product and original radius, with no child normalization.

## Scope that remains open

The new ceiling does not cover trajectories that go above the translated vertex, bind the third face, enter the body, remain on a face for several steps, or project to the vertex. It does not cover more complicated higher-dimensional coupling or the general multivalue K_a inverse construction. The origin is unique in the second geometry, so there is no hidden improvement from another original minimum inside that stated family.

No all-word lower construction, full-class upper certificate, or resolution of the exact limit-one target was obtained. The low-mass Huber region remains covered by the already accepted result. Neither this packet nor its bound changes the exact all-integer benchmark. Do not read `33/(32 D_m)` as a W(H_m) upper bound.

No further approach or renewal is included. All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. Preserve the full proof for scope and the feasibility induction; its final hashes are supplied in the child closeout message.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001576 -->
# R11: actual repeated-edge coupling and a complete-hierarchy failure bound

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft. The original minimax target remains ACTIVE / OPEN.**

Grant `3b9683ee8aaee8c8`. One assigned gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation; two substantive analytical approaches; no mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents, or additional model calls. This packet is an actual-construction attempt with a scoped failure theorem. It contains no complete original-target candidate.

The genuinely coupled construction in Section 4 uses one fixed triangle, alternates between its two slanted edges at every physical step of the complete inherited word, and charges the original radius. Translating its vertex produces a real coupling between the transverse drift and the alternating tangential component. Nevertheless, Section 5 bounds the entire specified trajectory class by `33/(32 D_m)`, below the exact benchmark on the supplied stress family. This is a bound on a restricted construction, not on the full worst-case error.

## 1. Target, evidence, and boundaries

The unchanged target is either

\[
 \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathcal W_n^*}{U_n^{\rm ZJ}}=1,
 \qquad
 \mathcal W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_*)}{L\|x_0-x_*\|^2},
 \quad U_n^{\rm ZJ}=B_n^{-1},
\]

or a rigorous asymptotic separation certified over the full smooth-convex function class. An affirmative result must hold for every sufficiently large integer n and every predetermined nonnegative word, with a schedule-independent error term. We preserve W = 2R, every original selected minimizer, the original denominator, the actual endpoint, finite dimensions, and the exact all-integer benchmark:

\[
 C_0=B_0=1,\quad
 C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),\quad
 B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l),
\]
\[
 \Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,
 \qquad G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2.
\]

Read in full: TASK.md; OPERATOR.md; PROBLEM.md; `MATHEMATICAL_ELABORATION_20261009_R10B.md`; `R10B_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md`; `variable_wall_recursive_witness_20261009_r10b/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`; `R10_PARENT_VARIABLE_WALL_NOTE_20261009.md`; and accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d`, including its statement. The designated TASK SHA256 is `832c4fbeadaeb4fa84199eeaa36fedb18b57f99f8a730ecace7d4848644f4ef5`, and the designated R10b proof SHA256 is `004ec6c3bf51b7c924bbf159f2b7b1b35b1a42347fa9ca84d5cff6d57693efce`; both were checked and matched.

The benchmark comparison `B_(2^m-1)/D_m < 47/50` is imported only at the scope supplied by the signed-fact handoff for `931b9653ce0f7201`. Its proof was not independently repeated in this call. The single-cut formula from `b98bb807d514e80d` is old; no independent bank of those witnesses is counted as a new approach.

The parent additionally reported that the general conic obstruction used in Section 3 is already a public route boundary, including finding `21d26350c9141917`. Accordingly Section 3 is explicitly an actual instantiation and sanity boundary, not a new promising mechanism or a novelty claim. The translated, nonhomogeneous triangle in Sections 4--5 is the second and final approach in this grant.

All results derived here are manual, source-bound research drafts, not verifier-accepted facts. No conclusion about failure of the original minimax proposition follows from failure of either policy.

## 2. The literal stress word and elementary transport quantities

Fix one enclosing m >= 1536 and retain its parameters in every child:

\[
 \sigma=1+\sqrt2,\quad \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad
 \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad c=\sigma(1+\epsilon),\quad a_h=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
 H_0=\varnothing,\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
 \quad d_j=a_hc^j,\quad S_j=\sum H_j,\quad D_j=1+2S_j.
 \tag{2.1}
\]

Every physical step is strictly larger than 1. For a word of length n write

\[
 P_0=1,\qquad P_t=\prod_{k<t}(h_k-1),\qquad
 A(h)=\sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t P_t.
 \tag{2.2}
\]

These are analytic finite sums and products, not numerical evaluations or an executed array. Let `Pi_j` be the complete product for H_j and `A_j=A(H_j)`. With `u=a_h/(c-2)` the exact identities are

\[
 S_j+1=uc^j+(1-u)2^j,
 \quad \Pi_j=\beta^{2^j-1}c^{-j},
 \quad q_j:=d_j\Pi_j=\frac{\beta^{2^j}}c.
 \tag{2.3}
\]

Here `1/2<u<1` and `2<c<5/2`; in particular `D_j<2c^j` for j>=1. Every prefix product of excesses in H_j is at most 1. To check this by induction, a prefix either lies inside the left child, ends just after the bridge, or consists of that left child and bridge followed by a right-child prefix. The latter two cases have prefix multiplier `q_(j-1)<1/c`. The empty prefix has product 1.

Splitting the actual sum in (2.2) into its left child, bridge, and right child gives

\[
 A_{j+1}=(1+q_j)A_j+\Pi_j+q_j.
 \tag{2.4}
\]

Since `Pi_j<=1` and `q_j<1/2`,

\[
 A_j<3(3/2)^j<2^{j+2}\qquad(j\ge1).
 \tag{2.5}
\]

Also `A_(j+1)>A_j`. None of these estimates resets the inherited parameter at a child.

At the entrance to the final H_(m-1) child in H_m, the true prefix product is

\[
 q:=d_{m-1}\Pi_{m-1}=\frac{\beta^{2^{m-1}}}{c}.
 \tag{2.6}
\]

For m>=16, `2^(m-1)>=4m^3`: it holds at 16, and the ratio between successive left and right sides is at least one because `2m^3>=(m+1)^3` for m>=4. Therefore

\[
 q<2^{-m^2},\qquad \Pi_m<2^{-m^2},\qquad
 D_m<2^{2m+1}.
 \tag{2.7}
\]

Indeed `(1-1/(4m))^(2^(m-1))<=exp(-2^(m-1)/(4m))<=exp(-m^2)<2^(-m^2)`. The very strong loss in (2.7) is specific to this same-parameter complete word; it is not claimed for arbitrary schedules.

## 3. Approach 1: actual repeated faces on a truncated cone

### 3.1 The general homogeneity boundary, including the original minimum

For a closed convex cone C, let

\[
 F_C(x)=\max_{g\in C}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}
       =\tfrac12\|P_Cx\|^2.
\]

The equality follows from the projection variational inequality: if `x=p+z`, with `p=P_Cx`, then `z` belongs to the polar cone `C^o` and `<p,z>=0`. Thus the minimum set is `C^o` and the distance from x to this entire minimum set is `||p||`. This also proves the asserted original closest-minimum radius; it is not a denominator replacement at a child.

For h=1+d>=1 and the next gradient `q=P_C(x-hp)`, write `x-hp=z-dp`. Cone projection gives

\[
 \|q\|^2=\langle q,z-dp\rangle
 \le-d\langle q,p\rangle\le d\|q\|\|p\|.
\]

Hence `||q||<=d||p||`. For any word with every step at least 1, and any original selected minimum x_*,

\[
 \frac{2F_C(x_n)}{\|x_0-x_*\|^2}
 \le\prod_t(h_t-1)^2.
 \tag{3.1}
\]

The same conclusion applies to a compact truncation when all queried projections coincide with the cone projections and the polar minimum cone is unchanged. This is the old homogeneity obstruction in direct form. Merely arranging many face reentries does not remove it.

### 3.2 A finite two-dimensional realization at every physical step

This realization makes the failure concrete rather than merely conditional on a possible itinerary. For the complete H_m let `S=S_m`, `n=2^m-1`, and

\[
 p=\min_{0\le t\le n}P_t>0,
 \qquad s^2=\frac{p}{16S(S+1)},\quad a=\sqrt{1-s^2},
 \quad u=(a,s),\quad v=(-a,s).
 \tag{3.2}
\]

Let C be the cone generated by u,v and use the compact triangle

\[
 K=\operatorname{conv}\{0,2u,2v\},\qquad x_0=u,
 \quad F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\]

The function is convex, globally 1-smooth, and has minimizer 0. Its full minimum set is C^o, and 0 is the original closest minimizer to x_0, so the original squared radius is exactly 1.

At time 0 the gradient is u. At time 1 the point is `-(h_0-1)u`. Thereafter write the candidate physical point as

\[
 x_t=((-1)^t r_t,-w_t),\quad r_t>0,\quad w_t>0,
 \quad \alpha_t=ar_t-sw_t>0.
\]

Projection onto C is then `alpha_t u` for even t and `alpha_t v` for odd t. To check this, its residual is orthogonal to the selected ray; its inner product with the other ray is nonpositive, since r_t,w_t>=0. Projection onto K is identical whenever `alpha_t<2`.

With the notation `r_0=a,w_0=-s,alpha_0=1`, the exact alternating update is

\[
 r_{t+1}=(h_ta^2-1)r_t-h_tasw_t,
 \qquad w_{t+1}=w_t+h_ts\alpha_t.
 \tag{3.3}
\]

The first transition gives `r_1=aP_1,w_1=sP_1`. Induction using every actual step gives

\[
 \frac{15}{16}aP_t\le r_t\le aP_t,\qquad
 0<w_t\le sS,\qquad \frac12P_t<\alpha_t\le P_t
 \quad(1\le t\le n).
 \tag{3.4}
\]

For the lower estimate, expand the first recurrence after time 1:

\[
 r_t=aP_t-P_t\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}
 \frac{h_ks(s r_k+a w_k)}{P_{k+1}}.
\]

Using `r_k<=a`, `w_k<=sS`, and `P_(k+1)>=p`, its relative loss is at most `s^2 S(S+1)/p=1/16`. Then

\[
 \alpha_t\ge(15/16)a^2P_t-s^2S
 \ge(225/256)P_t-p/[16(S+1)]>P_t/2,
\]

because `a^2>=15/16` and S>1. This proves positivity at the next query and closes the induction, including the assumed sign alternation. The upper estimates follow from `w_t>=0`, `r_(t+1)<=(h_t-1)r_t`, and `alpha_t<=a r_t` after the first query. Thus the truncating third edge never binds.

The trajectory really revisits the same slanted face after every two steps; it is not a collection of independent single-cut paths. Its complete terminal payoff satisfies

\[
 \Pi_m^2/4<\frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}=\alpha_n^2\le\Pi_m^2.
 \tag{3.5}
\]

By (2.7) this is far below `1/D_m`. This policy is fully realizable at all depths, but conic homogeneity makes it quantitatively unusable. The parent identified this mechanism as an instantiation of an old route boundary; it is not credited as a new lower-bound advance.

## 4. Approach 2: translate the vertex and couple drift to repeated returns

### 4.1 One fixed triangle and its exact projection law

Fix b>0 and `0<s<1`, put `a=sqrt(1-s^2)`, and retain the rays `u=(a,s),v=(-a,s)`. Let

\[
 e=(0,-b),\qquad
 K=\operatorname{conv}\{e,e+Ru,e+Rv\},
 \qquad R>b/s.
 \tag{4.1}
\]

The origin lies strictly inside this triangle. Consequently F_K is convex, globally 1-smooth, and has unique minimum 0 with value 0: for x nonzero a sufficiently small positive multiple of x belongs to K and gives a strictly positive envelope value. There is no minimum-cone reset in this approach.

At a query

\[
 x=(\zeta r,-b-w),\qquad \zeta\in\{-1,1\},\quad r>0,\quad w\ge0,
 \qquad \alpha=ar-sw>0,
 \tag{4.2}
\]

provided `alpha<R`, its gradient is

\[
 g=e+\alpha(\zeta a,s).
 \tag{4.3}
\]

Indeed `x-e=(zeta r,-w)` projects onto the selected cone ray with coefficient alpha. That projected point lies on the corresponding finite triangle edge, so it is also the triangle projection. The opposing-ray normal inequality is the same as in Section 3. No projected point touches the third face.

The value at this query is exactly

\[
 2F_K(x)=b^2+2bw+\alpha^2.
 \tag{4.4}
\]

This follows by shifting the maximization variable to `g=e+z`: the value is `<x,e>-||e||^2/2+||P_C(x-e)||^2/2`. The shift has a genuine cross effect in the dynamics because e is not orthogonal to either ray.

Suppose the sign flips at the next physical step while (4.2) remains valid. Then the exact recurrence is

\[
 r_{t+1}=(h_ta^2-1)r_t-h_tasw_t
         =(h_t-1)r_t-h_ts(s r_t+a w_t),
\]
\[
 w_{t+1}=w_t-h_tb+h_ts\alpha_t,
 \qquad \alpha_t=ar_t-sw_t.
 \tag{4.5}
\]

The same alpha feeds both equations. This coupling is absent in an orthogonal sum of Huber components. There is no splitting into radius-weighted independent single-cut payoffs.

### 4.2 An explicit complete-H_m instance with no unproved itinerary condition

For H_m, take

\[
 b=1,\quad r_0=1,\quad w_0=2S_m,\quad
 s=\min\left\{\frac14,\frac{p}{16S_m(3S_m+1)}\right\},
 \qquad R=2/s+2,
 \tag{4.6}
\]

where p is the positive finite minimum in (3.2). All of these choices depend on the whole physical word and are fixed before running it. Use the one triangle (4.1) and original start

\[
 x_0=(1,-D_m),\qquad \|x_0-0\|^2=D_m^2+1.
 \tag{4.7}
\]

The candidate recursion (4.5) is feasible through every depth and every physical step. More precisely,

\[
 S_m\le w_t<3S_m,\qquad
 \frac{15}{16}P_t\le r_t\le P_t,\qquad
 \alpha_t>P_t/2>0.
 \tag{4.8}
\]

Here is an induction that proves feasibility rather than assumes it. Let `T_t=sum_(k<t)h_k` be the physical prefix sum, distinct from the hierarchy sums S_j, and let `Q_t=sum_(k<t)h_k alpha_k`. The second recurrence gives

\[
 w_t=2S_m-T_t+sQ_t.
\]

When the preceding gradients are valid and `r_k<=P_k<=1`, their positive coefficients obey `alpha_k<=1`. Hence `S_m<=w_t<=2S_m+sS_m<3S_m`. Unrolling the first recurrence gives

\[
 r_t=P_t\left[1-\sum_{k<t}
 \frac{h_k s(s r_k+a w_k)}{P_{k+1}}\right]
 \ge P_t\left[1-\frac{s(s+3S_m)S_m}{p}\right]
 \ge(15/16)P_t.
\]

Because `a>=3/4`, `S_m>1`, and `3sS_m<=3p/[16(3S_m+1)]<=3p/64`, the next alpha is at least `(45/64)P_t-(3/64)p>P_t/2`. Thus the next r is positive, the sign really flips, and the next point satisfies (4.2). This closes the induction. All alpha values are below 1<R, so the third face is inactive for the entire forward run.

The terminal shape and exact payoff are

\[
 x_n=((-1)^n r_n,-1-S_m-sQ),\qquad
 Q=\sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t\alpha_t>0,
\]
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}
 =\frac{D_m+2sQ+\alpha_n^2}{D_m^2+1}.
 \quad}
 \tag{4.9}
\]

The original radius is fully paid, including the transverse drift. The two edges are visited in the pattern `u,v,u,v,...` throughout the literal complete H_m. There is one objective and one original minimizer; no future trajectory is overwritten, no wall is enlarged after use, and no child is renormalized.

At fixed finite word, sending s to zero through positive legal values gives the precise limiting payoff `(D_m+Pi_m^2)/(D_m^2+1)`: the tangential endpoint tends to Pi_m and must not be dropped. Thus the translated policy recovers the baseline order while keeping actual repeated-edge coupling. The next section shows that enlarging its angle or changing its original b,w_0,r_0 within the whole specified policy cannot pass the benchmark stress test.

## 5. A whole-class ceiling for this coupled policy

The class in this section permits arbitrary b>0, `0<s<1`, r_0>0, w_0>=0, and a finite R for which the whole physical trajectory satisfies (4.2)--(4.5), with strict alternation of the two slanted edges and no third-face projection. It is not restricted to the tiny-angle choice (4.6).

The conditions are geometric: every physical query, including the endpoint, lies on or below the horizontal line through the translated vertex; every projection lies strictly along the appropriate slanted edge; and the transverse sign changes at every step. Querying the vertex, entering the triangle, reaching above the vertex, holding one edge for several steps, or activating the third face is outside this policy. The selected minimum is necessarily the unique original 0.

### 5.1 Exact radius identity and two elementary consequences

Write `S=S_m`, `D=D_m`, `A=A_m`, `P=Pi_m`, `Q=sum h_t alpha_t`, and `v_0=b+w_0`. From (4.5),

\[
 0<r_t\le P_t r_0,\quad 0<\alpha_t\le ar_t,
 \quad Q\le aA r_0,
 \quad w_n\le ar_n/s\le aP r_0/s.
 \tag{5.1}
\]

The strict alpha inequality gives the last bound. The original radius and terminal twice-value are

\[
 R_0^2=r_0^2+v_0^2,
 \qquad N=b^2+2bw_n+\alpha_n^2
           =2b(v_0+sQ)-Db^2+\alpha_n^2.
 \tag{5.2}
\]

Completing the square in the actual b, followed by Cauchy--Schwarz in the original coordinates, gives

\[
 \frac{N}{R_0^2}
 \le\frac{1+s^2a^2A^2}{D}+P^2.
 \tag{5.3}
\]

This is only one estimate for an already specified legal trajectory. The proof below obtains a uniform quantitative ceiling from the actual final child, rather than treating (5.3) as an unproved global cost condition.

### 5.2 Small angle

If `sA<=1/8`, then by (2.7)

\[
 \frac{N}{R_0^2}\le\frac{65}{64D}+P^2
 \le\frac{33}{32D}.
 \tag{5.4}
\]

The last inequality uses `DP^2<2^(2m+1-2m^2)<1/64`, valid already at m>=16. This controls even angles chosen to depend on the entire horizon and word.

### 5.3 Larger angle: charge the full final child

Suppose `sA>1/8`, so `1/s<8A`. At entrance time tau to the final H_(m-1), (2.6) and (5.1) give

\[
 r_\tau\le q r_0,\qquad
 w_\tau\le a q r_0/s.
\]

Let `S_L=S_(m-1)` and `A_L=A_(m-1)`. Since the entire right child also obeys (4.5), its accumulated ray coefficient satisfies

\[
 Q_{\rm right}\le aA_L r_\tau\le aqA_Lr_0.
\]

Its terminal w is nonnegative, so the exact second recurrence requires

\[
 bS_L\le w_\tau+sQ_{\rm right},
 \qquad
 \frac b{r_0}\le\frac{aq(1/s+sA_L)}{S_L}\le9qA.
 \tag{5.5}
\]

Here `S_L>=1`, `A_L<=A`, `a<=1`, and `s<=1`. This is the cost of sustaining the drift through the actual final child. It retains the actual inherited product q; the denominator is still R_0.

Furthermore `w_n/r_0<8AP` and `alpha_n/r_0<=P`. Since `R_0^2>=r_0^2`,

\[
 \frac N{R_0^2}
 \le81q^2A^2+144qPA^2+P^2
 <2^{2m+12-2m^2}<\frac1D.
 \tag{5.6}
\]

For the middle inequality insert `q,P<2^(-m^2)` and `A<2^(m+2)` from (2.5)--(2.7); the coefficient is bounded by 226<256. For the last inequality multiply by `D<2^(2m+1)` and use `4m+13-2m^2<0` for m>=16. Every estimate applies to the whole physical suffix, regardless of how the original geometry was chosen.

Combining (5.4) and (5.6),

\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0-0\|^2}\le\frac{33}{32D_m}
 \quad\text{for the entire below-vertex, alternating-two-edge policy on }H_m.
 \qquad}
 \tag{5.7}
\]

Using only the supplied signed benchmark comparison,

\[
 B_{2^m-1}\frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}
 <\frac{33}{32}\frac{47}{50}
 =\frac{1551}{1600}<1.
 \tag{5.8}
\]

Thus this actual coupled family misses the benchmark by a fixed factor even after arbitrary horizon-dependent choices of its angle, translated vertex, initial point, and sufficiently long sides. Its failure is stronger than merely observing that the particular small-angle witness resembles Huber. It does not invoke monotone active sets or a decomposition into independent reservoirs.

## 6. Audit and exact remaining scope

Both geometries are finite-dimensional compact projection bodies after their explicit truncations. Their envelope functions are convex and globally 1-smooth with the displayed original minima. Projection optimality, strict branch signs, every physical step, terminal value, and original radius were derived explicitly. For the cone, the closest original polar minimum was accounted for; for the translated triangle the original minimum 0 is unique. All side lengths, angles, and starts are fixed before the forward run. No auxiliary-query or wall-freedom assumption is used.

The second approach is genuinely coupled: the common alpha in (4.5) affects the alternating component and the transverse drift, and the `2bsQ` term in (5.2) is retained and paid. Its exclusion uses the low-product final child when that coupling is too large for (5.3). It is not the old single-reflection Huber formula, an independent positive sum, or the nested-active-set decomposition.

The exact scope is essential. Nothing here excludes a triangle trajectory that reaches above the translated vertex, binds the third face, enters the interior, pauses on a face, or visits the vertex. Nothing excludes a higher-dimensional body with more complex returns, a globally coupled multivalue K_a inverse policy, or any full-function-class hard instance. The first cone observation is a known boundary and has no novelty claim. The second policy has a complete actual all-depth construction and a complete scoped failure calculation, but supplies no viable all-word lower rule.

In particular, (5.7) is NOT `W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=33/(32D_m)`. Replacing the restricted left side by W would be invalid. No full-class asymptotic separation has been obtained. No uniform all-word lower bound has been obtained. The already accepted Huber witness still covers `sum h <= (B_n-1)/2`; this packet does not close its high-mass complement. The exact all-integer C_n/B_n benchmark remains unchanged.

There is no complete original-target candidate for formal review. The two allotted approaches have reached their explicitly scoped outcomes. Continuing through an additional geometry or another model call would require a fresh allocation; no such continuation is performed here.

- One read-only search initially returned Windows path error 123 because a shell wildcard was passed literally; a corrected bounded directory search succeeded. There was no mathematical program failure or silent claim of successful execution.

# Dense-history family obstruction handoff

**JOINT UNVERIFIED — STOP.** One invocation completed; no renewal, formal submission, or accepted fact. Original minimax target OPEN. Parent closes allocation `6c03ab372c46e5bb`.

## Result to audit

`REPORT.md` independently checks the exact variable-step multiplier identity, all cut/divergence conventions, physical/terminal diagonal coefficients, and the full-class threshold

\[
\Gamma\ge\frac{225}{289}(S+\tfrac12)^2-\tfrac14
\implies \mathcal W_n(h)\le\frac{17}{16(1+2S)}.
\]

It then excludes the entire specified free monotone `A/D` family on the actual same-enclosing-`m` hierarchy for every `m>=5`, stronger than the requested range `m>=1536`. Arbitrary nonnegative diagonal slack and arbitrary terminal penalty are included. Zero explicit laws were instantiated because the family-level obstruction settles their feasibility jointly.

## Short proof chain

Define `delta_k=q_k-r_k^2 q_(k+1)`, `T_k=sum_(i<k)h_i`,

\[
B_k=\sum_{i<k}h_iA_i,\qquad E_k=\sum_{i\ge k}h_iD_{i+1}.
\]

All cuts imply the exact identity

\[
q_k+\sum_{i<k}\delta_i=(T_k+1)^2-2B_kE_k.
\]

The proof is an increment calculation using

\[
A_kE_k-D_{k+1}B_k-2r_kA_kD_{k+1}=T_k+h_k.
\]

Nonnegative slack gives `q_k<=(T_k+1)^2`, strictly at every interior index. Strictness follows because the last positive cut forces `D_n>0`, hence all `D_i>0`, and both `B_k,E_k` are positive in the interior.

At the central bridge of a palindrome with side mass `s` and step `1+r`, the central cut and decreasing `D` give

\[
s+1+r<A_kD_{k+1}(s+1-r).
\]

Thus `s+1-r>0` and

\[
q_{k+1}>\frac{3(s+1)+r}{s+1-r},\qquad
r^2q_{k+1}\le q_k<(s+1)^2.
\]

With `x=r/(s+1)`, these require

\[
(x+1)(x^2+2x-1)<0,
\quad\text{hence}\quad x<\sqrt2-1.
\]

For the actual word, fix the same enclosing `m`, set `j=m-1` and `Lambda=1+sqrt(2)`. Then

\[
r=ac^j,\qquad s=2^j-1+a\frac{c^j-2^j}{c-2},
\]
\[
\Lambda-\frac{s+1}{r}
=\frac{\varepsilon}{\beta(c-2)}
\left[\beta\Lambda^2-
(2\Lambda+3+\Lambda^2\varepsilon)(2/c)^j\right]>0
\]

for every `m>=5`. The exact uniform comparison is first bracket term `>9/2` and second term `<9/2`, using `epsilon<=1/20`, `beta>9/10`, `12/5<Lambda<5/2`, and `(2/c)^j<(5/6)^4<1/2`. Thus `x>sqrt(2)-1`, a contradiction.

## Scope and routing

The old `h_i h_j(u+v 1_(i>j))` kernel is a different family; its old exclusion was not used. Chikhi's paper is prior literature, not a new discovery or a variable-step theorem imported here.

This is a multiplier-family obstruction, not a legal hard instance or a disproof of the desired GD upper bound. No target-level verification should be requested as if this resolved the minimax problem. If the parent selects this result for verification, the exact statement to audit is the incompatibility of the specified scalar cut, monotonicity, and diagonal conditions, with the prefix identity and strict root inequality as its principal dependencies.

Resources used: public text/source reads, manual reasoning, local Markdown writes, byte hashes only. Mathematical programs, arithmetic/recurrence/trajectory replays, solvers, numerical-array analysis, formal calls, models beyond this invocation, subagents, service/worker actions, and truth-store edits: zero. The application Goal was not changed and is still paused in the supplied current report. Current-turn research authorization came from the explicit human continuation.

# R12 primal handoff — fixed A/B profiles rejected; C unresolved

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft.** Grant 8298ccaea9198109. Original minimax target OPEN. This is not a verifier-accepted fact or a full-class objective upper bound.

The completed proof is PROOF_ATTEMPT.md in this packet. Its Sections 1--8 give an actual static-function counterexample to both fixed dominance profiles A and B for **every sufficiently large odd literal enclosing depth \(m\)**. Section 10 records the distinct second approach on Profile C and the precise point where its proposed cyclic triangular trajectory fails feasibility.

## Result that the parent can use after checking

Keep exactly the enclosing-\(m\) parameters
\[
\epsilon=1/(4m),\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]
and the complete physical word \(H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j)\). Let \(S_j=\sum H_j\), \(S=S_m\), \(n=2^m-1\), \(k_j=ac^j\).

Use the one-dimensional static Huber function
\[
F(x)=x^2/2\ (|x|\le1),\qquad F(x)=|x|-1/2\ (|x|\ge1),
\quad g(x)=\operatorname{clip}(x,-1,1).
\]
It is convex and 1-smooth, has unique minimum \(x_\star=0\), and has \(Q_{\star,i}=0\) at every actual sample.

Choose
\[
X_1=\beta,\qquad
X_j=S_{j-1}+1+k_{j-1}-X_{j-1},\quad x_0=X_m.
\]
The proof establishes \(X_j\le k_j\). For each \(j\ge2\), the first child and bridge starting at \(X_j\) have gradient \(+1\) and end at \(-X_{j-1}\); odd symmetry then continues the actual right suffix. The last physical query is \((-1)^{m-1}\beta\), and the real endpoint is
\[
x_n=g_n=(-1)^m q,\qquad q=a\beta.
\]
All \(2^m-1\) physical steps are retained. The proof gives the explicit sample \(x_i\), gradient \(g_i\), and value \(F_i\) at every index. The original radius is \(X_m^2\), and the actual normalized objective value is exactly \(q^2/X_m^2\).

For profile \(P\), write
\[
U_P=\sum_{i<n}u_i^P,\quad G_P=\sum_{i<n}u_i^Pg_i,\quad
u_n^P=B_{\rm cert}-U_P,\quad B_{\rm cert}=1+(32/17)S.
\]
On odd \(m\), define \(M_P=u_n^Pq-G_P\). The exact given residual is
\[
D_{u^P}=-2X_mM_P-M_P^2-B_{\rm cert}q^2.
\]

With
\[
r=\sqrt2-1,\qquad v=\frac{r^2}{1+2\sqrt2}<1/20,
\]
the literal enclosing-depth limits are
\[
X_m/S\to r,\quad q\to r,\quad
U_A/S\to(3+v)/4,\quad
G_A/S\to r/2+(1+v)/(4\sqrt2).
\]
Consequently
\[
\frac{M_A}{S}\to
\eta_A=r\left(\frac{43}{68}-\frac v4\right)
-\frac{1+v}{4\sqrt2}
>\frac{411}{6800}>\frac3{50}.
\]
The B comparison is exact on the **physical indices only**:
\[
0\le\rho_i^A-\rho_i^B
=\frac{T_i(S-1-T_i)}{2S(S+1+T_i)}
\le r^2/2,\qquad i<n.
\]
Here \(T_i\le S-h_{n-1}<S-1\) because \(h_{n-1}=1+a>1\). The ordering fails at the terminal \(\rho_n\), which is not used in this comparison; \(u_n\) is separately prescribed by the total-mass rule. If \(N_-=\sum_{i<n}h_i\max(-g_i,0)\), then
\[
M_B\ge M_A-(r^2/2)N_-,\qquad
\limsup N_-/S\le(1-r)/2.
\]
It follows that
\[
\liminf_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}M_B/S
>\frac{207}{6800}>\frac3{100}.
\]
Both \(M_P\) are therefore positive for every sufficiently large odd enclosing \(m\), making both actual original-radius residuals strictly negative. The proof uses exact recurrences and uniform geometric tails, not a finite numerical sign or a free Gram direction.

## Original-goal boundary

The actual objective payoff satisfies
\[
S_m^2\frac{q^2}{X_m^2}\to1.
\]
It has order \(S_m^{-2}\). Thus the result rejects these particular dominance profiles because of their excessive terminal residual square; it does not attain the order-\(S_m^{-1}\) benchmark and does not disprove \(\mathcal W(H_m)\le1/B_{\rm cert}\). The exact original all-integer \(B_n\), normalization \(W=2R\), full function class, and selected original radius remain intact.

For these fixed profiles, a representation of \(D_u\) by nonnegative actual interpolation rows plus a PSD remainder is impossible if the current draft is correct. Other profiles and other certificates are not excluded.

## Second approach and Profile C

After the A/B counterexample was reported, the parent fixed Profile C, \(\rho_i=1-T_i/(8S)\), in R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md. Its checked SHA-256 is 9001df1d3b8ecdbc198d8be2e85657610263fc93fdf8fd50f3e295f058e58030. Only the unused second approach was authorized.

The attempt used one actual regular triangle
\[
K=\operatorname{conv}\{(1,0),(-1/2,\sqrt3/2),(-1/2,-\sqrt3/2)\},
\quad F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\|g\|^2/2\},
\]
with selected minimum 0, and a proposed three-vertex cyclic suffix policy. For rotation \(R\) by \(2\pi/3\), \(w_j=R^{m-j}v_0\), and \(L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}\), its preassigned start is \(Z_1=\beta w_1\), \(Z_j=L_jw_j+Z_{j-1}\), \(x_0=Z_m\).

The necessary root bridge normal is \(n_m=Z_{m-1}+k_{m-1}v_0\). The draft derives
\[
\frac{\langle n_m,v_1-v_0\rangle}{S_{m-1}}
\longrightarrow \frac{3r^2}{2(1+r+r^2)}>0.
\]
The cyclic vertex table therefore violates the projection normal cone at macroscopic scale. The first genuine departure from vertex \(v_0\) occurs when the physical cumulative time exceeds an explicit threshold \(\tau_m\), with
\[
\tau_m/S_{m-1}\to1-r^2/(1+r+r^2)\in(0,1).
\]
This is a real binding change within the first child or at its endpoint. The subsequent actual triangular trajectory was not solved. No sign of \(D_{u^C}\), and no terminal value computed from the infeasible table, is claimed. Profile C is unresolved.
