# R12 full-word primal obstruction to both fixed dominance profiles

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft.** Grant `8298ccaea9198109`. This packet gives a complete proposed counterexample to the two fixed dominance functionals, for every sufficiently large odd enclosing depth. It does not resolve the original minimax problem. No verifier has reviewed the new mathematics.

Resource scope: one authorized native invocation; two substantive analytical approaches. The first is an exact nested, repeatedly reflecting trajectory of one static capped quadratic. The second, authorized by the later Profile C addendum, checks cyclic binding vertices in one regular triangular projection body and identifies the first actual loss of that vertex policy. No mathematical programs, arrays, solvers, extra agents, external model calls, or formal submissions were used. Read/write/hash commands are administrative only.

## 1. Locked sources and statement

The governing inputs were read: `OPERATOR.md`, the complete project `PROBLEM.md`, `R11_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md`, the complete `R12_FIXED_DOMINANCE_PROFILES_20261009.md`, and `R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md`. The task SHA-256 was checked as `04af5ae694d258315035fff2b470f3ec1a4d59f123e304572d4ab2c21b1774fe`; the fixed-profile SHA-256 was checked as `277b5d3e3473a358acf98ae9f10c64b063b462195da307c73c1ca0dd0d7439f4`. No other R12 packet or private worker memory was read.

For one enclosing integer \(m\ge1536\), throughout this proof set

\[
c_0=1+\sqrt2,\quad \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad
\beta=1-\epsilon,\quad c=c_0(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
H_0=(),\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\qquad
N_j=2^j-1,\quad S_j=\sum H_j.
\]

All children use these same enclosing-\(m\) parameters. Put \(n=N_m\), \(S=S_m\), \(h=H_m\), and \(T_i=\sum_{t<i}h_t\). The certificate total, distinguished from the original all-integer benchmark \(B_n\), is

\[
B_{\rm cert}=1+\frac{32}{17}S.
\]

For \(P=A,B\), the fixed weights are

\[
\rho_i^A=1-\frac{T_i}{2S},\qquad
\rho_i^B=\frac{S+1}{S+1+T_i},\qquad
u_i^P=\rho_i^P h_i\quad(i<n),
\]
\[
U_P=\sum_{i<n}u_i^P,\qquad u_n^P=B_{\rm cert}-U_P>0.
\]

**Proposed conclusion.** There is an integer \(M\ge1536\) such that, for every odd \(m\ge M\), the static function and actual full-\(H_m\) trajectory constructed below have

\[
D_{u^A}<0,\qquad D_{u^B}<0.
\]

These are counterexamples to the *specified profile inequalities*, including their original selected minimizer and original radius. The actual objective payoff of the witnesses is of order \(S^{-2}\), well below the order \(S^{-1}\) benchmark. The conclusion consequently does not disprove an upper bound on \(\mathcal W(H_m)\), or settle the original limit \(\mathcal W_n^*/U_n^{\rm ZJ}\).

## 2. One static smooth convex function

Use the same one-dimensional function for every \(m\):

\[
F(x)=
\begin{cases}
x^2/2,& |x|\le1,\\
|x|-1/2,& |x|\ge1.
\end{cases}
\qquad g(x)=F'(x)=\operatorname{clip}(x,-1,1).
\tag{2.1}
\]

The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz, so \(F\) is convex and 1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is \(x_\star=0\), with \(F_\star=0\). At every real \(x\),

\[
2F(x)=2xg(x)-g(x)^2.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Thus all actual minimizer interpolation slacks satisfy

\[
Q_{\star,i}=-2F(x_i)+2g_i x_i-g_i^2=0.
\tag{2.3}
\]

The one scalar path changes its saturated sign at each nested suffix, on the entire physical word, and enters the quadratic interior at the last physical query and at the actual terminal query. Its terminal gradient is retained in \(D_u\). This repeated-reflection trajectory is not a globally affine Huber run or a bank of independent one-reflection components.

## 3. The starting point and all physical iterates

Define \(k_j=ac^j\), and define positive starts by

\[
X_1=\beta,
\qquad
X_j=S_{j-1}+1+k_{j-1}-X_{j-1}\quad(2\le j\le m).
\tag{3.1}
\]

The original start is \(x_0=X_m\). We first establish that this really generates the claimed full-word trajectory at the literal enclosing parameters.

Let

\[
A=\frac{a}{c-2}=\frac{\beta}{c(c-2)}.
\]

Summing the word recursion gives, for all \(j\ge0\),

\[
S_j+1=Ac^j+(1-A)2^j.
\tag{3.2}
\]

Since \(c>c_0\), \(c(c-2)>1\); hence \(0<A<\beta<1\). For \(j\ge2\), put

\[
\delta_j=k_j-(S_{j-1}+1)
=(\beta-A)c^{j-1}-(1-A)2^{j-1}.
\tag{3.3}
\]

Writing \(r=\sqrt2-1=1/c_0\), the first such difference is exactly

\[
\delta_2=\beta c-2-a
=\frac{\epsilon[2r-c_0\epsilon(1+\epsilon)]}{1+\epsilon}>0.
\tag{3.4}
\]

For the displayed positivity it is enough that \(\epsilon\le1/16\), which holds here: \(r>2/5\), \(c_0<5/2\), and \(c_0\epsilon(1+\epsilon)\le85/512<4/5<2r\). Also,

\[
\delta_{j+1}=c\delta_j+(c-2)(1-A)2^{j-1}>\delta_j>0.
\tag{3.5}
\]

Let \(E_j=k_j-X_j\). Then \(E_1=0\), because \(k_1=\beta\), and (3.1) gives

\[
E_j=\delta_j-E_{j-1}.
\tag{3.6}
\]

Induction using (3.5) gives \(0\le E_j\le\delta_j\) for every \(j\ge2\), with \(E_j>0\) there. In particular,

\[
X_j=S_{j-1}+1+E_{j-1}\ge S_{j-1}+1
\quad(j\ge2),\qquad X_j\le k_j.
\tag{3.7}
\]

Start \(H_j\), \(j\ge2\), at \(X_j\). Every query in its first \(H_{j-1}\) has gradient \(+1\): the endpoint of this first child is \(X_j-S_{j-1}=1+E_{j-1}\ge1\). The bridge query also has gradient \(+1\); its physical step \(1+k_{j-1}\) sends that endpoint to

\[
1+E_{j-1}-(1+k_{j-1})=-X_{j-1}.
\tag{3.8}
\]

Oddness of \(g\) implies that the remaining \(H_{j-1}\) is the sign-reversed trajectory starting at \(X_{j-1}\). At the bottom, \(H_1=(1+a)\) starts at \(X_1=\beta\in(0,1)\) and ends at \(-a\beta\). Define

\[
q=a\beta\in(0,1).
\]

The terminal point and terminal gradient of the complete run are therefore exactly

\[
x_n=g_n=(-1)^m q,\qquad F(x_n)=q^2/2.
\tag{3.9}
\]

For completeness, the preceding recursive description specifies *every physical sample* without dropping repeated queries. Let \(I_j=n-N_j\), so that the final nested \(H_j\) starts at physical index \(I_j\). For \(j=m,m-1,\ldots,2\), set

\[
\sigma_j=(-1)^{m-j},\qquad
L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}=S_{j-1}+1+k_{j-1}.
\]

For each physical index \(I_j\le i<I_{j-1}\), put \(p_{j,i}=\sum_{t=I_j}^{i-1}h_t\). Then

\[
x_i=\sigma_j(X_j-p_{j,i}),\quad g_i=\sigma_j,
\quad F(x_i)=|x_i|-1/2.
\tag{3.10}
\]

The last physical sample, at \(i=n-1=I_1\), is

\[
x_{n-1}=g_{n-1}=(-1)^{m-1}\beta,
\qquad F(x_{n-1})=\beta^2/2,
\tag{3.11}
\]

followed by (3.9). Each block in (3.10) consists of the first child and the bridge of its actual suffix; it contains \(2^{j-1}\) physical steps, and its mass is \(L_j\). Together with the final physical step these blocks have exactly \(2^m-1\) steps and mass \(S_m\).

The original radius is \(R_0^2=X_m^2\). There is no later choice of minimizer, body, scale, or child radius.

## 4. Literal enclosing-depth asymptotics

All limits in this section are along \(m\to\infty\) with the literal parameters \(\epsilon_m=1/(4m)\). No fixed-child-depth limit is substituted for this enclosing-depth limit.

Solving (3.1) with (3.2) gives the exact expression

\[
X_m=\frac{A+a}{c+1}c^m+\frac{1-A}{3}2^m
+(-1)^{m-1}\left[\beta-\frac{(A+a)c}{c+1}-\frac{2(1-A)}3\right].
\tag{4.1}
\]

Since \(c\to c_0>2\), \(A\to1\), and \(a\to r\), equations (3.2) and (4.1) imply

\[
S_m\to\infty,\qquad \frac{X_m}{S_m}\longrightarrow
\frac{c_0-1}{c_0+1}=r,\qquad q\longrightarrow r.
\tag{4.2}
\]

Let \(V_j=\sum_{h\in H_j}h^2\). Direct summation of \(V_j=2V_{j-1}+(1+ac^{j-1})^2\) gives

\[
V_j=(2^j-1)
+2a\frac{c^j-2^j}{c-2}
+a^2\frac{c^{2j}-2^j}{c^2-2}.
\tag{4.3}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\frac{V_m}{S_m^2}\longrightarrow
v:=\frac{r^2}{1+2\sqrt2},\qquad 0<v<\frac1{20}.
\tag{4.4}
\]

The last inequality is equivalent to \(59<42\sqrt2\), whose square is \(3481<3528\).

## 5. Exact evaluation of Profile A on the full path

The unsigned physical mass identity fixed by the source gives

\[
U_A=\frac{3S}{4}+\frac{V_m}{4S},
\qquad
\frac{U_A}{S}\longrightarrow\frac{3+v}{4}.
\tag{5.1}
\]

Define the actual signed physical gradient sum \(G_A=\sum_{i<n}u_i^A g_i\). For any block of physical steps of mass \(L\), squared-step sum \(V_{\rm block}\), and starting cumulative time \(t\),

\[
\sum_{\rm block}h_iT_i=tL+\frac{L^2-V_{\rm block}}2.
\tag{5.2}
\]

For the actual block \(j\ge2\), \(t=S-S_j\), \(L=L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}\), and \(V_{\rm block}=V_j-V_{j-1}\). Hence its unsigned Profile A weight is exactly

\[
W_j=\frac{L_j}{2}
+\frac{S_j^2-S_{j-1}^2+V_j-V_{j-1}}{4S}.
\tag{5.3}
\]

The same expression with \(S_0=V_0=0\) defines the last single-step weight \(W_1\). All block gradients are their signs except for the final physical gradient, whose magnitude is \(\beta\). Thus

\[
G_A=\sum_{j=1}^m(-1)^{m-j}W_j
+(-1)^{m-1}(\beta-1)W_1.
\tag{5.4}
\]

The correction in (5.4), divided by \(S\), tends to zero. For each fixed \(k\ge0\), (3.2) and (4.3) give

\[
\frac{S_{m-k}}S\to r^k,\qquad
\frac{V_{m-k}}{S^2}\to vr^{2k}.
\]

Accordingly,

\[
\frac{W_{m-k}}S\to
\frac{1-r}{2}r^k+\frac{(1+v)(1-r^2)}4r^{2k}.
\tag{5.5}
\]

Here the passage from fixed \(k\) to the entire alternating sum is uniform. Indeed, for all sufficiently large enclosing \(m\) and \(0\le j\le m\), (3.2) and (4.3) give \(S_j\le Cc^j\), \(V_j\le Cc^{2j}\), and \(S_m\ge c^m/C\), for one absolute constant \(C\). Formula (5.3) then bounds \(|W_{m-k}|/S\) by \(C'(r^k+r^{2k})\), because \(c\ge c_0\). The tails beyond \(k=K\) tend to zero uniformly as \(K\to\infty\). Taking a finite-prefix limit and then this geometric tail bound justifies summing (5.5).

The resulting limit is

\[
\frac{G_A}{S}\longrightarrow
\gamma_A:=\frac{1-r}{2(1+r)}
+\frac{(1+v)(1-r^2)}{4(1+r^2)}
=\frac r2+\frac{1+v}{4\sqrt2}.
\tag{5.6}
\]

Use odd \(m\), so that the actual terminal gradient is \(-q\). Define the positive-direction residual increment

\[
M_A=u_n^Aq-G_A.
\]

By (4.2), (5.1), and (5.6),

\[
\frac{M_A}{S}\longrightarrow
\eta_A:=r\left(\frac{32}{17}-\frac{3+v}{4}\right)-\gamma_A
=r\left(\frac{43}{68}-\frac v4\right)
-\frac{1+v}{4\sqrt2}.
\tag{5.7}
\]

This constant is strictly positive with a rational margin. Since \(r>2/5\), \(v<1/20\), and \(1/\sqrt2<5/7\),

\[
\eta_A>
\frac25\left(\frac{43}{68}-\frac1{80}\right)
-\frac{21}{20}\frac5{28}
=\frac{411}{6800}>\frac3{50}.
\tag{5.8}
\]

Therefore \(M_A>0\) for every sufficiently large odd enclosing \(m\).

## 6. Profile B: an exact comparison on physical indices

The \(S+1\) in Profile B is retained. Its terminal \(\rho_n\) is not used in the comparison of physical sums below.

For \(0\le t\le1\), define

\[
\rho_\infty^B(t)=\frac1{1+t},\qquad
d(t)=1-\frac t2-\frac1{1+t}
=\frac{t(1-t)}{2(1+t)}.
\]

An elementary derivative check gives

\[
0\le d(t)\le d_*:=\frac{r^2}{2},
\tag{6.1}
\]

with maximum at \(t=r\). For the literal profiles, direct subtraction gives

\[
\rho_i^A-\rho_i^B
=\frac{T_i(S-1-T_i)}{2S(S+1+T_i)}.
\tag{6.2}
\]

For every physical index \(i<n\), the last physical step is \(h_{n-1}=1+a>1\), so \(T_i\le S-(1+a)<S-1\). Thus (6.2) is nonnegative at all the physical indices. Also \(\rho_i^B\ge1/(1+T_i/S)\), and hence

\[
0\le\rho_i^A-\rho_i^B\le d(T_i/S)\le d_*
\qquad(i<n).
\]

This ordering is false at \(i=n\), where \(\rho_n^B>1/2=\rho_n^A\). The terminal weight is separately fixed by \(u_n=B_{\rm cert}-U_P\); terminal \(\rho_n\) only parameterizes the auxiliary \(\ell_n\) in the parent matrix representation and is not used here.

Applying the physical-index inequalities to the actual gradients gives

\[
U_B\le U_A,
\qquad
G_B:=\sum_{i<n}u_i^Bg_i\le G_A+d_*N_-,
\tag{6.3}
\]

where \(N_-:=\sum_{i<n}h_i\max(-g_i,0)\) is the physical negative-gradient mass. The exact displacement identity is \(\sum_{i<n}h_i g_i=X_m-x_n\), so

\[
N_-=
\frac{\sum_{i<n}h_i|g_i|-\sum_{i<n}h_i g_i}{2}
\le\frac{S-X_m+x_n}{2}.
\tag{6.4}
\]

For odd \(m\), \(x_n=-q\), and in particular

\[
\limsup_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}\frac{N_-}{S}
\le\frac{1-r}{2}.
\tag{6.5}
\]

Define \(M_B=u_n^Bq-G_B\). Equations (6.3) give the finite-depth inequality

\[
M_B\ge M_A-d_*N_-.
\tag{6.6}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\liminf_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}\frac{M_B}{S}
\ge \eta_A-\frac{r^2(1-r)}4
>\frac{207}{6800}>\frac3{100}.
\tag{6.7}
\]

For the last strict inequality, \(r>2/5\) and \(r^2=3-2\sqrt2<1/5\) give \(r^2(1-r)/4<3/100\), while (5.8) gives \(\eta_A>411/6800\). Thus \(M_B>0\) for every sufficiently large odd enclosing \(m\), with the \(S+1\) in the fixed profile fully retained. The parent independently identified the physical-index restriction used in (6.2).

## 7. The actual original-radius residual is negative

For either profile, (2.3) and (3.9) reduce its *given* doubled residual exactly to

\[
D_{u^P}=X_m^2-B_{\rm cert}q^2
-\left(X_m-G_P-u_n^Pg_n\right)^2.
\tag{7.1}
\]

When \(m\) is odd, \(g_n=-q\), and hence

\[
X_m-G_P-u_n^Pg_n=X_m+M_P.
\]

For every sufficiently large odd \(m\), Sections 5 and 6 give \(X_m>0\) and \(M_P>0\). Therefore

\[
\boxed{\quad D_{u^P}=-2X_mM_P-M_P^2-B_{\rm cert}q^2<0,
\qquad P=A,B.\quad}
\tag{7.2}
\]

For A the normalized limiting residual is explicitly

\[
\frac{D_{u^A}}{S^2}\longrightarrow
r^2-(r+\eta_A)^2<0
\qquad(m\to\infty\text{ through odd integers}).
\tag{7.3}
\]

For B, (6.7) already implies the strict bound

\[
\limsup_{m\to\infty,\ m\ {\rm odd}}
\frac{D_{u^B}}{S^2}
\le r^2-\left(r+\frac3{100}\right)^2<0.
\tag{7.4}
\]

An explicit numerical depth threshold is unnecessary for this all-sufficiently-large-odd-depth counterexample: the displayed exact formulas and uniform geometric tail estimates prove existence of a finite threshold. No finite computation or freely assigned Gram direction supplies the sign.

## 8. Original payoff and exact scope

The actual witness value, with the original selected minimum and radius, is

\[
\frac{2(F(x_n)-F_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}
=\frac{q^2}{X_m^2},
\qquad
S_m^2\frac{q^2}{X_m^2}\longrightarrow1.
\tag{8.1}
\]

Thus this family rejects the two dominance decompositions because their large terminal weight produces an excessive residual square. Its original objective payoff is only order \(S_m^{-2}\). It does not supply a full-class worst-case upper certificate, a benchmark-scale lower witness, or a resolution of the original minimax comparison. In particular, failure of these \(D_u\) profiles does not imply failure of \(\mathcal W(H_m)\le1/B_{\rm cert}\).

The conclusion does rule out constructing a nonnegative-interpolation-plus-PSD proof of either fixed \(D_u\) on all original trajectories, provided the identities in the parent profile note are used without changing the profiles. On the actual vectors above, such a representation would have a nonnegative right side, contradicting (7.2). It says nothing about a different profile or a different upper-bound certificate.

## 9. Audit register and disposition

| Claim | Support in this draft | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Static convex 1-smooth function with unique selected minimum 0 | Explicit derivative in (2.1) | UNVERIFIED exact derivation |
| All physical \(H_m\) queries and the real endpoint | Positivity induction (3.3)--(3.8), explicit samples (3.10)--(3.11) | UNVERIFIED exact derivation |
| Literal enclosing-\(m\) asymptotics | Exact sums (3.2), (4.1), (4.3), uniform geometric tails after (5.5) | UNVERIFIED exact derivation |
| Strict A-profile failure on unbounded full-word depths | Rational margin (5.8), original residual (7.2) | UNVERIFIED proposed counterexample |
| Strict B-profile failure on the same full-word family | Exact physical-index comparison (6.2), negative-mass bound, margin (6.7) | UNVERIFIED proposed counterexample |
| Original benchmark attained or original minimax target resolved | No supporting argument; (8.1) has smaller scale | Not claimed; original target OPEN |

The first contemplated unit-quadratic and globally affine checks were only mental calibrations and supplied no result used above. Sections 1--9 use one substantive actual-function approach; Section 10 uses the second and final approach under the parent's later addendum. No mathematical programs or formal submissions ran. No service, worker, shared state, Goal, truth store, operator profile, task, budget file, or other research packet was modified.

The parent was notified promptly after the actual all-depth obstruction and both profile margins were derived. This bounded call stops after saving and hashing this proof and its handoff.

## 10. Second approach: a binding triangular body, with Profile C unresolved

The parent subsequently fixed Profile C in the complete addendum
R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md, whose checked SHA-256 is
9001df1d3b8ecdbc198d8be2e85657610263fc93fdf8fd50f3e295f058e58030.
Its new physical profile is \(\rho_i^C=1-T_i/(8S)\), with the same \(B_{\rm cert}\) and terminal mass rule. The parent authorized only the previously unused second approach. The addendum already establishes that the critical orbit above does not reject C; its re-evaluation is not counted as a different approach.

The distinct geometry attempted here is the single compact triangle

\[
v_0=(1,0),\quad v_1=(-1/2,\sqrt3/2),\quad
v_2=(-1/2,-\sqrt3/2),\qquad K=\operatorname{conv}\{v_0,v_1,v_2\}.
\]

Use the static function

\[
F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\|g\|^2/2\},
\qquad \nabla F_K(x)=P_K(x).
\tag{10.1}
\]

The maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection. Convexity follows from the supremum of affine functions of \(x\). Projection's variational inequalities give
\(\|P_K(x)-P_K(y)\|^2\le\langle P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y\rangle\), hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The usual upper and lower comparisons at the two maximizers give differentiability with the displayed gradient. Since 0 is interior to \(K\), every \(x\ne0\) has \(F_K(x)>0\) by testing a sufficiently small positive multiple of \(x\); thus the original minimizer is uniquely 0. Equation (2.3) again holds at every actual sample of this function.

Let \(R\) denote rotation by \(2\pi/3\). For one enclosing \(m\), let

\[
w_j=R^{m-j}v_0,\qquad
Z_1=\beta w_1,\qquad
Z_j=L_jw_j+Z_{j-1}\quad(2\le j\le m),
\tag{10.2}
\]

where \(L_j=S_j-S_{j-1}\) is the exact physical block mass used earlier. The actual candidate start is \(x_0=Z_m\), selected before the forward run on the fixed body \(K\). Its original radius is \(\|Z_m\|^2\).

The proposed cyclic policy would keep projection \(w_j\) throughout the first child and bridge of suffix \(H_j\), and then enter the next suffix at \(Z_{j-1}\). It is a policy about actual vertices of one triangle, not independent scalar components. Crucially, it requires the bridge-query normal

\[
n_j=Z_{j-1}+k_{j-1}w_j
\]

to belong to \(N_K(w_j)\). This condition is necessary because the proposed bridge query is \(Z_{j-1}+(1+k_{j-1})w_j\). It is also sufficient for all earlier queries in that block, since their normals add nonnegative multiples of \(w_j\), and
\(\langle w_j,v-w_j\rangle\le0\) for all \(v\in K\).

This normal-cone condition fails at the root at an order-one scale. Put \(r=\sqrt2-1\), and represent the plane as the complex plane with \(v_0=1\), \(Rv_0=z=-1/2+i\sqrt3/2\). The same geometric-tail argument as in Section 5, now for the vector sum (10.2), gives

\[
\frac{Z_{m-1}}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow
(1-r)\frac{z}{1-rz},\qquad
\frac{k_{m-1}}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow r.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\frac{n_m}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow
\nu:=r+(1-r)\frac{z}{1-rz}
=\frac{r(1+z)}{1-rz}
=\frac{r}{1+r+r^2}
\left(\frac{1-r}{2},\frac{\sqrt3(1+r)}{2}\right).
\tag{10.3}
\]

Here \(1-r-r^2=r\) was used. The normal row toward the next vertex has the strictly wrong sign:

\[
\left\langle\nu,v_1-v_0\right\rangle
=\frac{3r^2}{2(1+r+r^2)}>0.
\tag{10.4}
\]

Thus the proposed cyclic vertex table cannot be the actual complete GD trajectory for all sufficiently large enclosing \(m\). This is a geometric feasibility failure, not a negative value of \(D_{u^C}\).

The first binding event in the genuine forward run can also be located, without replacing any gradients. While the projection stays \(v_0\), the exact queries are \(Z_m-T_i v_0\). The supporting row for \(v_1\) first leaves the vertex normal cone when

\[
T_i>\tau_m:=-\frac23\langle Z_m,v_1-v_0\rangle-1
=S_{m-1}+k_{m-1}-\frac23\langle Z_{m-1},v_1-v_0\rangle.
\tag{10.5}
\]

Its normalized position satisfies

\[
\frac{\tau_m}{S_{m-1}}\longrightarrow
1-\frac{r^2}{1+r+r^2}\in(0,1).
\tag{10.6}
\]

For sufficiently large \(m\), the start has a positive vertical component; the competing \(v_2\) row crosses later than the \(v_1\) row. Also \(\tau_m>0\). It follows that the actual path agrees with the \(v_0\) policy up to the first physical index with \(T_i>\tau_m\), and at that actual query its projection differs from \(v_0\). Such a physical index exists no later than the root bridge query, whose cumulative mass is \(S_{m-1}>\tau_m\). Equality \(T_i=\tau_m\) still has projection \(v_0\). The path therefore encounters a genuine binding change involving another edge or vertex inside the first child or at its endpoint.

The subsequent actual projection path was not solved in this bounded approach. No claimed terminal vector, original-radius value, or sign of \(D_{u^C}\) is inferred from the rejected cyclic table. This second approach supplies a precise obstruction to that simple triangular inverse construction, while Profile C remains unresolved. Its outcome does not alter the complete A/B counterexample in Sections 1--8. Both substantive approach slots are now used, and no continuation or additional model is authorized by this packet.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001580 -->
# R13 handoff: fixed increasing profile remains OPEN

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Grant 9cb17568ad79648c. Original Goal OPEN.** One authorized invocation and two analytical approaches are complete. No full PSD certificate and no actual negative-D_u trajectory were obtained. Read the complete PROOF_ATTEMPT.md before using either result below.

The original target remains `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1`, or a certified full-class asymptotic separation, with W=2R and the exact all-integer Zhang--Jiang denominator B_n. Preserve every finite dimension, original selected minimizer/radius, actual endpoint, predetermined nonnegative schedules, and the SAME enclosing parameters throughout each H_m. This packet does not resolve that target.

## Fixed data

For each enclosing m>=1536:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), k_j=a c^j,
    s_j=sum H_j, S=s_m, T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,
    Bcert=1+(32/17)S,
    u_i=(1+T_i/(2S))h_i (i<n),
    u_n=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i.

No weights were retuned. The nonnegative Q convention, actual Bmat, original-radius dominance functional D_u, and FULL remainder R are those in TASK.md.

## Result 1: the positive interval cone is excluded at these new weights

This is a new derivation; the old b_i=h_i result was not reused outside its hypotheses. It covers arbitrary finite positive sums of the explicit variable-step interval modes, including m-dependent coefficients and supports. Those modes have diagonal `H=C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T`. It does NOT cover every general C or every possible diagonal-H C.

Let `delta_i=u_i-h_i`, and use the mode quantities q_i, B_i^nu, E_i^nu defined in the proof. The FULL diagonal is

    R_ii=q_i-(h_i-1)^2 q_(i+1)
         -delta_i[2(h_i-1)+delta_i].

The exact generalized prefix identity is

    q_k+sum_(i<k)R_ii
      =(T_k+1)^2-2sum_nu B_k^nu E_k^nu+Xi_k,

where, writing s=T_k and V, W3, K2 for the physical prefix sums of h_i^2, h_i^3, and h_i^2 T_i^2,

    Xi_k=[(s+1)(s^2-V)/2-(s^3-W3)/3]/S-K2/(4S^2)
         <=s^3/(6S)+s^2/(2S).

At the middle bridge, `s=s_(m-1)`, `K=k_(m-1)`, `S=2s+1+K`, and `delta_k=s(1+K)/(2S)`. The positive-mode cut equations and FULL PSD necessity imply

    K^2[1+2(s+1+K)/(s+1-K)]+2K delta_k
      <=(s+1)^2+s^3/(6S)+s^2/(2S).

With r=sqrt(2)-1, the literal enclosing-m limit forces `1+r^3<=1+r/6`, impossible because `6r^2>1` (equivalently `289>288`). Thus this entire cone fails for all sufficiently large inherited m. This already uses the full diagonal after Laplacian cancellation, so retaining signed diagonal terms and their joint control cannot repair a member of that cone.

A free matrix direction/family exclusion is not a realizable-function counterexample. General nonseparable circulations remain open. No actual column-matching C with PSD R was constructed.

## Result 2: broad actual Huber inner phases all pass

For the fixed scalar Huber function with gradient clip[-1,1] and original minimizer 0, the ACTUAL map obeys

    T_(H_j)([-k_j,k_j]) subset [-a beta,a beta]

at every inherited depth j. The proof uses the exact image-radius update, the real physical word, and c<3.

Set J=floor(m/2), `d_j=k_(j+1)-(s_j+1)>0`. Choose any

    Y_J in [max(0,k_J-d_J),k_J],
    Y_(j+1)=s_j+1+k_j-Y_j  for j>=J.

Execute all of H_J from Y_J without prescribing its inner itinerary. Every higher left child and bridge is then actually saturated, lands at -Y_j, and recurses by oddness. All complete H_m trajectories so constructed have `|x_n|=|g_n|<=a beta` and Q_star,i=0.

Uniformly over these inner phases,

    Y_m/S -> r,
    M_m/S^2 -> v=r^2/(1+2sqrt(2)),
    Z/S=r+v/2+q g_n+o(1), q=43/68+v/4,
    Z=sum_i u_i g_i.

The exact original-radius residual is `D_u=2Y_m Z-Z^2-Bcert x_n^2`. With

    eta=r-v/2-q r
       =r[25/68-1/(4(1+2sqrt(2)))]>2r/17,

it satisfies the uniform positive bound

    liminf inf_(admissible inner phases) D_u/S^2
      >=eta(2r-eta)>0.

This includes both parities and an increasing-depth inner phase. It rejects the attempted Huber obstruction, not the fixed dominance profile. Other starts, other functions, and other dimensions are not covered. No stalled-minimizer witness was constructed; parent speculation about one is not used.

## Receipt and recovery boundary

- Invocations 1; analytical approaches 2; mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions 0 each; additional agents/models 0.
- Only PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md were written here. No shared administration, Goal mutation, TARGET, verification request, service/worker change, old grant reuse, publication, or finalization.
- New math remains UNVERIFIED. The source hashes were checked in PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. Final output hashes are returned to the parent after readback.
- No per-call token figure is exposed or invented. This grant has no remaining approach credit and must not renew itself.

The required full-class premise `W(H_m)<=1/Bcert` remains missing. A future continuation needs a genuinely different explicit C outside the excluded positive cone, a direct original-space proof, or an actual complete-word negative D_u trajectory. Merely presenting matrix feasibility conditions again would not close that premise. No original-target candidate is ready for formal review.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001581 -->
# R13 fixed increasing weights: two exact scope results, no full certificate

**UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Grant 9cb17568ad79648c. Original target OPEN.**

This packet does not construct an admissible general interpolation matrix with a PSD full remainder, and it does not produce an actual trajectory with negative prescribed dominance residual. The two authorized approaches give the following narrower results.

1. The whole positive cone of variable-step interval modes is excluded for the FIXED increasing physical weights, even when the Laplacian and signed diagonal terms are retained together. This is a new derivation with the actual columns `b_i=u_i`; the old result with `b_i=h_i` is not imported. The contradiction uses a necessary diagonal condition of the FULL remainder, not separately nonnegative diagonal slack after removing the Laplacian.
2. A substantial family of actual symmetric-Huber complete trajectories, allowing arbitrary admissible inner phases at depth `floor(m/2)`, has a uniformly positive residual of order `S^2`. Thus changing the inner phase of that saturated outer recursion cannot provide the requested actual obstruction. This is a calibration on a named family, not a full-class validity result.

The positive interval cone does not exhaust arbitrary admissible cyclic matrices. The first result therefore leaves genuinely nonseparable circulations open. The original minimax question and the fixed increasing dominance functional remain unresolved.

## 1. Sources, authorization, and locked notation

Read completely: OPERATOR.md; PROBLEM.md; R12_FINAL_HANDOFF_20261009.md; R12_EXCESS_WEIGHT_LAPLACIAN_LEAD_20261009.md; R12_PARENT_RANKONE_FLOW_OBSTRUCTION_20261009.md; R12_AB_COUNTEREXAMPLE_HANDOFF_20261009.md; brownian_dominance_certificate_20261009_r12/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md; profile_c_full_certificate_20261009_r12b/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md; and R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md, including its complete Section 5. The mathematical-academic-writing skill and its mathematical-integrity reference were used for evidence and quantifier discipline. The initialized marker is present. No private worker memory was read.

Checked input hashes:

- TASK.md: `abd19f8e86df1dcabcc1afe63cc014ea96ed2277831ee71ed9b4310a743de6e6`.
- R12_EXCESS_WEIGHT_LAPLACIAN_LEAD_20261009.md: `af20644806bbe030993835b5b4258e005a0fc0b30f64835ea68a6c51dd0e6593`.
- R12_PARENT_RANKONE_FLOW_OBSTRUCTION_20261009.md: `870d3f51efa4f7ddb01d6e2632885f578c920e5da7940ba32200d5de77e88eb8`.

The exact original question remains

    lim_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1,

or a rigorous disproof by a full-class asymptotic separation. Here `W=2R`, and `U_n^ZJ=1/B_n`, where `B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1` is the EXACT sequence with maximization over all integer splits from PROBLEM.md. The class includes every finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every original selected minimizer and distinct initial point, and the actual last iterate of every predetermined nonnegative schedule. Nothing below changes that class, radius, benchmark, or endpoint.

Fix ONE enclosing integer `m>=1536` and retain throughout every child

    c0=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=c0(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).

Write

    k_j=a c^j, s_j=sum H_j, n=2^m-1, h=H_m,
    S=s_m, T_i=sum_(t<i) h_t,
    Bcert=1+(32/17)S,
    u_i=(1+T_i/(2S))h_i for i<n,
    u_n=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i.

`Bcert` is not `B_n`. No physical step is added at query n. Put

    V=sum_(i<n) h_i^2,
    U=sum_(i<n)u_i=5S/4-V/(4S),
    u_n=1+(43/68)S+V/(4S)>0,
    b_i=u_i for i<n, b_n=-U.

Let Bmat have diagonal 1 and entries `(Bmat)_ij=h_j` for `j<i`. For normalized L=1 trajectories use the NONNEGATIVE rows

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
    Q_star,i=-2(f_i-f_star)+2<g_i,x_i-x_star>-||g_i||^2.

The unchanged functional and full remainder are

    D_u=||x_0-x_star||^2-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2Bcert(f_n-f_star)-||x_0-x_star-sum_i u_i g_i||^2,

    R=L+diag(u_i(1-u_i)+b_i-L_ii)
      -(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T),

    D_u=sum_(i,j) C_ji Q_ij+g^T R g.                 (1.1)

Here C has zero row sums, nonnegative off-diagonal entries, and column sums b. The Laplacian has weights `w_ij=u_j(u_i-h_i)` for `i<j`. Its diagonal cancels from the full diagonal in (1.1); in particular, if `H=C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T`, then

    R_ii=u_i(2-u_i)-H_ii,       i<n.                (1.2)

Equation (1.2) is a necessary PSD test regardless of signs in any decomposition of R.

## 2. Approach 1: exclude the positive interval-mode cone at the actual weights

### 2.1 Explicit index-dependent modes and the precise family

For each atom nu, let `A_i^nu` be nonnegative and nondecreasing, with `A_-1^nu=0`, and let `D_i^nu` be nonnegative and nonincreasing, with `D_(n+1)^nu=0`. Interval-supported atoms are included by extending their cumulative weights by zero or constants outside their support. Absorb a nonnegative combination coefficient into A. Set `r_i=h_i-1` at physical indices. For `i<j`, the actual entries of an atom are

    C_ji^nu=(A_i^nu-A_(i-1)^nu)(D_j^nu+r_j D_(j+1)^nu),
    C_ij^nu=(A_(i-1)^nu+r_i A_i^nu)(D_j^nu-D_(j+1)^nu).

At j=n the dummy r_n multiplies zero. Diagonal entries make row sums zero. All off-diagonal entries are nonnegative. The sum `C=sum_nu C^nu` is an actual, generally index-dependent cyclic coefficient family, although no feasible member with the target columns and PSD remainder is asserted.

Use any finite number of atoms, with arbitrary m-dependent coefficients and supports. This includes positive sums with different interval destinations and does not assume one rank-one destination law.

For this family the symmetric product H is diagonal. Define

    Z_i=sum_nu A_i^nu D_(i+1)^nu,
    q_i=1+2 sum_nu A_(i-1)^nu D_i^nu,
    B_i^nu=sum_(t<i) h_t A_t^nu,
    E_i^nu=sum_(t>=i,t<n) h_t D_(t+1)^nu.

Thus `q_(i+1)=1+2Z_i`, and direct multiplication with the ACTUAL Bmat gives

    H_ii=2[r_i^2 Z_i-sum_nu A_(i-1)^nu D_i^nu].    (2.1)

The off-diagonal cancellation is the interval-mode identity in the source; it follows by writing the upper entry of `C Bmat` as the product of the corresponding forward cumulative factor and backward cumulative factor, and the lower entry as its negative.

### 2.2 The new column equations and full diagonal recurrence

Let `U_i=sum_(t<=i)u_t`, for physical i. The required column sums give

    sum_nu[A_i^nu E_i^nu-D_(i+1)^nu B_i^nu
                       -2r_i A_i^nu D_(i+1)^nu]=U_i. (2.2)

Here is a flow check of (2.2). Across the cut after i, an atom's forward outgoing mass is

    D_(i+1)^nu [B_i^nu+r_i A_i^nu],

and its backward incoming mass is

    A_i^nu [D_(i+1)^nu+E_(i+1)^nu].

Subtract the first from the second and use
`E_i^nu=h_i D_(i+1)^nu+E_(i+1)^nu`. Summing atoms gives the net incoming mass U_i. No individual atom is required to have the target columns.

Put

    delta_i=u_i-h_i=h_i T_i/(2S),
    e_i=U_i-(T_i+h_i)=sum_(t<=i)delta_t.

Combining (1.2) and (2.1) yields the FULL physical diagonal

    R_ii=q_i-r_i^2 q_(i+1)-delta_i(2r_i+delta_i).   (2.3)

The last term is essential. Increasing u changes the physical diagonal as well as the cut equations.

### 2.3 Generalized prefix identity, including the entire correction

For a physical cut k define

    Xi_k=sum_(i<k)[2h_i e_i-delta_i(2r_i+delta_i)].

Then the exact identity is

    q_k+sum_(i<k)R_ii
      =(T_k+1)^2-2sum_nu B_k^nu E_k^nu+Xi_k.       (2.4)

To verify it, use

    B_(i+1)^nu=B_i^nu+h_i A_i^nu,
    E_(i+1)^nu=E_i^nu-h_i D_(i+1)^nu.

Equation (2.2) gives

    sum_nu(B_(i+1)^nu E_(i+1)^nu-B_i^nu E_i^nu)
      =h_i U_i+h_i(h_i-2)Z_i.

Therefore the increment of the right side of (2.4) before adding Xi is

    (1-r_i^2)q_(i+1)-2h_i e_i.

Adding the increment of Xi gives `(1-r_i^2)q_(i+1)-delta_i(2r_i+delta_i)`, which equals the increment of the left side by (2.3). Both sides initially equal 1. This proves (2.4) using only the combined column equations.

For a prefix with total physical mass `s=T_k`, set

    V_<k=sum_(i<k)h_i^2,
    W_<k=sum_(i<k)h_i^3,
    K_<k=sum_(i<k)h_i^2 T_i^2.

The FIXED increasing clock makes the correction explicit:

    Xi_k=[(s+1)(s^2-V_<k)/2-(s^3-W_<k)/3]/S
         -K_<k/(4S^2).                             (2.5)

For completeness, interchanging finite sums gives

    2sum_(i<k)h_i e_i
      =(1/S)sum_(i<k)h_i T_i(s-T_i).

Subtracting `sum delta_i(2r_i+delta_i)` leaves

    (1/S)sum_(i<k)h_i T_i(s+1-T_i-h_i)
      -K_<k/(4S^2).

Now use `sum h_i T_i=(s^2-V_<k)/2` and
`sum h_i T_i(T_i+h_i)=(s^3-W_<k)/3`. These prove (2.5) exactly, without a continuous-clock approximation.

Since `W_<k<=s V_<k` and `K_<k>=0`, (2.5) implies

    Xi_k<=s^3/(6S)+s^2/(2S).                        (2.6)

If the FULL R is PSD, each R_ii is nonnegative. Also `B_k^nu E_k^nu>=0`. Consequently (2.4) gives

    q_k<=(T_k+1)^2+Xi_k.                            (2.7)

No separate nonnegativity of `R-L`, of the signed diagonal in (1.1), or of an invented slack variable has been assumed.

### 2.4 Central cut contradiction

At the central query `k=2^(m-1)-1`, put

    s=s_(m-1), K=k_(m-1), h_k=1+K, S=2s+1+K.

The right physical suffix has total mass s. In each atom, monotonicity of D gives

    E_(k+1)^nu<=s D_(k+1)^nu.

Separating the bridge in (2.2) therefore gives

    U_k<= (s+1-K) Z_k.                              (2.8)

The omitted term `-sum D_(k+1)^nu B_k^nu` is nonpositive. Since `U_k>=s+1+K>0`, existence would force `s+1-K>0`, and

    q_(k+1)>=1+2(s+1+K)/(s+1-K).                   (2.9)

This lower bound deliberately uses only `u_i>=h_i`; retaining the full larger U_k would strengthen it.

At this bridge `delta_k=s(1+K)/(2S)`. Equations (2.3), (2.6), (2.7), and (2.9) imply the following necessary condition for the FULL R to be PSD:

    K^2[1+2(s+1+K)/(s+1-K)]+2K delta_k
      <=(s+1)^2+s^3/(6S)+s^2/(2S).                 (2.10)

The nonnegative `delta_k^2` has only been dropped from the left side.

Let `r=sqrt(2)-1`. The exact inherited-word sum is

    alpha=a/(c-2),
    s_j+1=alpha c^j+(1-alpha)2^j.                   (2.11)

Here alpha tends to 1 and is uniformly positive; `2/c<=2/c0<1`. Along the actual enclosing m, (2.11) gives

    K/s -> r, S/s -> c0=1/r,
    delta_k/s -> r^2/2, s -> infinity.

Divide (2.10) by s^2. Its limiting necessary condition is

    r^2[1+2(1+r)/(1-r)]+r^3 <= 1+r/6.

The identity `r^2+2r=1` makes the bracketed first contribution exactly 1. Thus it would require

    r^3<=r/6.

This is false: `6r^2>1` is equivalent to `17>12sqrt(2)`, and both sides are positive with squares `289>288`. The limiting contradiction has a strict constant gap. All quantities in (2.10) depend only on the literal word and prescribed weights, not on the atoms. Hence every finite positive interval-mode combination fails the full PSD requirement for all sufficiently large enclosing m, even if the number, supports, and weights of the atoms vary arbitrarily with m.

This proves a family obstruction. It does not prove that every admissible C has diagonal H, or that every nonnegative C with diagonal H belongs to this positive cone. Neither assertion is used. General C, and diagonal-H matrices outside this cone, remain unexcluded.

## 3. Approach 2: actual Huber inner-phase variation cannot reject the profile

### 3.1 A complete interval-image bound for the physical word

Use the SAME static function at every query,

    f(x)=x^2/2 if |x|<=1, and |x|-1/2 otherwise,
    g(x)=clip(x,[-1,1]), x_star=0.

It is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, with unique original minimizer 0, and `Q_star,i=0` at every real query. Every conclusion in this section concerns this function only.

For `h>1`, the image of `[-R,R]` under `x -> x-h clip(x,[-1,1])` is the interval of radius

    Phi_h(R)=(h-1)R,                     0<=R<=1,
    Phi_h(R)=max(R-h,h-1),               R>=1.       (3.1)

This follows from continuity, oddness, and the three affine pieces of the update. In particular it is a statement about actual images, not a freely selected gradient table. For all R>=0,

    Phi_h(R)<=max(R-h,h-1).                         (3.2)

Define `t=a beta`. For every inherited depth j>=1,

    T_(H_j)([-k_j,k_j]) subset [-t,t].              (3.3)

Proof: at j=1, `k_1=beta<1`, so the one step `1+a` gives radius `a beta`. For j>=2, iterate (3.2) through the left H_(j-1). Its output radius is at most

    max(k_j-s_(j-1), k_(j-2)).

To see this, expansion of the scalar max recurrence subtracts the suffix mass from each reset value; each reset value is at most the largest `h-1` in that child, namely k_(j-2).

Both terms are at most `1+2k_(j-1)`. The only nontrivial inequality is

    k_j-s_(j-1)<=1+2k_(j-1),

equivalently `(c-2)k_(j-1)<=s_(j-1)+1`. Formula (2.11) proves it because `0<c-2<1`, so

    s_(j-1)+1>=k_(j-1)/(c-2)>=(c-2)k_(j-1).

Here `c<3` holds throughout the stated parameter range. The bridge of length `1+k_(j-1)` maps every interval of radius at most `1+2k_(j-1)` into `[-k_(j-1),k_(j-1)]`, by (3.1). The induction hypothesis on the final child then proves (3.3). In particular t<1, so every endpoint in (3.3) lies in the actual quadratic region.

### 3.2 Many admissible inner phases and exact outer execution

Set `J=floor(m/2)`. Define

    d_j=k_(j+1)-(s_j+1)
       =(beta-alpha)c^j-(1-alpha)2^j.

For the actual parameters, `0<alpha<1`,

    d_1=epsilon[2/(c0(1+epsilon))-c0 epsilon]>0,
    d_(j+1)=c d_j+(c-2)(1-alpha)2^j>d_j.            (3.4)

Choose ANY base start

    Y_J in [max(0,k_J-d_J), k_J].                   (3.5)

Execute the full physical H_J from that Y_J; no inner itinerary is prescribed. For j>=J define the outer starts by

    Y_(j+1)=s_j+1+k_j-Y_j.                          (3.6)

Let `e_j=k_j-Y_j`. At the base, `0<=e_J<=d_J`, and (3.6) gives

    Y_(j+1)=s_j+1+e_j,
    e_(j+1)=d_j-e_j.

Using (3.4), induction gives `0<=e_j<=d_j` at every later level. Consequently, at level j+1 the entire left H_j and bridge have actual gradient +1; the bridge query is `1+e_j`, and the bridge lands exactly at `-Y_j`. The remaining child is the negative of the complete H_j trajectory by oddness. This constructs every one of the complete H_m trajectories in (3.5)-(3.6), retaining every physical update and the original minimizer.

By (3.3), regardless of its inner phase, the endpoint satisfies

    |x_n|=|g_n|<=t.                                (3.7)

In particular the endpoint is not replaced by an arbitrary gradient of magnitude 1. Such a replacement would be infeasible for this family.

### 3.3 Uniform moments at the actual enclosing depth

Let the reference critical starts be

    X_1=beta,
    X_(j+1)=s_j+1+k_j-X_j.

The source's exact trajectory proof gives `0<X_j<=k_j`; it also follows from (3.4) with `k_1-X_1=0`. Put

    r=sqrt(2)-1,
    v=r^2/(1+2sqrt(2)).

The needed reference limits are

    X_m/S -> r,
    V_m/S^2 -> v,
    M_m^X/S^2 -> v,                                (3.8)

where `V_j=sum_(H_j) h_i^2` and `M_j^X=sum_(H_j)h_i x_(i+1)` along the reference trajectory. These are limits with the SAME enclosing parameters in all children.

For a direct check of their uniformity, write

    d(c)=(c-1)/(c+1),
    P=alpha c d(c), Q=2(1-alpha)/3, R0=beta-P-Q.

The exact finite-depth formulas are

    X_j=P c^(j-1)+Q 2^(j-1)+R0(-1)^(j-1),
    V_j=(2^j-1)+2alpha(c^j-2^j)
        +a^2(c^(2j)-2^j)/(c^2-2).                  (3.9)

The moment satisfies

    M_(j+1)^X+M_j^X
      =s_j X_(j+1)-(s_j^2+V_j)/2-(1+k_j)X_j.      (3.10)

The left-child term is the sum of `h_i(X_(j+1)-T_i-h_i)`, the bridge term is `-(1+k_j)X_j`, and the right child contributes `-M_j^X`. With

    v(c)=(c-2)^2/(c^2-2),
    w(c)=[2d(c)-(1+v(c))/2]/(c^2+1),

(2.11), (3.9), and (3.10) give

    X_j=d(c)alpha c^j+O(2^j),
    V_j=v(c)alpha^2 c^(2j)+O(c^j+2^j),
    M_j^X=w(c)alpha^2 c^(2j)+O((2c)^j).

All constants are uniform for c in the compact interval
`[c0,c0(1+1/6144)]`. For the last estimate, subtract its stated leading term from (3.10); the resulting error plus its predecessor is `O((2c)^j)`, whose finite geometric sum has the same order uniformly. Finally `d(c0)=r`, `v(c0)=v`, and `w(c0)=v`. This proves (3.8) without exchanging a fixed-depth and enclosing-depth limit.

Now compare the starts (3.5)-(3.6) with X. For j>=J,

    Y_j-X_j=(-1)^(j-J)(Y_J-X_J),
    |Y_J-X_J|<=k_J.

The moment recurrence (3.10) also holds for the Y trajectories at every outer level. Every query of its base H_J has absolute value at most k_J: (3.1) cannot increase the radius beyond the larger of its previous value and the maximum `h-1`, and `k_J>1`. Thus both base moments have absolute value at most `s_J k_J`. Subtracting the outer recurrences gives

    |M_m^Y-M_m^X|=O(k_J S),
    |Y_m-X_m|<=k_J,

uniformly for all choices in (3.5). Since `k_J/S=O(c^(J-m)) ->0`, (3.8) therefore strengthens to

    Y_m/S -> r, M_m^Y/S^2 -> v                    (3.11)

UNIFORMLY over the entire family of chosen inner phases. The physical V_m is independent of the phase and retains (3.8).

### 3.4 Strictly positive residual for every such complete phase

Let `Z=sum_(i=0)^n u_i g_i`. Finite summation by parts on the actual recursion gives

    sum_(i<n)u_i g_i
      =Y_m-(3/2)x_n+M_m^Y/(2S).                    (3.12)

For example, `sum T_i h_i g_i=M_m^Y-S x_n`, which proves (3.12) directly. This uses the last physical step in M and introduces no terminal update.

Set

    q=43/68+v/4.

The exact mass identity in Section 1, (3.7), and the uniform limits (3.11) imply

    Z/S=r+v/2+q g_n+o(1), |g_n|<=t -> r,          (3.13)

where the error is uniform over (3.5). The terminal gradient need not converge; its absolute bound is enough. Since q>0,

    limsup |Z/S-r| <= v/2+q r < r.                (3.14)

The strict inequality has an exact margin. Indeed

    eta=r-v/2-q r
       =25r/68-v(r+2)/4
       =r[25/68-1/(4(1+2sqrt(2)))]
       >2r/17>0,                                 (3.15)

using `r(r+2)=1`.

Here every Q_star,i=0 and the endpoint lies in the quadratic region, so the ORIGINAL functional is exactly

    D_u=2Y_m Z-Z^2-Bcert x_n^2.                    (3.16)

Equations (3.7), (3.11), and (3.14) imply the uniform lower bound

    liminf_(m->infinity) inf_(Y_J in (3.5)) D_u/S^2
      >= r^2-(v/2+q r)^2
       =eta(2r-eta)>0.                             (3.17)

The notation `Y_J in (3.5)` means in the closed interval specified there, not an open interval. The term `Bcert x_n^2/S^2` tends uniformly to zero because the terminal point is bounded. Both parities of m, all admissible inner phases, and the original radius Y_m^2 are included.

Thus this attempted actual-function obstruction fails decisively on its stated complete-word family. It neither proves D_u>=0 on other Huber starts nor establishes any inequality for other functions or dimensions.

## 4. Scope and the remaining exact gap

The matrix approach proves more than failure of one rank-one destination law, but less than failure of every admissible cyclic matrix. Its family is the finite positive cone of the explicitly displayed interval modes. All these matrices have diagonal H. The contradiction concerns the full diagonal R_kk and therefore cannot be evaded inside that family by using the Laplacian to compensate a signed diagonal. Matrices outside the family were not excluded.

The actual-function approach constructs all the claimed physical trajectories and proves positive, rather than negative, D_u on them. It supplies no full-function counterexample. No negative coordinate direction in Section 2 is represented as an actual gradient trajectory.

The parent mentioned an optional fixed-prefix trajectory that could stall at another minimizer; no such trajectory was constructed here. A later parent message reports a pending coercivity argument against fixed-prefix versions near exact Silver. Neither speculation is used in a proof in this packet. No claim about growing-depth stalled trajectories is made.

The missing original-target premise remains an actual original-space certificate valid for the full class:

    W(H_m)<=1/Bcert<=17/[16(1+2S)].

If that premise were proved, the supplied accepted benchmark-comparison fact 931b9653ce0f7201 would give the intended strict 799/800 separation. Neither approach here establishes it. There is no complete original-target object for a finished-object adversarial audit or formal submission.

## 5. Resource and stop record

- Fresh native research invocations: 1, this grant only; requested model/effort gpt-6-astra / ultra.
- Substantive analytical approaches: 2: the positive interval-cone full-matrix test at the fixed increasing weights; the actual symmetric-Huber inner-phase attempt.
- Mathematical programs, generated mathematical arrays, solvers, formal submissions: 0 each.
- Additional agents/models, service or worker changes, old grants reused, truth-store or Goal changes: 0 each.
- Writes: PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md in this packet only. Reads, text searches, hashes, and output readback are administrative file operations.
- One attempted R12b source path, `profile_c_cyclic_certificate_20261009_r12b/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`, returned `Cannot find path ... because it does not exist.` A bounded filename search located `profile_c_full_certificate_20261009_r12b/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`, which was then read completely. No success is attributed to the failed read.
- Parent messages were coordination within the existing invocation. No exact per-call token receipt is exposed, and none is invented.

The bounded two-approach grant is exhausted. Stop after the two output readbacks and final hashes. No coefficient replacement, new model, mathematical execution, formal request, or automatic renewal is authorized by this packet.

# R10 full actual right-gap handoff

**UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN; all-depth upper closure not obtained.**

Allocation `94fa373cf827421a` used one native invocation and two substantive analytical approaches. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, further agents and further model calls: zero. Parent owns the ledger; this invocation stops after delivery.

Read the complete `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`. The same enclosing `m>=1536`, `epsilon=1/(4m)`, `beta=1-epsilon`, `c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon)`, `a=beta/c`, and `H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j)` are used throughout. Original target remains `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1`, with `W=2R`, or a certified full-function asymptotic separation with the original selected minimizer/radius and actual last iterate. The accepted `47/50` benchmark comparison still needs its full-class upper premise.

## First approach: actual leaf reserve and a paid right normal

For a child of mass s, with `D=d+2kappa s`, define

    r=P(w/D), q=P(y/d), nR=w-Dr,
    zR=[w-y+(D-s)q]/D, uR=r-zR,
    RR=Rem(w,D,zR), BR=Rem(w,D,q),
    ER=U_D(w)-U_d(y).

The actual convex weights give the exact row identity

    RR = D||uR||^2
       + (2/D) sum_right h_i <nR,r-g_i>
       + 2(D-s)/D <nR,r-q>.

Every row is nonnegative. Hence `<nR,r-q> <= D RR/[2(D-s)]`. This corrects the idea that the R9 normal has no available quantitative bound. It does not control `q-r` by RR; the R9 interval counterexample remains valid.

For the actual inherited physical leaf `h=1+a`, every compact convex body containing zero, every start, every `d>=1`, and every `16/17<=kappa<=1`, the new derivation proves

    E >= R,  E >= B/300,
    E >= eta R + (1-eta) B/300  for 0<=eta<=1.

One concrete all-body base is `(eta,gamma)=(3/4,1/1200)`. The proof keeps the two actual leaf projection rows, obtains an exact positive decomposition of `||x||^2-||y+hq||^2`, and handles `d>=2` and `1<=d<=2` with explicit inequalities. It is not a finite-body numerical check.

In the R9 parent, let `M=D+2kappa h`, `sigma=2kappa-1`, and suppose the right child satisfies `ER>=eta RR+gamma BR`. Then the right normal terms in a parent retention of the same eta are actually paid:

    M ER - 2 eta D<nR,uR> - 2 eta sigma h<nR,r-q>
      >= eta M D||uR||^2 + gamma M D||q-r||^2
       + (4 eta kappa h/D) sum_right h_i<nR,r-g_i>
       + (2 eta h d/D + 2 gamma M)<nR,r-q>.

The cancellation is `(M-D)(D-s)/D-sigma h=hd/D`. No independent normal value is introduced.

The remaining left normal is substantive. Using the exact identity

    nR=nL+(alpha-b)p+A u-D r,

the coefficient of `<nL,u>` after inserting child fraction eta and subtracting parent fraction eta' is

    2A[eta-1+(1-eta')theta].

Moreover

    A<nL,u> = sum_left h_i<nL,ell-g_i> + alpha<nL,ell-p>.

The boundary reserve BL separately controls only the terminal row in this fan. The direct termwise allocation therefore needs

    1-eta' >= (1-eta)/theta.

At kappa16/17, feasible theta is below3/4 for every j>=2. The concrete eta3/4 base consequently cannot propagate through arbitrary depth under this allocation: five such joins make the required loss exceed one. This is a failure of the stated allocation, not a proof that richer correlated-row payments or the two-remainder inequality itself are impossible.

The proof also retains the complete exact right gap:

    ER = D||r||^2-(D-2delta s)||q||^2-2D<uR,q>+2<nR,r-q>.

For theta0 and proposed full canonical retention, conditional on the actually unproved higher-child `ER>=RR`, its exact use yields the unpaid interface

    M(Psi-Rall) >= A(EL-RL)+J+h ER+h(D+h)||r||^2
                   -sigma h(D-2delta s+sigma h)||q||^2
                   -2delta A s||p||^2.

The normal cancels; the remaining actual terminal-norm payment was not proved. This expression is not offered as a completed certificate or another unproved PSD theorem.

## Second approach: scaled physical-displacement comparison actually fails

To reduce the left terminal weight, try

    z_lambda=[lambda(x-y)+(M-lambda S)q]/M,
    1<lambda<=2kappa.

It is feasible. The parent theta1 mixture has weights

    A ell + (lambda h-A)p + (M-lambda h)r,

so it avoids the old negative bridge coefficient when `lambda h>=A`. But the desired full retention `E>=Rem(x,M,z_lambda)` is false on actual leaves.

For every inherited leaf h=1+a, take

    kappa16/17, lambda3/2, d16,
    q=(1,0), g=(5/4,1), K=conv{0,q,g},
    T=16+(5/4)(h-1), x=g+T(1,-1/4), y=x-hg.

The projection normals prove `g=P(x)` and `q=P(y/16)` exactly. All comparison weights are positive. With `M=16+(32/17)h`,

    M[E-Rem(x,M,z_(3/2))]/h
      = -9M/68+(252-153h)/64 < -621/1088 <0.

The full U gap itself is positive on this same run:

    E=(16+5h/4)^2/(16+32h/17)-16 >0.

Therefore this is a counterexample to the fully retained repair, not to the U law. The same triangle construction with `g=(1+z,1)`, `z>(1-kappa)/(lambda-1)`, disproves the fixed-lambda fully retained law for every lambda>1 at sufficiently large trailing d. A d-dependent lambda, partial fraction or richer potential was not proved to compose.

## Exact remaining scope

There is no all-depth recurrence reaching final kappa at least16/17, no full W upper certificate on H_m, no original-target resolution and no complete candidate for formal verification. The packet supplies an actual all-body leaf base, an actual right-normal payment, the precise left-fan loss in the attempted recursion, and an admissible counterexample to the alternative fixed-lambda full-retention invariant.

No private worker memory was read. No service/worker, Goal, truth store, TARGET, CURRENT_RUN, OPERATOR, other packet, publication or budget was changed. File writes are only this proof and handoff. Hashes are reported to the parent after read-back; no resource allowance carries forward.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001583 -->
# R10: actual full-gap reserves, normal payment, and a failed displacement repair

**All new mathematics in this packet is UNVERIFIED under Danus policy. The all-depth upper bound is not established. The original target remains OPEN.**

Allocation `94fa373cf827421a`: one native analytical invocation, at most two substantive approaches. Exactly two approaches were used. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents and additional model calls: zero. All derivations below were done analytically. The parent owns the resource ledger and shared administration.

The first approach obtains a genuine all-body leaf estimate retaining both the canonical maximization remainder and the endpoint comparison remainder. It also pays the two troublesome right-child normal terms in the R9 transport identity, using their actual convex weights. The selected two-remainder induction does not close: its remaining left initial-normal fan has an expanding loss of retained fraction under the direct termwise allocation. The second approach changes the comparison mixture by multiplying the physical displacement. A concrete actual two-dimensional leaf disproves the uniformly fully retained version needed by that repair. Neither failure is a counterexample to the desired U law.

## 1. Fixed target, parameters, and accepted inputs

The original problem is

\[
W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f(x_\star))}{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\qquad W_n^*=\inf_{h\in[0,\infty)^n}W_n(h),
\qquad U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}=\frac1{B_n},
\]

and asks whether `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1`, or for a certified full-function-class asymptotic separation. The supremum includes every finite dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every originally selected minimizer and distinct start, and the actual final GD iterate. The normalization is `W = 2R`.

Fix ONE enclosing integer `m >= 1536` and use, at every child,

\[
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\frac\beta c,
\qquad H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

Write `S_j = sum H_j`. No parameter is reset at a child. The physical leaf is `H_1 = (h)` with `h = 1+a`; in this regime

\[
\frac43<h<\sqrt2<\frac32. \tag{1.1}
\]

Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` gives

\[
\frac{B_{2^m-1}}{1+2S_m}<\frac{47}{50}.
\]

Thus a full-class bound `W(H_m) <= 17/[16(1+2S_m)]` for every sufficiently large m would give a strict `799/800` subsequence separation. Its upper-bound premise remains unproved.

Fix one arbitrary nonempty compact convex body `K` containing zero in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. All physical and auxiliary points below use this one body. Set

\[
P=P_K,\qquad
F(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\},
\qquad U_d(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{2\langle x,g\rangle-d\|g\|^2\}.
\]

Then `grad F = P`. For an actual sample `r = P(x/D)`, put `n = x-Dr`. Its normal property is

\[
\langle n,r-z\rangle\ge0\quad(z\in K).
\]

For any feasible `z in K`, define

`phi_D(x,z)=2<x,z>-D||z||^2` and

\[
\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,z)
=U_D(x)-2\langle x,z\rangle+D\|z\|^2
=D\|r-z\|^2+2\langle n,r-z\rangle. \tag{1.2}
\]

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4`, whose statement and complete Parts B/C were read, supplies the actual same-m H3 U law, the H1/H2 primitive certificates, and the finite-word supremum transfer. Higher primitive positivity is not assumed here.

For every selected minimizer `x_star in K^polar`, completion of the square gives

\[
U_D(x)\le\frac{\|x-x_\star\|^2}{D}. \tag{1.3}
\]

Consequently an all-body law

\[
U_1(T_{H_m}x)\le U_{1+(32/17)S_m}(x)
\]

would give the required full W bound, since

\[
\frac1{1+(32/17)S_m}\le\frac{17}{16(1+2S_m)}.
\]

This implication keeps the original selected minimizer and radius. No such all-depth premise is proved below.

## 2. Approach 1: retain the actual endpoint gap and pay the actual normals

### 2.1 A comparison remainder that contains the missing boundary motion

For an actual child of mass `s`, input `w` and endpoint `y`, take

\[
\frac{16}{17}\le\kappa\le1,\quad
\delta=1-\kappa,\quad\sigma=2\kappa-1,\quad
D=d+2\kappa s,\quad d\ge1,
\]

\[
r=P(w/D),\qquad q=P(y/d),\qquad n_R=w-Dr.
\]

The two distinct remainders are

\[
z_R=\frac{w-y+(D-s)q}{D},\quad
u_R=r-z_R,\quad R_R=\operatorname{Rem}(w,D,z_R),
\]

\[
\mathcal B_R=\operatorname{Rem}(w,D,q)
=D\|r-q\|^2+2\langle n_R,r-q\rangle. \tag{2.1}
\]

The actual gap is `E_R = U_D(w)-U_d(y)`. Unlike `R_R`, the remainder `B_R` directly contains the missing endpoint difference. This definition assigns no independent values to the two remainders.

Writing `w-y = sum_i h_i g_i`, define the actual nonnegative rows

\[
N_{R,i}=\langle n_R,r-g_i\rangle,
\qquad N_{R,q}=\langle n_R,r-q\rangle.
\]

The convex weights in `z_R` give the exact identity

\[
R_R=D\|u_R\|^2
 +\frac2D\sum_i h_iN_{R,i}
 +\frac{2(D-s)}D N_{R,q}. \tag{2.2}
\]

All coefficients are nonnegative, and `D-s > 0`. In particular,

\[
N_{R,q}\le\frac{D}{2(D-s)}R_R. \tag{2.3}
\]

This is a proved bound on the real normal exposed in R9. The vector `q-r` still need not be controlled by `R_R`: the R9 interval example has `R_R=0` and `q-r != 0`. Equation (2.3) does not make that invalid vector estimate.

### 2.2 A genuine all-body leaf base

For the actual inherited leaf `h=1+a`, let

\[
g=P(x),\quad y=x-hg,\quad q=P(y/d),\quad
D=d+2\kappa h,\quad r=P(x/D),\quad n=x-Dr,
\]

\[
z=q+\frac hD(g-q),\quad u=r-z,\quad
R=\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,z),\quad
\mathcal B=\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,q),\quad
E=U_D(x)-U_d(y).
\]

**Leaf claim.** For every body, start, `d >= 1`, and `16/17 <= kappa <= 1` just specified,

\[
E\ge R,\qquad E\ge\frac{\mathcal B}{300}.
\tag{2.4}
\]

Consequently, for every `eta in [0,1]`,

\[
E\ge\eta R+\frac{1-\eta}{300}\mathcal B.
\tag{2.5}
\]

One explicit base is `eta = 3/4`, with boundary coefficient `1/1200`. These are proved leaf constants, not suggested values for higher children.

Here is a complete proof. Put `t=g-q`, and retain the two actual rows

\[
N_g=\langle x-g,g-q\rangle\ge0,
\qquad N_q=\langle y-dq,q-g\rangle\ge0.
\]

Define

\[
V_d=\|x\|^2-\|y+hq\|^2.
\]

Since `y+hq=x-ht`, expansion yields `V_d=2h<x,t>-h^2||t||^2`. Also

\[
N_g=\langle x,t\rangle-\langle g,t\rangle,
\quad N_q=-\langle x,t\rangle+h\langle g,t\rangle+d\langle q,t\rangle.
\]

Therefore

\[
V_d=A_gN_g+A_qN_q+\zeta\|t\|^2, \tag{2.6}
\]

where

\[
A_g=\frac{2h(h+d)}{h+d-1},\quad
A_q=\frac{2h}{h+d-1},\quad
\zeta=h\left(\frac{2d}{h+d-1}-h\right).
\]

The denominators are positive. For `h>1`, the fraction `2d/(h+d-1)` increases with d, so `zeta >= 2-h^2 > 0` by (1.1). In particular `V_d >= 0`.

The discounted exact gap identity gives

\[
E=\frac{V_d}{D}+2\delta h\|q\|^2+R. \tag{2.7}
\]

Thus `E >= R`. It remains to control the boundary vector.

Let `N_rq=<n,r-q>` and `N_rg=<n,r-g>`. Both are nonnegative. By the two convex weights of z,

\[
R=D\|u\|^2+\frac{2h}{D}N_{rg}
                   +\frac{2(D-h)}D N_{rq}. \tag{2.8}
\]

Here `D>2h`: indeed `D-2h >= 1-(2/17)h > 0`. Because `r-q=u+(h/D)t`,

\[
\mathcal B
\le 2D\|u\|^2+\frac{2h^2}{D}\|t\|^2+2N_{rq}
\le4R+2T,
\quad T:=\frac{h^2}{D}\|t\|^2. \tag{2.9}
\]

For `d >= 2`, (1.1) gives

\[
\frac\zeta h\ge\frac4{h+1}-h\ge\frac1{10}.
\]

Consequently `T <= 15 V_d/D <= 15E`, and (2.9) gives `B <= 34E`.

Now suppose `1 <= d <= 2`. Set

\[
\tau=\frac{D-1}{\sigma h},\qquad
\tau-1=\frac{d+h-1}{\sigma h}.
\]

The exact normal identities

\[
x-g=(h-1)t+(D-1)q+Du+n,
\qquad y-dq=\sigma hq+Du+n
\]

give

\[
(h-1)\|t\|^2
=(\tau-1)\bigl(D\langle u,t\rangle+\langle n,t\rangle\bigr)
+N_g+\tau N_q. \tag{2.10}
\]

Since `<n,t>=N_rq-N_rg <= N_rq`, Young's inequality gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
\|t\|^2\le{}&
\frac{(\tau-1)^2D^2}{(h-1)^2}\|u\|^2
+\frac{2(\tau-1)}{h-1}N_{rq}\\
&+\frac2{h-1}N_g+\frac{2\tau}{h-1}N_q.
\end{aligned} \tag{2.11}
\]

For this range, the elementary bounds

\[
h\le\frac32,\quad h-1\ge\frac13,\quad
\sigma\ge\frac{15}{17},\quad D-h>2,
\quad \tau-1\le\frac{17}{8},\quad \tau\le\frac{25}{8}
\]

are sufficient. Multiply (2.11) by `h^2/D`. Using (2.6)--(2.8), its four terms are bounded by, respectively,

\[
\frac{23409}{256}E\le92E,\qquad
\frac{459}{64}E\le8E,\qquad
\frac92E\le5E,\qquad
\frac{1125}{32}E\le36E.
\]

For the third bound use `A_g >= 2h`; for the fourth use `A_q=2h/(h+d-1)`. For the second use `N_rq <= D E/[2(D-h)]`. Thus `T <= 141E`, and (2.9) gives `B <= 286E <= 300E`. This proves (2.4). Taking the convex combination of its two lower bounds proves (2.5).

The proof used actual projection rows, including the initial and endpoint auxiliary samples. It did not free the vectors or normals. The deliberately conservative coefficient `1/300` is compatible with the much smaller-than-one boundary fraction forced by the core-quadratic leaf calibration.

### 2.3 Exact payment of the right normals in the parent transport

Use the R9 actual parent notation

\[
s=S_j,\quad b=1+k,\quad h=s+b,\quad C=1+s,
\quad S=s+h,
\]

\[
A=1+2\kappa s,\quad\alpha=A-s,\quad
D=d+2\kappa s,\quad M=D+2\kappa h.
\]

The physical parent is `x -> v`, `p=P(v)`, `w=v-bp`, then `w -> y`. Put

\[
\ell=P(x/A),\quad
z_L=\frac{x-v+\alpha p}{A},\quad u=\ell-z_L,
\quad n_L=x-A\ell,
\]

and retain the right variables in (2.1). Set `R_L=Rem(x,A,z_L)` and

\[
\mathcal B_L=\operatorname{Rem}(x,A,p),
\quad E_L=U_A(x)-U_1(v),\quad
\Psi=U_M(x)-U_d(y).
\]

For a feasible `theta`, R9 uses

\[
0\le\theta\le\min(1,b/\alpha),\qquad
z_\theta=z_P+\frac{\theta A}{M}u,
\]

where `z_P=[x-w+(M-h)r]/M`. Define

\[
z_{\rm all}=\frac{x-y+(M-S)q}{M},\quad
R_{\rm all}=\operatorname{Rem}(x,M,z_{\rm all}),\quad
R_\theta=\operatorname{Rem}(x,M,z_\theta).
\]

Its complete canonical transport is

\[
t_0=-Du_R+\sigma h(q-r),\qquad
t=t_0-\theta Au,
\qquad n_\theta=n_R-\sigma hr-\theta Au,
\]

\[
M(R_{\rm all}-R_\theta)=-2\langle n_\theta,t\rangle+\|t\|^2. \tag{2.12}
\]

Consider the two-remainder induction statement actually selected for this approach:

The coefficients satisfy `0 <= eta_j <= 1` and `gamma_j >= 0`.

\[
E_j\ge\eta_jR_j+\gamma_j\mathcal B_j
\quad\text{for every body, start, and trailing }d\ge1.
\tag{2.13}
\]

The leaf base (2.5) establishes `(eta_1,gamma_1)=(3/4,1/1200)`. The right normals in the transport can be paid without losing a common retained fraction. More precisely, suppose the right child satisfies (2.13) with `eta,gamma`, and the proposed parent canonical fraction is also eta. The relevant right-normal expression is

\[
\mathcal R_N
=M E_R-2\eta D\langle n_R,u_R\rangle
             -2\eta\sigma hN_{R,q}.
\]

Using (2.2), the child statement gives the explicit lower bound

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal R_N\ge{}&
\eta MD\|u_R\|^2+\gamma MD\|q-r\|^2\\
&+\frac{4\eta\kappa h}{D}\sum_i h_iN_{R,i}
+\left(\frac{2\eta hd}{D}+2\gamma M\right)N_{R,q}.
\end{aligned} \tag{2.14}
\]

Every displayed coefficient is nonnegative. The terminal coefficient follows from the exact cancellation

\[
(M-D)\frac{D-s}{D}-\sigma h
=2\kappa h\frac{D-s}{D}-\sigma h
=\frac{hd}{D}. \tag{2.15}
\]

This is an actual payment of both right normal terms, not an assumed normal bound and not a PSD condition. It uses the real right-child gradients with their original step weights.
